The per-tool counter inside `read_buffer` is deleted; `ReplyGuard` is the single place a model-supplied conversation is observed. `nora.testing.scripted_agent._invoke_tool` now filters a scripted turn's arguments through the tool's own `inputSchema` before calling it, because a fake that raised `TypeError` on an out-of-schema argument could not express the case production actually has. | `tests/test_tools.py::test_no_tool_asks_the_model_which_conversation_it_is_in` (the schema, which is what the model sees), `::test_strands_drops_an_out_of_schema_argument_instead_of_rejecting_the_call` (the SDK behaviour the counter depends on), `tests/test_guards_replyguard.py::test_a_well_formed_call_never_touches_the_scope_counter` (the counter is not pegged), `::test_the_scope_counter_fires_for_any_tool_not_just_the_two_scoped_ones`, `tests/test_guards_hooks.py::test_before_tool_counts_an_out_of_schema_conversation_and_still_allows_the_call` (the counter is not dead), `tests/integration/test_tagging.py::test_send_to_other_group_cannot_escape_the_tagged_conversation` (R-3 end to end) |
| DEC-25 | **The retry budget is derived from the invocation timeout, never configured beside it.** `nora.agent.factory.create_agent` never set `retry_strategy`, so Strands' default applied: `max_attempts=6, initial_delay=4, max_delay=240` (`strands/event_loop/_retry.py`), i.e. 4+8+16+32+64 = 124s of backoff. `nora.engagement.stages.invoke` wraps the whole invocation in `asyncio.wait_for(timeout=llm.timeout_seconds)` — 30s — and the retry sleeps happen INSIDE that wait, so the very first provider throttle guaranteed the timeout fired first and the turn was counted `nora_silent_failures_total{reason="invocation_timeout"}` when the cause was quota. A8 exists to make the silent-failure rate trustworthy, and a `reason` label naming the wrong cause is worse than a missing one: it aims the next investigation at latency. `build_retry_policy(timeout_seconds=...)` returns the largest exponential ladder (`initial_delay=1`) whose worst-case backoff fits in `RETRY_BACKOFF_FRACTION` (0.25) of the timeout — at today's 30s that is 4 attempts and 1+2+4 = 7s asleep, leaving 23s for the calls. **Derived rather than configured** because `timeout_seconds` is already per-tier config and a second knob is a second thing to keep consistent with the first; the invariant `total_backoff_seconds < timeout_seconds` cannot be violated if it is never written down twice. A timeout too small for even one retry yields `max_attempts=1`, which is Strands' own "no retries" value, rather than a startup failure. `RetryPolicy` carries scalars and `create_agent` builds a FRESH `ModelRetryStrategy` per agent: the strategy holds mutable `_current_attempt`, and one instance shared across overlapping tags would let one tag's throttle decide another tag's backoff — the same per-tag-state rule `ReplyGuard` follows. | `tests/test_factory.py::test_the_retry_budget_fits_inside_the_invocation_timeout` (the invariant, across five timeouts), `::test_the_retry_budget_at_todays_thirty_second_timeout`, `::test_a_timeout_too_short_for_even_one_retry_still_produces_a_usable_policy`, `::test_the_agent_carries_the_bounded_retry_strategy_not_the_sdk_default`, `::test_each_agent_gets_its_own_retry_strategy_instance` |
| DEC-26 | **The agent is named, and what its traces carry is an open operator decision.** (1) `Agent(...)` was constructed without `name=` or `agent_id=`, so `name` defaulted to `"Strands Agents"` and every span this process ever produced was called `invoke_agent Strands Agents` — correct and unsearchable. Now `name="nora"`, `agent_id="nora"`, stable across tags because the tag is already carried as a trace attribute (`nora.obs.tracing.trace_attributes` → `tag_id`, `conversation_id`, `doctrine_sha256`, provenance only, never content) and a per-tag `agent_id` would make every trace its own agent. Kept as module constants because a span name is what a saved Langfuse view filters on. (2) **Trace content is NOT changed here, on purpose — it needs an operator call.** Findings, all verified against the installed 1.50.2 and recorded so the decision is not re-investigated: the whole group transcript, Nora's replies and every tool result reach the exporter UNREDACTED, because `Tracer._redaction_enabled` is False unless `OTEL_SEMCONV_STABILITY_OPT_IN` contains a `gen_ai_unredacted_attributes=` token — an allowlist, so absent the token nothing is redacted. Setting it empty redacts all three sensitive keys; setting `gen_ai.output.messages` keeps Nora's own replies and drops the group's words and every tool result (`tool.result` is policed under `gen_ai.input.messages`). The lever is all-or-nothing per canonical key — there is no "redact the transcript but keep the trajectory". Two corrections to the premise: `Tracer.is_langfuse` reads `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`/`LANGFUSE_BASE_URL` from the ENVIRONMENT, and `nora.obs.tracing` passes the endpoint to `OTLPSpanExporter` programmatically instead, so span-attributes-only mode is NOT active here and message content is emitted as span EVENTS; and the configured endpoint is `127.0.0.1:3100`, a self-hosted Langfuse, so the transcript does not leave the host. **Separately, and not fixable by the native lever at all:** `Agent._start_agent_trace_span` passes `system_prompt=self.system_prompt` as a `**kwargs` extra, which `start_agent_span` copies onto the span as a plain `system_prompt` attribute without going through `_redact` — the entire doctrine is on every agent span in every configuration. The options and their costs are written up in `design/13-trace-content-and-redaction.md`. | `tests/test_factory.py::test_the_agent_is_named_so_a_trace_can_be_found`; `tests/test_tracing.py` (trace attributes carry provenance, never content); no test for (2) — nothing changed |
| DEC-27 | **Three room-scoped tools — `group_members`, `lookup_person`, `recall` — replace the reverted `<roster>` prefill block, and the scope is the ROOM rather than the address book.** design/12 §5's list, built. (1) **What was wrong.** The prefill was handed every `PersonRef` in `identity.toml`; asked who was in the group she named five people who had never been members. The block carried no phone number and no ACI, so R-18 had nothing to match — the disclosure was already authorised by the time text existed. Reverting it left her with no way to answer the question at all, and no way to know that 阿豹 is Tyler Cortez. (2) **Where membership comes from.** signal-cli's `listGroups`, through `nora.platform.signal.directory` — the read-only two-method allowlist the operator control plane already uses (DEC-20), not a second copy of the RPC. `nora.platform.signal.roster.SignalRoster` adds exactly two things that module does not need: selecting ONE group by id, and never raising. It is reached through a new port, `nora.domain.ports.RosterSource`, kept OFF `PlatformAdapter` because membership is a directory read rather than part of the send/receive/react lifecycle every adapter must implement — putting it there would oblige every future adapter and every fake to implement a roster it may have no endpoint for. Its timeout is 4s, not `directory`'s 10s default: ten seconds is a fine budget for an operator at a terminal and a terrible one inside a turn. (3) **`nora.agent.room.Room` is the scope**, constructed fresh per tag and holding the roster read for the tag's length, so `group_members` plus two `lookup_person` calls stays ONE daemon round trip. Two people are deliberately absent from its `people`: Nora herself (her account IS in the member list and maps to nobody, so without `self_ids` — `PlatformIdentity.aliases`, the same flattened set R-12 matches on — she would be reported as a stranger in her own room), and anyone unmapped, who is reported as a COUNT because the only thing Nora holds for them is exactly what R-18 keeps off the wire. One person holding two accounts in the group is one person in the room (DEC-14's baseline case, and the live `Ridgeline` group's actual shape). (4) **Recall is `POST /peers/{id}/representation`, not `peer_card`.** Verified live 2026-08-06: ~0.2s, `{"representation": "<markdown>"}`. `peer_card` returns `null` even for peers that demonstrably have conclusions — a dead endpoint. `target=` is not sent (404s unless the target peer exists, and Nora is not a peer). `search_query` is the TAGGING MESSAGE, closed over rather than declared, so the model never invents the question it was just asked. Returns `Recollection(reachable, conclusions)` rather than `peer_card`'s `list[str]`, because an unknown peer answers 200-with-an-empty-string and the reachability bit cannot be recovered afterwards (R-22). (5) **DEC-24 applied.** `group_members()` takes nothing; the other two take one name each. Not a conversation, not a peer id, not a phone number, not the search query — every one of those is a value Nora already holds, and DEC-24 measured what declaring one costs. `recall` also filters its own conclusions through `nora.agent.egress` before returning them: R-18 fails closed on the WHOLE reply, so one conclusion carrying somebody's number would cost the entire message rather than one fact, and Honcho conclusions are distilled from what people actually said. The same guard covers `asked_about`, the one field these tools parrot back — found by a test, not by review. *(Both call sites used `scan_egress`, which also matched model names; DEC-30 moved them to `scan_identifiers` and deleted `scan_egress`.)* (6) **Budgets** (R-7, DEC-10): `group_members=1` (membership cannot change inside one turn and the read is cached, so a second call is a guaranteed-identical answer four seconds later), `lookup_person=2` and `recall=2` (one message routinely names two people; the currency is model round trips, ~4s each, against `llm.limits.turns = 8`). Adding all three took no edit to `guards.py` — appearing in `NATIVE_TOOL_BUDGETS` is what makes a tool callable, which is the property DEC-10 exists for. (7) **Not done, deliberately**: `read_peer_card` is left in place. It takes a raw `sender_id` and is not room-scoped — a pre-existing gap documented in `nora.agent.tools` — and deleting it is design/11 Part 1's job, not this one's. It is reachable only by a model that already holds a platform id, which R-18 and the prompt both work to prevent. | `tests/test_room_tools.py` (the three tools), `tests/unit/test_agent_room.py` (the scope), `tests/contract/test_roster_source_contract.py` (both `RosterSource` implementations), `tests/test_honcho_representation.py` (the live shapes), `tests/unit/test_engagement_pipeline.py::test_the_room_scoped_tools_are_bound_to_the_tagged_conversation` (the wiring) |
| DEC-28 | **The doctrine states CAPABILITIES; only `_context_header` and the schemas state tools — and the doctrine's claims about what Nora knows are audited against what she actually has.** Audited 2026-08-06, after a live tag was read as evidence that the model would not reach for the DEC-27 lookup tools. (1) **The premise was wrong twice over, and both errors are worth keeping.** The evidence was "zero occurrences of `lookup_person` in `data/nora.log`" — but **no tool logs its own name on any path**: `now` logs its result, `read_buffer`/`read_peer_card`/`recall` log only on exception, and `lookup_person`/`group_members` emit nothing ever. That grep returns zero whether the tool ran on every turn or never, so it cannot distinguish the two. The observable that can is `nora_room_lookups_total{tool,status}`, which exists for exactly this reason (R-22). Nora's actual reply — 「呢個group冇人叫阿豹妹喎」 — is a near-verbatim rendering of `_miss()`'s `not_found` note, i.e. she *did* call it and relayed the answer faithfully; and the answer was CORRECT, because `listGroups` reports `Ridgeline` as three accounts (two Miles, one Nora) and Tyler is in `identity.toml` but not in that group. R-21 working as designed looked like a tool never being called. (2) **Measured, 6 runs each, real schemas + real assembled doctrine:** `lookup_person` 6/6 on turn one and `recall` 6/6 on turn two, under the pre-audit doctrine *and* the current one. `reasoning_effort` is flat across none/low/medium/high. The doctrine was not suppressing selection; **design/11 §1.6's settling measurement is therefore run, and §1.2's "the model will not ask" is retracted.** (3) **What was actually false**, and fixed: `identity.md` said she knows "Two things, and only these … the recent messages … and what you durably recall about THE PERSON WHO CALLED YOU" (false since DEC-27, and contradicted by `_context_header` in the same prompt); `conduct.md` carried a second prose tool catalogue listing three of seven beside "that covers almost every call"; both files stated "no web access **in this version**"; `_context_header` described a `peer_card` block's *empty* state though the block is omitted when empty, and conditioned `read_peer_card` on "the recall block", which does not exist. (4) **The rule.** No doctrine file may name a tool (`test_no_doctrine_file_carries_a_tool_catalogue`); the one prose catalogue is `_context_header`, pinned for coverage AND for tools-that-no-longer-exist (`test_the_header_names_no_tool_at_all (renamed by DEC-29; the header names none)`). It is deliberately NOT generated from `ToolSpec`: every native tool ships `description=""` and derives its text from the callable, so generation would emit a bare name list and lose the only thing the prose adds — when to reach for it in this situation. (5) **The measured effect was on proportional cost, not on selection** (design/12 §4): under the old doctrine a greeting spent a tool round trip 12/12 and 「你係邊個」 answered directly 5/12; under the new one, 5/12 and 7/12 respectively. Doctrine cost 2,278 → 2,540 tokens of a ~4,400-token call. `voice.md` is UNCHANGED — how talkative she is and when a GIF is in register are operator decisions (design/15 §3, design/16 §2.1). | `tests/test_doctrine_content.py::test_no_doctrine_file_carries_a_tool_catalogue`, `::test_identity_makes_no_claim_about_what_she_can_find_out`, `::test_the_silence_doctrine_is_stated_exactly_once_in_guardrails`; `tests/test_prefill.py::test_the_header_names_no_tool_at_all (renamed by DEC-29; the header names none)` |
| DEC-29 | **The prompt is split by SOURCE OF TRUTH, not by topic: `nora/persona/` becomes `nora/doctrine/`, every file declares a `kind` the loader enforces, and capability is GENERATED from the tool registry instead of written down.** design/17, built in three steps. (1) **The name was working against the boundary.** "Persona" names a character, and a directory named for a character invites sentences about what that character is like — which is why nobody writing `soul.md` felt structural pressure to keep a capability claim out of it. Doctrine is what has been DECIDED, which is exactly the authored half of design/17 §2's table. The rename was done with PROVABLY zero behaviour change: `git diff -M` over the four markdown bodies is empty, and the assembled block differed from its pre-rename form only in the wrapper tag (pre-rename sha256 `092e0b80…`, pinned against a checked-in fixture for the two commits it was needed). `PersonaStore`/`Persona`, `persona_sha256`, `nora_persona_reload_failures_total` and the `persona_leak`/`persona_span` reason strings all followed; a counter saying persona beside a class saying doctrine is the same stale-label failure this change exists to close. (2) **`kind` is the ONLY part of design/12 §2's authority model that can be mechanised**, so it is. Five kinds — `constraint`, `identity`, `conduct` authored; `capability`, `situation` derived. Seven load gates, each OBSERVED FAILING against a deliberate probe with its own gate removed before it was claimed to work: no `kind`; a `kind` outside the five; an `identity`/`conduct` file naming a tool (structural — a bare snake_case identifier, which needs no list of today's tools and cannot rot — plus the registry's own names for `now`/`recall`, which are ordinary English); a file claiming a DERIVED kind; an empty `not_here`; an unknown `change_policy`; an `authority_above`/`authority_below` pair whose two ends disagree; and — added 2026-08-20, from the prompt-architecture audit's F5 — an authority declaration that is CYCLIC. Symmetry alone accepted `a` above `b` and `b` above `a` (verified passing, as was a 3-cycle): perfectly symmetric, and a precedence no reader can satisfy. The walk reports the loop it found rather than a bare verdict. **`check_authority_chain` was renamed `check_authority_graph` in the same change, because it never was a chain.** `identity`/`voice` and `silence`/`conduct` declare byte-identical authority fields and neither pair names the other — they are unranked PEERS, and design/17 §7b writes the tier order with `identity/voice` as one rung deliberately, while `kind` puts `silence` and `conduct` on one rung by construction. They were left unranked and the name was corrected: inventing an order between two files nothing reads at inference time asserts a fact no gate can check. What is declared is a strict partial order over sections (a total order over TIERS), and that is now what is enforced — irreflexive, symmetric, acyclic. The audit's other half, rendering `authority_*` into the prompt, is NOT done: it needs a measurement, not a patch. `constraint` is exempt from the tool gate because R-14 must be able to say `group_transcript` and `tag_message`. (3) **Authority is stated relatively, purpose carries negative space, and files have a change policy** — the three things design/17 §7b takes from the operator's own `~/.hermes` doctrine, structure only, explicitly not volume. `constraints.md` used to call itself "not negotiable", an adjective with no position in an ordering; it now says it has nothing above it and names everything below, down to `tool-output` — the slot design/14 §2.1 needed and could not cite. `not_here` is the boundary §3 says `soul.md` lacked: had it carried one, the capability claim would have looked wrong the day it was written rather than eight weeks later. `change_policy` marks `voice.md`, `identity.md` and `constraints.md` `operator-only`. None of the three renders — they are for whoever opens the file, and the prompt pays for nothing it does not use. (4) **The section tag drops `version` and gains `kind` and `description`** (§5b). `version` is build metadata, paid on every model call of every tag, telling her nothing; `description` was already parsed and being discarded. One field feeds two consumers — the gate reads `kind`, the renderer emits it — so a section's declared authority and its enforced boundary cannot disagree. Escaped through the same helper the prefill uses, which is why `escape`/`escape_attr` moved to `nora/domain/prompt.py`. **No behavioural claim is made for the attributes**; whether the model attends to them is unmeasured. The change stands on the gate and on `version` leaving. (5) **Capability is generated, and the two hand-written catalogues are deleted.** `ToolSpec` gained `when` with NO DEFAULT, so a tool cannot be registered without saying when to reach for it; `NATIVE_TOOLS` is one table carrying budget and `when` together, and `NATIVE_TOOL_BUDGETS` is derived from it. `ToolRegistry.capability()` renders the `kind: capability` section from that same table at order 35, handed to the store by the composition root — there is no file, and a file claiming one will not load. `conduct.md`'s catalogue died in DEC-28; `_context_header`'s died here. It must NOT become a schema dump: the schemas are already in the request (1,222 tokens, 25% of the call) and say what a tool does; this says only when to reach for it. (6) **`identity.md` is split** (§4): it keeps who she is, why she is here, where she comes from. Its whole "What you actually know" section is deleted, not rewritten. The negative space split by kind — "Not an assistant announcing itself", "Not a summarizer", "Not a moderator" to `conduct.md`; "Not eager" verbatim to `voice.md`. **"Not a search engine with a personality layer" is a capability claim and was DELETED, not moved**; the generated block states the true version of it from the registry. `voice.md`'s register, teasing rules, language policy and length guidance are untouched — it is `operator-only` and received exactly one moved line. (7) **Cost, measured on `opencode-zen/deepseek-v4-flash`**, doctrine block alone: 2,504 → 2,578 (frontmatter attributes) → 2,656 (capability section absorbed from the header, minus the deleted identity prose). `_context_header` fell 398 → 188 in the same step, so the two together went 2,902 → 2,844, a net **−58**. The doctrine block grew by 152 because a catalogue MOVED INTO it from a user message; nothing was added twice. (8) **Behaviour: no measurable movement, reported as such.** Same probe shape as DEC-28 — real schemas, real assembled doctrine, `tool_choice="required"`. Selection was already saturated at 6/6 and could not show a win; on the axis that could — proportional cost, whether a greeting is answered directly — see the step-3 commit message for the numbers. | `tests/test_doctrine_loader.py` (the gate probes, including `::test_a_two_section_authority_cycle_is_a_startup_failure` and `::test_a_three_section_authority_cycle_is_a_startup_failure`), `tests/test_doctrine_content.py::test_identity_voice_and_silence_conduct_are_peers_on_purpose`, `tests/test_doctrine_assembly.py::test_the_capability_section_is_generated_and_sits_between_conduct_and_constraints`, `tests/test_doctrine_content.py::test_identity_makes_no_claim_about_what_she_can_find_out`, `::test_the_negative_space_split_by_kind_and_the_capability_claim_died`, `tests/test_prefill.py::test_the_header_names_no_tool_at_all`, `::test_the_generated_capability_block_names_exactly_the_tools_that_exist`, `tests/test_composition.py::test_the_doctrine_the_root_builds_carries_the_generated_capability`, `::test_the_root_hands_the_loader_the_tool_names_its_gate_needs` |
| DEC-30 | **`scan_egress` is split into two predicates — `scan_identifiers` (numbers, ACIs, key-shaped strings) and `scan_reply` (those plus the model name) — and the old name is deleted, not deprecated.** design/14 §2.2's prerequisite, built, plus a live bug it exposed. (1) **One function was answering two questions.** `_MODELNAME` matches nine ordinary English words (`claude`, `gemini`, `haiku`, `opus`, `sonnet`, `grok`, `gpt`, `openai`, `anthropic`), and blocking them is exactly right for a REPLY — R-6/R-18, she must never name what she runs on — and wrong everywhere else, because `Deliverer.deliver` fails closed on the WHOLE reply. (2) **The bug.** `recall` filtered every Honcho conclusion through `scan_egress`, so any durable memory mentioning an LLM was silently withheld from a group of software engineers who talk about them constantly. The `withheld` count made the loss countable, not recoverable. What she runs on stays hers because `Deliverer` refuses to SAY it, not because she is made to forget what other people said. (3) **`_asked_about` also moves to `scan_identifiers`.** It echoes a MODEL-SUPPLIED word back into a tool result: a phone number there is the hole it was written to close, but a model name is not a disclosure at all — the model wrote it, and if the word reaches the reply `Deliverer` blocks it there. Under the old predicate a lookup naming any of those nine words silently lost the disambiguation `asked_about` exists to provide. (4) **Deleted, not aliased.** A third name that does one of the two jobs is how the wrong one gets called later; there were three call sites in `nora/` and two in `tests/`, none in `evals/`. (5) **`nora_egress_blocks_total{kind}` is unchanged** — append-only, and no new value was needed: `Deliverer` is still the only caller that counts, and it still reports `phone`/`uuid`/`keylike`/`model`. Only the tie-break order changed: identifiers are now checked first, so a reply carrying both a number and the word `claude` is labelled `phone`, which is the exposure that would have mattered. (6) **R-18's registered rule test is unchanged and still about replies** (`test_a_reply_carrying_someones_phone_number_is_refused`); `_MODELNAME` had no end-to-end test at all before this and now has one. | `tests/unit/test_egress_predicates.py` (the two predicates, every case asserted against BOTH), `tests/test_guards_deliverer.py::test_a_reply_naming_the_model_she_runs_on_is_refused` (R-18 keeps the strict predicate), `tests/test_room_tools.py::test_recall_keeps_a_conclusion_that_merely_names_an_llm` (the bug), `tests/test_room_tools.py::test_a_model_named_in_a_lookup_argument_is_still_echoed_back` (`_asked_about`) |
| DEC-31 | **Every tool says that it ran; the `↳` arrow quotes the message it answers; and messages-per-tag gets a baseline before it can move.** Step 2 of the grounding plan, three small changes with one theme — an observable that was missing, not a behaviour that was wrong. (1) **The regression that paid for this.** `grep lookup_person data/nora.log` returned zero, and it was read as "the tool was never called". It could not have returned anything either way: `nora/agent/tools.py` emitted exactly ONE log line on any success path (`now`'s), `read_buffer`/`read_peer_card`/`recall` logged only on exception, and `lookup_person`/`group_members`/`send_reply` emitted nothing ever. Two architectural conclusions were drawn from that zero, both wrong, and one nearly retracted design/11. DEC-28 diagnosed this and did not fix it. (2) **What a tool line may say: the tool's name, one status, and COUNTS.** `data/nora.log` is a file on disk and nothing scans it on the way out, so no conclusion, no transcript body, no reply text, no phone number or ACI, and never the model's own argument — `_asked_about` exists because a model does put a phone number in a `name` parameter, and a log file is a worse place for one than a tool result. `send_reply` logs `chars=`, `recall` logs `conclusions=`/`withheld=`, `read_buffer` logs `rows=`/`threaded=`/`limit=` (the CLAMPED limit, so the clamp working stays visible). `tag_id` is never passed: `nora.obs.logging` binds it to a contextvar and Strands dispatches each tool through `asyncio.create_task`, which copies the context — asserted against a real task hop rather than assumed. (3) **`nora_tool_calls_total{tool,status}` covers the four tools that had no counter**, in `nora_room_lookups_total`'s shape and deliberately NOT as a superset of it: names are append-only, `nora_room_lookups_total` is already alerted on (R-22), and a tool appearing in both series would silently double any dashboard summing tool volume. The two together are every tool call; neither alone is. `read_peer_card` now separates four answers that were all `[]` to the model — unmapped sender, nothing stored, something stored, transport raised — with the honest limit that the live endpoint answers `null` rather than failing, which is what DEC-27 replaced it with `representation` for. (4) **The `↳` snippet.** `_parent_of` already resolved the exact parent ROW and `_transcript_block` rendered only its label, so two messages from one author made `↳ Grant` unable to say which was answered. It now renders `[Tyler ↳ Grant「去邊食」]`, clipped at **14 characters** — a complete short Cantonese question, below ~12 a shared opening still collides, above ~16 it stops discriminating and becomes a second copy. Escaped (R-13) and whitespace-collapsed FIRST, which is what stops a newline in the parent forging a line out of the label; clipped before escaping so a cut cannot land inside an entity. It discloses nothing new — `_parent_of` resolves in-window only, so the parent's full text is already on its own line. **Measured on the pinned route:** transcript block 774 → 817 tokens (+43, ~7 per reply-line) on a 40-row window with 6 replies, `_context_header`'s notation clause +18 fixed, whole prefill 1,024 → 1,085 (+61). **design/18 §3's handle map, `#N` labels and `read_thread` stay DEFERRED** — ~80–120 tokens on every call regardless of threading, no measured need, and a `#\d+` egress predicate would mute her on `#1`. (5) **`nora_messages_per_tag`**, a count-bucketed histogram observed once per ADMITTED tag on every exit path, silent turns as 0. It is flat at 0-or-1 today because R-2 latches, and that is the point: multi-message's predicted failure is that the cap becomes a quota always spent in full, and a histogram first written after the behaviour changes is not a baseline. `Deliverer.delivered` is now derived from `delivered_count` rather than kept beside it, so the flag and the count cannot disagree, and the count is what the ADAPTER accepted — reading R-2's attempt latch instead would report a message the group never received. | `tests/test_tool_logging.py` (the whole file; `::test_a_turn_that_calls_lookup_person_says_so_in_the_log` is the DEC-28 grep as a probe), `tests/test_tools.py::test_the_four_tools_without_a_counter_of_their_own_now_have_one`, `::test_the_new_counter_never_double_counts_a_room_scoped_tool`, `tests/test_prefill.py::test_the_arrow_quotes_the_parent_so_two_lines_from_one_author_are_told_apart`, `::test_the_snippet_cannot_forge_a_transcript_line_out_of_a_newline`, `tests/unit/test_engagement_pipeline.py::test_a_silent_tag_observes_zero_rather_than_nothing_at_all`, `tests/test_metrics_export.py::test_the_per_tag_message_histogram_is_bucketed_for_counts_not_seconds` |
| DEC-32 | **Terminality is a property of the TOOL, and the message cap is the `send_reply` BUDGET — one number, checked before R-2 ever is.** Step 4 of the grounding plan: two prerequisites for multi-message that neither design/15 nor design/16 declared, both refactors rather than behaviour changes. (1) **What was hardcoded.** `ReplyGuard._before_model` — the cancellation that is the ONLY thing ending a turn, because `tool_choice="required"` means the model cannot stop by declining to call something (DEC-21) — decided "she has replied" by asking whether the DELIVERER had delivered. That expresses terminality as one specific tool's side effect, which is the pre-DEC-10 hardcoded `BUDGETS` class attribute wearing a different hat. `design/proposed/15-gif-replies.md` §1 proposed fixing it by defining the verb class *in the guard*; a second terminal verb is now more likely than when that was written — `send_gif` is one candidate and a Honcho investigation on 2026-08-06 raised another, a distinct feed step on the tag path — so the class is declared on the tool instead. `NativeTool` and `ToolSpec` gain `terminal: bool` (default False: the wrong default truncates a turn), `ToolRegistry.terminal_tools()` exposes it exactly as `budgets()` exposes the other per-tool number, and `ReplyGuard` takes it with NO DEFAULT — a guard constructed without it would end no turn at all, which is the ~25s-per-tag runaway DEC-21 fixed. **Exactly one tool is terminal today (`send_reply`) and the next one is a one-line table edit, verified by making it one:** marking `now` terminal in `NATIVE_TOOLS` stopped the real Strands loop after a single model call, with no edit to `guards.py`. (2) **`ToolSpec`s are now built from the table by one helper** (`nora.agent.tools._spec`) rather than seven hand-written constructor calls — copying `when=`/`budget=` seven times to add a third field is how a field gets forgotten and silently takes the dataclass default. (3) **The latch is set on a call that RAN**, not on one that was attempted: Strands fires `AfterToolCallEvent` for a call `_before_tool` cancelled too, with `exception=None` and only `cancel_message` to tell them apart (`tools/executors/_executor.py`, verified against 1.50.2, now asserted by `test_strands_api_contract`). Without that check an empty-text `send_reply` refused by R-4 would end the turn in silence. **One behavioural difference is accepted and pinned:** a `send_reply` the `Deliverer` REFUSED (R-6, R-18, audit or send failure) now ends the turn, where `delivered` staying False used to leave the loop running. The group sees the same thing either way — the call was recorded, `send_reply`'s budget is 1, so the retry was already refused — and the difference is only the forced turns that followed. (4) **The trap under multi-message.** `evaluate` checks the R-7 per-tool budget BEFORE `Deliverer.attempted`, and `NATIVE_TOOL_BUDGETS["send_reply"]` is 1, so the second reply is refused by the BUDGET and never reaches R-2's latch — while the veto it emits is labelled `rule="R-2"`. Relaxing R-2 alone therefore leaves multi-message silently dead, refused by a number nobody edited and reported as the rule that was just relaxed. Measured, not reasoned: with `Deliverer.attempted` forced to False the whole suite still passed except one unrelated reporting test. design/16 §4 caught this shape one line lower down, in `Deliverer._attempted`, and missed it here. (5) **Where the cap will live, decided now:** `NATIVE_TOOLS["send_reply"].budget` IS the message cap. design/16 §2.2's `llm.limits.messages` must NOT become a second number checked beside it — the ordering above guarantees the smaller one wins silently — so if the cap ever becomes operator-tunable, config hands it to `ToolRegistry`, which overrides that one table entry, and `ReplyGuard` still reads exactly one number (DEC-10). Not implemented: no measured need, and a config key with no reader is how the last one rotted. | `tests/test_guards_terminal.py` (the whole file; `::test_a_second_verb_declared_terminal_ends_the_turn` drives the REAL loop and is red the moment `_before_model` asks the deliverer again, `::test_the_same_turn_with_nothing_terminal_runs_to_the_cap` is its control), `tests/test_guards_replyguard.py::test_relaxing_r_2_alone_does_not_buy_a_second_message`, `::test_the_budget_is_the_number_that_refused_it`, `::test_with_the_budget_raised_the_latch_is_what_refuses_the_second_message`, `tests/test_strands_api_contract.py::test_obligation_3_hook_events_expose_cancel_tool_and_retry` (`cancel_message` is load-bearing now) |
| DEC-33 | **A VETOED tool call consumes no budget — and the double that made scripted scenarios blind to it now drives the guard through its hooks.** A live bug of R-5's shape, plus the test-fidelity gap that hid it. (1) **The bug.** DEC-32 (3) established that Strands fires `AfterToolCallEvent` for a call `_before_tool` cancelled as well, with `exception=None` and only `cancel_message` to tell it apart, and applied that to the terminal latch. It did **not** apply it to `record`, three lines above. So a cancelled call still spent its budget — and `NATIVE_TOOLS["send_reply"].budget` is 1. The observed sequence: the model calls `send_reply("")`, R-4 refuses it, the model corrects itself in the same turn with real text, and R-7's budget check — which runs BEFORE R-2's latch (DEC-32 (4)) — answers *you already sent your reply*. One recoverable mistake, and **the recovery is what gets blocked**; the turn then ends `no_send_call` and under R-5 the group simply hears nothing. The fix is one early `return` on `cancel_message`, shared by `record` and the latch, so the two can no longer disagree about what "the call ran" means. (2) **Why nothing caught it, and the more important half of this entry.** `nora.testing.FakeAgentRunner` — the double every scripted scenario in `tests/integration/` and `evals/` runs against — replayed the guard by calling `evaluate` and `record` BY HAND, and on a veto it `continue`d without calling `record` at all. That is the SDK's behaviour inverted: the fake charged nothing for a vetoed call, production charged a full budget. A regression test written against that double could not fail whatever `_after_tool` did, because `_after_tool` was never on the path. **Measured, not argued:** with the old double in place, the end-to-end probe below PASSES against the unfixed guard. This is exactly CLAUDE.md's "make the fake behave like the real server", in its third recorded instance. (3) **The double now dispatches hooks instead of methods.** `guard.register_hooks(...)` is handed a `_HookRegistry` and the replay fires `BeforeToolCallEvent`, `AfterToolCallEvent` (for cancelled calls too, carrying `cancel_message`) and `BeforeModelCallEvent`, with the field values `tests/test_strands_api_contract.py` asserts against the real SDK. Callbacks are keyed by `event_type.__name__` because `nora.testing` may not import `strands` (`test_strands_import_boundary`) and `add_callback` is handed the real class object anyway. Two consequences beyond the bug: what a veto costs and what ends a turn are now `ReplyGuard`'s decisions in tests as well as in production, and a script with turns after a terminal verb now stops where the real loop stops rather than replaying turns production cannot reach. **No existing test changed behaviour** — the whole suite was green on the double's fix alone, which is the measurement of how little the old seam was proving. (4) **`nora_guard_vetoes_total{rule="R-2"}` is deliberately NOT split.** It cannot distinguish the budget branch from the latch branch, and that was noted rather than fixed. A `rule` VALUE is as append-only as the metric name — a new one silently splits every existing `rule="R-2"` query — and the branch it would name is unreachable while the budget is 1, so it would be a series that can never increment, which R-17 calls indistinguishable from a deleted one. Raising the cap makes both branches live in the same edit; that is when the label earns its cardinality. Recorded here so the next reader sees a decision rather than an oversight. | `tests/integration/test_tagging.py::test_a_vetoed_send_reply_leaves_the_budget_for_the_correction` (the live bug, end to end), `tests/test_guards_hooks.py::test_after_tool_does_not_count_a_call_the_guard_cancelled`, `::test_the_budget_a_vetoed_call_leaves_is_the_one_the_correction_uses`, `tests/test_scripted_agent_fidelity.py` (the whole file; `::test_the_real_sdk_fires_after_tool_for_a_call_the_guard_cancelled` takes the claim from the SDK and `::test_the_scripted_double_fires_the_same_after_tool_for_a_cancelled_call` is red the moment the double stops dispatching it) |
| DEC-34 | **An @-mention is rehydrated into a NAME at render time, resolved through `Persons`, and never into an identifier.** A live bug since deployment, fixed retroactively from `raw_json`. (1) **What she was reading.** A Signal mention is not text: the body carries U+FFFC where the name appears and the identity travels in a parallel `dataMessage.mentions` array. `nora.platform.signal.parser._mentioned_ids` has documented that precisely since the first trigger fix — and read the array ONLY to decide whether Nora had been tagged, then discarded the names, while the transcript rendered the raw body. Real stored rows: id 28 `'￼ 邊個係阿豹妹？'`, id 24 `'￼'` (a message that was ONLY a mention, reaching the model as one invisible character). **Including mentions of HERSELF**: 「@nora 你好」 arrived as 「￼ 你好」, so the one message shape that is unambiguously addressed to her was the one she could not see was addressed to her. (2) **Where it runs, and the two places it deliberately does not.** `nora.engagement.mentions.MentionRenderer`, constructed fresh per tag, applied to the whole `TagContext` between `context.assemble` and the prefill. NOT at ingest: `messages.body` is an append-only audit of what arrived (A9), and a rendering depends on `identity.toml`, which changes when a human joins — doing it here reads `raw_json`, which every row ever audited already carries, so the fix is retroactive with no backfill. NOT in `nora.agent.prefill`: resolution needs `Persons` and Nora's own id forms, and `nora.agent` may not import `nora.platform` (independent siblings in the layers contract); `nora.engagement` sits above both. (3) **`Persons`, not `Room`, and that is a scope argument rather than a convenience.** design/12 §3's leak was answering *who is in this group* from the address book, which is why `RoomView` resolves within the platform's membership list. A mention is not a lookup: the account was named INSIDE this conversation, by a member of it, in a message Nora received — resolving it discloses nothing the room did not already assert. Intersecting with a roster would buy no scope and would put a daemon round trip at the front of every model call, which is the one thing `Room`'s laziness exists to avoid. Nora herself comes from `PlatformIdentity.aliases` (the R-12 set), because she is deliberately absent from `identity.toml` and would otherwise render `@someone` — an assertion that a stranger was tagged. (4) **What an unresolved mention renders: `@someone`, plus `nora_unmapped_mentions_total{platform}`.** Never the number, never the ACI, and never `mentions[].name` — which on EVERY real stored row IS the E.164 phone number, not a display name (verified against `data/messages.db`, ids 3-28). `MentionSpan.ids` is therefore `(uuid, number)` only, while `_mentioned_ids` keeps `name` as before: a superset costs nothing when the question is "was Nora tagged" and is a disclosure when the answer is rendered. The resolved name goes through `scan_identifiers` as well, so an `identity.toml` whose `name` field is itself an identifier cannot launder one into the prompt. The counter reads as `nora_unmapped_senders_total`'s other half — that one says somebody talked to her and she does not know who, this one says somebody was TALKED ABOUT — with the same fix and the same startup-only trap. (5) **The offsets, and why the substitution runs RIGHT TO LEFT.** `start`/`length` index the body BEFORE any substitution and a handle is longer than the one character it replaces, so a left-to-right pass shifts every later span by the growth of every earlier one. Descending `start` makes every edit land strictly to the right of every span not yet applied, so no offset a later iteration reads has been touched. Two further corrections the wire shape forces: `mention_spans` REBASES `start` by the leading whitespace `_body_parts` strips (the stored body is `message.strip()`, so a raw offset lands late on any message with leading space), and a span whose slice is not all-U+FFFC is SKIPPED with the body left exactly as stored — cutting `length` characters out on a guess would silently rewrite what a person said, which is worse than the void this change removes. (6) **Escaping (R-13/R-13b) happens exactly once, upstream.** The name is inserted before the single `nora.domain.prompt.escape` call every prompt block already makes on the body, so it is escaped by the same code that escapes the group-authored text around it. Escaping in `MentionRenderer` too would put `&amp;amp;lt;` in front of the model; escaping in neither place is the hole. Both directions are asserted. (7) **`nora.testing.group_event` gained a `mentions` parameter**, because there was previously no way to express a mention in a scripted scenario at all — which is a smaller instance of DEC-33's lesson, and the reason no test could have caught this. | `tests/integration/test_mentions.py` (the whole file, end to end through real ingest, real store and real prefill; `::test_a_stored_mention_renders_the_persons_name` and `::test_an_unmapped_mention_leaks_no_identifier_and_is_counted` are the two probes, `::test_two_mentions_in_one_line_both_land_on_their_own_names` is the offset order, `::test_a_row_whose_offsets_do_not_fit_is_left_exactly_as_stored` is the degradation), `tests/test_signal_parser.py::test_mention_spans_never_offers_the_name_field`, `::test_mention_offsets_are_rebased_onto_the_body_that_was_stored`, `::test_mention_spans_is_total` |
| DEC-35 | **Arithmetic is a TOOL, not a thing the model does in its head — `calculate(expression)`, Decimal throughout, and the remainder is reported rather than dropped.** R-5's family, in the one place a wrong answer is indistinguishable from a right one at the point somebody acts on it: 「每人 $247.50」 reads identically whether or not it is correct, and a group splitting a bill has no way to check it. (1) **Generic, never scenario-shaped.** `calculate(expression)`, never `split_bill(total, people)`. A tool is a capability, not a question — the expression is a genuine model decision, whereas `people=6, total=1485` is a form Nora designed in advance for one use case, which is exactly what DEC-24 deleted from `read_buffer` and `send_reply`. The generic form answers the dinner split, the 15% service charge, «how many days until the 20th» and the question nobody has thought of yet. (2) **Four things were measured before anything was specified, and each is a case where the obvious implementation is confidently wrong rather than obviously broken.** **Rounding**: Python's default is ROUND_HALF_EVEN and you get it by NOT choosing — `Decimal("2.005").quantize(Decimal("0.01"))` is **2.00**, and 0.125 goes to 0.12. Banker's rounding is right for a statistician and wrong on a bill. Every quantize passes `ROUND_HALF_UP` at the CALL, not on the context, so ambient state elsewhere cannot undo it. **The remainder**: 1485/7 is 212.1428…, and 212.14 x 7 is 1484.98 — two cents short, with nobody able to say who is short. `split` reports `each`, `leftover`, and a `settles_as` written as an EXPRESSION (`2 * 212.15 + 5 * 212.14`) so it can be handed back to this same tool and checked; a settlement nobody can verify is just a more confident version of the number this exists to distrust. It is computed in whole cents with ROUND_DOWN, so the leftover is always «somebody pays a cent MORE» rather than the harder-to-explain «two of you are owed a cent», and it reconciles exactly. `rounded_to_cents` and `split` are mutually exclusive: two money figures side by side that do not sum to the same total is the confusion the tool exists to remove. **`Pow` is a denial of service before it is a safety question**: `2**9999999999` parses cleanly as `BinOp`/`Pow`, passes any operator-level whitelist, and over ints was still running when `timeout 10` killed it. Over `Decimal` it raises `Overflow` in 54 microseconds — a real mitigation, but one this module does not OWN (it is the ambient `Emax=999999` plus a trap another `localcontext` could clear) and «Overflow» is not a sentence a model can act on. `Pow` is KEPT, because compound interest and percentages are ordinary group-chat questions, with `MAX_POW_EXPONENT = 1000` checked explicitly and refused legibly. **Literals must not be read through floats**: `ast` hands back `Constant(value=0.1)`, already a float, and `Decimal(0.1)` is 0.1000000000000000055…, so `0.1 + 0.2` would not be `0.3` — the tool wrong in precisely the way it exists to prevent. `Decimal(str(value))` fixes that one case and not the general one, so a float literal is re-read from its SOURCE TEXT; an int literal takes `Decimal(int)`, which is exact and is the only form that reads `0xff` correctly. `type(v) is int`, never `isinstance` — **`bool` subclasses `int`**, so `True + 1` would otherwise quietly answer 2. (3) **A node-type ALLOWLIST is the only safe shape, and a denylist of today's escapes is not one.** `(1).__class__` is a bare `Attribute` over a `Constant` carrying neither a `Call` nor a `Name`; `__import__('os').system('x')` is `Call` + `Name`. But `1 if 2 else 3`, `[1,2][0]` and `lambda: 1` are none of those three and a denylist waves all of them through. `_eval` is a recursive descent with one branch per permitted node and a refusal in the `else`, so validation and evaluation are the SAME walk and cannot drift apart the way a separate `ast.walk` pass does. No `eval()`, and no `literal_eval` either — the `ast` docs retract its old «safe» claim outright, and it cannot do arithmetic anyway. Three further bounds are owned here rather than inherited: `MAX_EXPRESSION_CHARS = 200` before parsing (the docs warn `ast.parse` can crash the interpreter on nested input; 3.13 happens to defend itself at depth 1000, which is an implementation detail, and the cap also bounds `_eval`'s recursion), `MAX_MAGNITUDE_DIGITS = 20` on every operand AND every intermediate (the render bound — `f"{Decimal('1E+999998'):f}"` is a megabyte into the model's context — and what keeps values clear of the 28-digit precision so nothing is silently re-rounded), and a zero-divisor check before `/`, `//` and `%`, because `decimal` signals that one thing as `DivisionByZero` in one case and `InvalidOperation` in two others. (4) **Two states, and the third is stated as impossible rather than added.** `ok` and a legible refusal. **`unavailable` cannot occur**: no clock, no socket, no injected collaborator, so there is nothing to be unreachable — a dead branch would be a state an operator eventually sees on a dashboard and acts on, meaning nothing. `evaluate` NEVER raises: an exception inside a turn costs the whole reply, which under R-5 is silent rather than wrong. (5) **What it does not fix, which is why the expression reaches the trace.** Constructing the expression is still reasoning: she can divide by 5 when six people ate. This removes ARITHMETIC error, not MODELLING error, and the second is the one that will actually happen — so `expression` comes back beside `result` (`1485/6` is checkable, `247.5` is not). The LOG line keeps DEC-31's shape — counts and status only, never the model's own argument — because `+85290000101 + 0` is valid arithmetic and `data/nora.log` is a worse place for a phone number than a tool result is, nothing scanning it on the way out; the echo itself is dropped through `scan_identifiers` exactly as `_asked_about` does. `budget: 4` is the only budget in the table sized by the QUESTION rather than by cost — a bill with a service charge and one person who paid ahead is genuinely four steps, and this is a pure function with no network. `terminal=False` explicitly: working out a total is the middle of an answer, never the end of one. `time_shiftable` has no meaning here in either direction — unlike `now`, the answer to `1485/7` is the same whenever it is asked. | `tests/unit/test_arithmetic.py` is the measurement suite — `::test_money_rounds_half_up_and_not_the_way_python_does_by_default`, `::test_a_split_that_loses_money_says_where_the_money_went`, `::test_the_settlement_it_reports_adds_up_to_the_total_exactly`, `::test_a_huge_exponent_is_refused_rather_than_computed`, `::test_the_node_check_is_an_allowlist_and_not_a_list_of_todays_escapes`, `::test_a_malformed_expression_is_refused_and_does_not_raise`, `::test_it_only_ever_reports_two_states`; `tests/test_tools.py::test_calculate_hands_back_the_expression_beside_the_result` and `::test_calculate_does_not_echo_an_identifier_back_at_the_model` are the tool's own half, and `tests/test_tool_logging.py::test_no_calculate_line_carries_the_expression_the_model_wrote` is DEC-31's |
| DEC-36 | **One peer's stream holds one speaker's words — the tag path feeds the TAGGER'S message and never Nora's reply.** The corpus bug behind ADR 0001, and the only Honcho failure in this repo a backfill cannot undo. (1) **What it was.** `nora/engagement/stages/feedback.py` fed `f"[{tagger}]: {body}\n[{nora}]: {reply}"` under `ctx.tagger.honcho_peer_id`. Honcho's deriver is handed a peer's message stream as that peer's own utterances — its prompt names it the *Target peer* — so every reply Nora ever sent became evidence about the person she was answering. The store keeps the CONCLUSION and not the text it came from, so re-feeding a clean corpus would add good conclusions BESIDE the bad ones rather than replacing them; there is no repair, only stopping. Fixed before anything else in this phase because retrieval quality, parsing and call shape are all downstream of what is in the corpus. (2) **Nora's reply is not fed at all, and that is the decision rather than an omission.** Under the tagger's peer it is the bug above. Under a `nora` peer it needs a session that is a CONVERSATION — Honcho sessions do hold multiple peers (verified against the live instance), but `HonchoClient.ensure_session` builds one session PER PERSON (`nora-<peer>`), so today her reply would land inside a session meaning *that person's private stream* and produce a `nora` peer whose entire corpus is her own output. Session-per-conversation is deferred, and half of it invented here would be worse than none. And ADR 0001's operative test — *could she have read this in the buffer?* — answers yes: the reply is in SQLite, which is the RECORD, and `read_buffer` already reaches it. (3) **`tagger_name`, `display_name` and `deliverer` were REMOVED from `feedback.run`'s signature, not left unused.** With no handle on the reply the stage is structurally unable to feed it, which is a stronger guarantee than a comment saying it must not — and it is what `tests/unit/test_engagement_feedback.py::test_noras_reply_never_reaches_any_peers_stream` is really pinning. (4) **A redundancy this made visible, recorded rather than folded in.** `nora.ingest.stages.memory_feed` runs for EVERY message before the engage gate, so it has ALREADY fed the tagging message's body under the same peer into the same session by the time this stage fires. With the `[Nora]:` half gone the two writes are byte-identical, and the tag path's is a pure duplicate costing the deriver a second pass over one message. It is not corrupting, and removing it deletes a pipeline stage that the session-per-conversation work is expected to want back, so it is pinned by an equality assertion instead of quietly tolerated — `tests/integration/test_tagging.py::test_the_tag_path_feed_happens_exactly_once_per_delivered_reply` asserts BOTH writes, and goes red the moment either disappears. (5) **What the old tests were actually asserting.** Three of them discriminated the tag-path feed by searching the fed text for `"[Nora]"` — the marker of the very corruption. They passed for the whole life of the deployment and could not have failed; the shape they keyed on was the defect. | `tests/unit/test_engagement_feedback.py::test_only_the_taggers_own_words_are_fed_under_the_taggers_peer` (the probe; revert `feedback.run` and it is red on the exact string), `::test_noras_reply_never_reaches_any_peers_stream`, `tests/integration/test_tagging.py::test_happy_tag_produces_one_reply_one_row_one_feed` (end to end — the reply demonstrably reached the group and reached no peer's stream), `::test_the_tag_path_feed_happens_exactly_once_per_delivered_reply` |
| DEC-37 | **`_conclusions()` parses one conclusion per OBSERVATION, against Honcho's four real sections — and the prose is parsed rather than replaced by `POST /conclusions/query`, deliberately.** (1) **What it did.** `Representation.format_as_markdown` (honcho 3.0.11, read out of the running container) emits four shapes, and only `## Explicit Observations` is one-line-per-fact. `## Deductive Observations` carries `   Premises:` and indented bullets; `## Inductive Observations` carries ` **Pattern** [high]: …`, `   **Type**:` and `   **Sources**:`; `## Contradictions` carries ` **CONTRADICTION**: …`. The old parser split on lines and ran `.lstrip("-*")`, which eats markdown bold **from the left only** — so a pattern arrived as `Pattern** [high]: …`, and every premise and source bullet arrived as an independent fact. **Measured:** the live body for `probe_zzz` produced **151 conclusions from 25 documents**; it now produces exactly 25, with no bold, no label and no bullet surviving. (2) **Why it had never fired.** The only fixture was `miles_novak`, whose 16 documents are all `level='explicit'` (counted in Postgres) — the exact fake-that-cannot-produce-the-real-shape CLAUDE.md names, and the fourth instance in this subsystem. It was also masked in production by the call shape DEC-38 fixes, which zeroed the two channels that surface derived levels. **Ordering is a correctness constraint:** shipping DEC-38 without this ships the mangling. (3) **`POST /conclusions/query` was evaluated and rejected.** It returns structured `Conclusion` objects (`id`, `content`, `observer`, `observed`, `session_id`, `created_at`) with clean text for every level and no parser at all — verified live. It is still the wrong tool: its handler is one `crud.query_documents` call over a single embedding (`src/routers/conclusions.py`), i.e. **semantic search and nothing else**, with no access to the most-derived or recent channels `get_working_representation` blends. Adopting it would hard-code the very failure DEC-38 undoes, and it carries no `distance` in its response either, so it does not buy back a ranking signal in exchange. (4) **The discriminator is the emitter's grammar, not any one body.** A conclusion is never a bullet and never indented; a premise, a source and a sub-label are always one or the other. An unrecognised bold label is DROPPED, so a level Honcho adds upstream degrades to silence rather than arriving as a fact Nora believes. Inductive confidence (`high`/`medium`/`low`) goes with the label: `recall` returns a flat list of things she knows, and a number she cannot calibrate or explain is noise in a group chat. (5) **The `[timestamp]` correction.** Stripping it stays, but the old comment's reason was wrong: it is not "when Honcho drew this conclusion". It is `internal_metadata["message_created_at"]` — the created_at of the message that TRIGGERED the derivation — copied onto every conclusion in the batch (`_safe_datetime_from_metadata`). `probe_zzz`'s 102 documents carry six distinct stamps, one of them shared by 77. It is not a recency signal at conclusion granularity and nothing may sort or age by it. (6) **No `## Contradictions` exists anywhere in the live database** (`select level, count(*) from documents` returns explicit/deductive/inductive only, across every workspace), so that fixture is reproduced line for line from `format_as_markdown`'s source and says so in the file — omitting it is how the explicit-only fixture came to be mistaken for coverage. | `tests/test_honcho_representation.py::test_the_three_derived_levels_all_become_plain_conclusions` (the probe; revert `_conclusions` and it is red on `Pattern** [high]: …`), `::test_a_premise_never_returns_as_an_independent_fact`, `::test_no_conclusion_carries_markdown_the_old_parser_would_have_mangled`, `::test_a_contradiction_is_its_description_and_not_its_label`, `::test_an_unknown_future_section_contributes_nothing_rather_than_garbage` |
| DEC-38 | **`max_conclusions` is a budget split three ways, not a row limit — so `search_top_k` must be strictly under it, `include_most_frequent` must be on, and there must be a distance floor.** (1) **The arithmetic**, from `src/crud/representation.py::_get_working_representation_internal` (honcho 3.0.11, read out of the running container): `semantic = min(search_top_k or total // 3, total)`, then `derived = min(total // 3, total - semantic)` **only if `include_most_frequent`**, then `recent = total - semantic - derived`. This client sent `max_conclusions=5, search_top_k=5` and no `include_most_frequent`, which computes to **semantic 5, derived 0, recent 0** — the semantic channel ate the whole budget and the other two were structurally unreachable, at any corpus size, forever. (2) **Why the derived channel is the one that matters.** `most_derived` orders by `times_derived DESC`, which counts how often the deduplicator has re-derived the same conclusion — i.e. it ranks the durable core of what is known about a person above whatever one question happens to embed near. That is precisely what ADR 0001 says Honcho is FOR, and it was the channel receiving zero. (3) **`search_top_k = max(1, max_conclusions // 3)`, sent explicitly.** It reproduces Honcho's own default for an omitted `search_top_k`, and is sent anyway: an upstream default is a value that can change without a diff, and this endpoint has already proven its behaviour is worth pinning from this side. `max(1, ...)` keeps it inside the API's `ge=1` bound, where a computed 0 would be a 422 — an outage reported for a number this client chose itself. (4) **`max_conclusions` 5 -> 9**, because the split turns one budget into three. At 5 the semantic channel is ONE row, and measured against `probe_zzz` — a corpus that demonstrably contains the answer — one row does not answer «how does he get to work»: the nearest hit is *does the morning school run*, and *drives to work instead of taking the train* is third. 9 gives 3/3/3 at ~1,100 characters of tool result, the same order as `read_buffer(40)`. The number is no longer restated at the `recall` call site: it only means something next to the arithmetic that divides it. (5) **`search_max_distance = 0.5`, bracketed on BOTH sides** against the live instance rather than picked. pgvector ranks, it does not judge, so with no floor the semantic channel always returns `top_k` rows — «quantum chromodynamics lattice gauge renormalisation» came back with the VW Golf and the rusty written Chinese, at d=none and at d=0.8 alike, which Nora would read out as an answer. At 0.5 the nonsense query returns 0 and the real query still returns all 5. At 0.3 the real query returns 0 too. Note the budget is computed from the REQUESTED counts, so a floor that rejects rows shrinks the answer rather than being topped up from elsewhere — that is upstream's behaviour and the fake reproduces it. (6) **The fake had to be rewritten, not extended.** The bug is entirely about which numbers were sent; the endpoint answered 200 with a well-formed body throughout. So `tests/test_honcho_representation.py` now REIMPLEMENTS the retrieval arithmetic and the markdown emitter over a corpus in which three rows are reachable through exactly one channel each — the most-derived row is the FURTHEST from the query and the oldest, the recent row is the second furthest. A stub with canned bodies cannot tell `search_top_k=9` from `search_top_k=3`, which is the shape of all three no-ops CLAUDE.md records. (7) **A correction to ADR 0001.** Its measurement — «`{}` returns Explicit + Deductive; our shape returns Explicit ONLY» — was taken against `miles_novak`, whose 16 documents are all `level='explicit'`; the sections it saw were an artefact of that peer. Against `probe_zzz` the OLD shape returns all three sections too. The defect is real and is the channel arithmetic above; it is not diagnosable from which headings come back. | `tests/test_honcho_representation.py::test_all_three_channels_reach_the_answer` (the probe; revert `search_top_k` or drop `include_most_frequent` and two of three rows vanish while the call still answers 200), `::test_the_old_shape_could_not_have_reached_two_of_them` (the counterfactual, same engine — it is what proves the probe measures the call shape and not the fake's generosity), `::test_a_question_the_corpus_cannot_answer_returns_no_nearest_neighbours`, `::test_the_budget_is_split_three_ways_and_never_spent_on_one_channel`, `::test_search_top_k_stays_inside_the_apis_lower_bound` |
| DEC-39 | **`recall` is renamed `about` — and the name was chosen by MEASUREMENT, against the one that read better.** (1) **Why rename.** `recall` promised memories and returned a standing profile; its docstring's «something they told you before» invited Nora to repeat a transcript back to the room that had just watched it arrive, which ADR 0001's operative test («could she have read this in the buffer?») calls negative value. The docstring now leads with what the tool holds — conclusions built up about a person — and points at `read_buffer` for anything that was actually said. (2) **`person_profile` was the better-reading candidate and was REJECTED on a number.** It is snake_case, so `nora.doctrine.loader`'s structural detector would catch it for free rather than it needing a place on that module's hand-kept `tool_names` list — a real argument, and wrong. Measured with the docstring held BYTE-IDENTICAL and only the identifier changed, against real `build_tool_specs` schemas, the real assembled doctrine as system prompt, `tool_choice="required"`, `opencode-zen/deepseek-v4-flash`, n=16: on a question the tool exists to answer («我平時鍾意食咩？», where the asker is named in the tag message so `lookup_person` has nothing to resolve), first-tool selection was **`recall` 15/16, `person_profile` 0/16, `about` 16/16**. `person_profile` also dragged an unrelated case down — `read_buffer` on «what did he say earlier» fell from 14/24 to 3/16 (z≈2.8). (3) **The mechanism, and the general lesson.** `person_profile` shares the token `person` with `lookup_person`, and person-shaped routing collapsed onto that tool. **A tool name is not documentation; it is the feature the router matches on**, and a name that overlaps a sibling's is a name that competes with it. Nothing in the schemas or the doctrine changed between those three runs. (4) **The measurement had to be redesigned before it said anything.** The first four cases were saturated by `lookup_person` (16/16 in most), because the harness reads only the FIRST tool call of a single completion and cannot see a two-step plan («work out who Tyler is, then look him up»). A question naming nobody ambiguous is what made the difference visible; the earlier cases are reported but carry no signal. (5) **What the docstring rewrite itself cost, also measured.** A first draft replaced «what they like» with «what they are into» and led with a paragraph steering to `read_buffer`; that scored 11/16 where the name alone scored 16/16. Restoring the lexical overlap with the question and shortening the steer to one clause recovered it to 16/16 with the anti-episodic correction intact. Two iterations, not tuned further: n=16 on one model is not a budget for fitting prose. (6) **`nora_recall_total` keeps its name.** Metric names are append-only in this repo (`nora.obs.metric_specs`' own docstring, R-17's argument): renaming does not move history, it starts an empty series beside a frozen one. The `tool` LABEL on `nora_room_lookups_total` DID move, because that value must agree with what `_log_call` writes to `data/nora.log` or the line stops being greppable by the tool's name — which is the entire reason DEC-28 added those lines. (7) **Two prose gates had to be told about it, and that is `about`'s standing cost.** `nora.doctrine.loader` keeps it on the ordinary-English `tool_names` list beside `now`. `tests/test_prefill.py::test_the_header_names_no_tool_at_all` used a raw substring, and `_context_header` legitimately contains «what you durably remember ABOUT the person who called you» — so that test now uses two detectors, the same split the doctrine loader already has: substring for names no sentence contains, catalogue shape (`- <name>:`) for the two that are ordinary words. Narrowed deliberately and stated, rather than contorting generated prose around a test. (8) **`read_buffer` is deliberately NOT renamed here** (GLOSSARY calls it misnamed too). Renaming both at once makes the selection measurement above unreadable — the `earlier` case is precisely a `read_buffer`-vs-this-tool decision. | `tests/test_room_tools.py` (the whole file, under the new name), `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py::test_the_registry_exposes_every_declared_tool` and `::test_budgets_match_todays_table`, `tests/test_prefill.py::test_the_header_names_no_tool_at_all` (the two-detector split), `tests/test_composition.py::test_the_root_hands_the_loader_the_tool_names_its_gate_needs` and `tests/test_doctrine_loader.py::test_a_backticked_one_word_tool_is_caught_when_the_registry_is_supplied` (both now inject `about`, which is only catchable via `tool_names`). | DEC-40 | **`transit(route)` resolves the OPERATOR itself, from a route catalogue, and declares itself live-only — the first grounding capability (`design/proposed/14-grounding.md` §3, §5.1, §5.5).** (1) **Two operators, and the route number does not say which.** `68X` is KMB (`GET data.etabus.gov.hk/v1/transport/kmb/route-eta/{route}/{service_type}`), `K73` is MTR (`POST rt.data.gov.hk/v1/transport/mtr/bus/getSchedule`), and asking the wrong one returns `200` with an empty body. Under DEC-24 the model is never asked to supply an operator: it is a fact Nora can look up, so she does — the KMB route list (791 routes, republished 05:00 daily) and MTR's route CSV (26 routes, monthly; there is **no** MTR route-list endpoint, only the one its Data Dictionary's appendix points at). Both are fetched in one `asyncio.gather` and cached on the `ToolRegistry`'s client for six hours, because a per-tag client would re-pull 350 KB for every question anybody asked. (2) **The catalogue is what makes `nothing_found` honest** — see R-27. (3) **`time_shiftable` joins `budget`, `when` and `terminal` on `NativeTool`/`ToolSpec`**, three-valued: `None` (no time dimension — `calculate`), `False` (live only — `now`, `transit`), `True` (takes a target time — nothing yet; `route()`'s `arrive_by` is the first). `ToolRegistry.live_only_tools()` renders it into the generated capability block, so a declared field is not decoration: `rt.data.gov.hk` cannot time-travel, and 「仲有 2 分鐘」 quoted for a 19:30 departure is checkably wrong. (4) **The typed envelope is NOT wrapped `untrusted="true"`** (design/14 §2.1) — numbers, booleans, RFC 3339 instants and destinations from a published route table are closer to `listGroups` than to a web page. The one exception the same paragraph names is honoured: KMB's `rmk_en` is an operator-authored SENTENCE ("This route only operates on Sundays and Public Holidays" — the only statement of why every `eta` is null), so that field alone is escaped and wrapped. (5) **Read from the published specifications, and three things they say that four probes did not.** MTR's `status` is documented `"1" = normal` / `"0" = error or alert` — the inverse of how it reads — and the live endpoint returns `"0"` for a good route and a fake one alike, so nothing reads it. `isScheduled` separates a TIMETABLE entry from a bus the system can see, which is the difference between two identical-sounding sentences. `arrivalTimeInSecond` is `"108000"` at every origin stop — a thirty-hour sentinel, not an ETA — and `departureTimeInSecond` goes negative for a bus that has left. (6) **No config section and no key.** Three published government endpoints and one timeout, all constants: they are properties of the feeds, not of this deployment. | `test_an_unknown_route_is_never_reported_as_no_service`, `test_the_live_only_tools_are_the_ones_whose_answer_decays` |
| DEC-41 | **`transit(route, near=...)` — the place catalogue is a COMPONENT behind the existing tool, never a tool of its own (`design/proposed/14-grounding.md` §5.2).** (1) **`near` is the only new model-facing surface.** The model names WHAT — a place somebody actually said — and `nora/infra/transit/places.py` resolves WHERE, the same division `Room` draws for people: no `place_lookup` tool, and stop ids, coordinates and sequence numbers never appear in any schema (DEC-24). The model's `near` string never reaches a URL; only dataset-authored stop ids do. **The scope boundary lives in that module's docstring and it is places only, never a person** — the day a Life360/Places half is added (design/14 §5.2) it inherits this seam, and nothing in the module consults `identity.toml`, Honcho, the roster, or any store that knows a human. (2) **Two stop datasets, both keyless, joined to the ETA responses on the operators' own keys.** KMB: `GET /v1/transport/kmb/stop` (6,740 rows, ~1.2 MB) intersected with the per-route route-stop lists, then `eta/{stop}/{route}/{service_type}` per resolved pole. MTR: `mtr_bus_stops.csv`, whose `STATION_ID` (`K73-D070`) is the SAME string `getSchedule` sends as `busStopId` — that equality is the join the resolver stands on. At a named stop `arrivalTimeInSecond` is the answer where it is real (past the origin), screened against the thirty-hour `108000` origin sentinel; both halves verified live 2026-08-06. Cached like the route catalogue: `StopDirectory` on the registry-owned client, six-hour TTL, and a dataset that never loaded is `None`, never empty (R-27b's hinge). (3) **Matching is exact-after-normalisation first, widened twice, each widening bounded and for a measured reason.** The wrapper punctuation set is `room.py`'s, against `name_tc` AND `name_en`; every KMB name carries a trailing `(TN970)`-style code, so the code is set aside or the flagship query 「天恆」 could never exact-match anything KMB runs; only when exact finds nothing does a substring pass run (query ≥ 2 chars, capped at 24 matches). (4) **Ambiguity is an answer, never a guess.** Matches within ~150 m are one place (the three 天恆邨總站 poles span ~12 m); further apart they come back as `could_be` candidates to ask about — 輕鐵 legitimately means two K73 stops 2.2 km apart, and picking one silently is this feature's version of the wrong confident answer. On-route resolution runs FIRST, then existence — which is what keeps `not_on_route` and `not_found` distinct (R-27b). (5) **The docstring rewrite regressed selection and the fix was measured, not argued (DEC-39's discipline, same harness, deepseek-v4-flash, `tool_choice="required"`).** With the near text woven through the schema, plain 「K73 幾時到？」 fell from 31/48 to 14/48 first-tool `transit` (the loss absorbed by `now`, z≈3.5). Restoring the pre-change anchor sentence — «You give it a route number.» — and compressing the `near.status` steer to one clause recovered 22/32, with the near-shaped case at 12/16 and `near="天恆"` extracted on every transit pick. One iteration; n=16-per-run on one model is not a budget for fitting prose. (6) **`near` is the fourth model-supplied string echoed inside a tool result** (after `lookup_person`'s name, `calculate`'s expression and `route`), so `near.asked` — and the dataset-authored names beside it — go through the same identifier screen at the tool layer, where the scanner is reachable. | `test_a_place_the_route_does_not_serve_is_never_no_such_place` (R-27b), `tests/test_transit.py::test_near_times_come_from_the_named_stop_not_the_origin`, `::test_kmb_near_asks_the_per_stop_eta_endpoint`, `::test_an_ambiguous_place_is_an_answer_not_a_guess`, `::test_colocated_terminus_poles_are_one_place_not_an_ambiguity`, `::test_unreadable_stop_data_is_unavailable_not_place_not_found`, `::test_a_resolved_stop_with_nothing_showing_is_its_own_sentence`, `::test_the_tool_passes_near_through_to_the_resolver`, `::test_the_tool_never_echoes_an_identifier_from_the_near_argument`.
