| DEC-42 | **`tool_choice` is a SEQUENCE, not a constant — `required` until she has spoken, `auto` after — and that required one `OpenAIModel` per tag first.** (1) **Why `required` cannot simply be dropped.** Measured 2026-08-06, `opencode-zen/deepseek-v4-flash`, `reasoning_effort="low"` (design/16 §2.1): under `auto` on the FIRST call the model answers in plain prose 5/6 — no tool call, therefore no message, therefore Nora silent in the group while the turn looks successful — and end to end, `auto` throughout sent **0 messages 6/6**. That is R-5's exact failure shape, and relaxing globally (the obvious simplification) would have shipped it. Against that, `auto` AFTER a reply exists stops cleanly 8/8 where `required` never stops 6/6. So `required` is what makes her speak at all and `auto` is what lets her stop. (2) **The cap is not the stopping mechanism and must not be asked to be one.** With no way to stop early a cap becomes a quota that is always spent in full, and every question gets the maximum number of messages. The cap is a backstop. (3) **The flip lives in `ReplyGuard._before_model`, contrary to design/16 §4's first two blockers.** `BeforeModelCallEvent._can_write` is `return name == "cancel"`, which guards attribute assignment ON THE EVENT — it says nothing about reachability. `HookEvent.agent` is a required dataclass field and `OpenAIModel.update_config` exists, so `event.agent.model.update_config(params=…)` is the route. Pinned against the real SDK by `tests/test_strands_api_contract.py::test_obligation_7_…`, including the fact that `update_config` REPLACES `params` (it is `self.config.update(...)`), which is why `nora.agent.tool_choice.relax` reads, copies and writes the params dict back whole rather than passing one key and dropping `max_tokens`, `temperature` and `reasoning_effort` for the rest of the turn. (4) **The second blocker was real and is fixed by `factory.ModelSource`.** `nora.app.composition` built ONE `OpenAIModel` per process and handed it to `LiveAgentRunner`; `EngagementPipeline._in_flight` keys on `(platform, conversation_id)`, so two conversations run concurrently and flipping `tool_choice` for tag A would have flipped it for tag B's FIRST call — i.e. the fix for R-5's failure shape would have REINTRODUCED R-5's failure shape, non-deterministically, only under concurrent load. The model is now built per tag over the process-wide `AsyncOpenAI` client, which keeps audit T2's actual guarantee (one CLIENT per process is what pools connections) and is the identical argument `factory.RetryPolicy`'s docstring already makes for `ModelRetryStrategy`. It also makes `reasoning_effort` per-tag, the escape hatch `config.toml` already flags as needed. (5) **The turn now ends at the CAP rather than at the first terminal call.** DEC-32's `_turn_is_over` fired the instant a terminal verb RAN, which is why `send_reply` could never produce a second message however high its budget went — the loop was cancelled before the model was asked again. It now fires when NO terminal verb can be called again (every terminal tool's budget spent). At `send_reply`'s shipped budget of 1 the two predicates are identical, which is what makes this ship INERT. The emptiness check is load-bearing: `all(())` is True, so a guard told nothing is terminal would otherwise cancel before the first model call and every tag would go silent. (6) **`Deliverer` gains a REQUIRED `message_cap`, and it is the same number.** DEC-32 established that the cap must be one number — R-7's budget check runs before R-2's latch, so two numbers that disagree resolve silently in favour of the smaller and file the refusal under the other one's name. `deliver.build` therefore reads `message_cap` out of the same `budgets` mapping it hands the guard, and the guard asks the deliverer `exhausted` rather than `attempted`. No default on `message_cap`: a default of 1 would let "raised the budget, forgot the deliverer" construct cleanly and cap her at one message anyway, which is the invisible half of the trap. `tests/unit/test_engagement_pipeline.py` turns six tests red if that wiring is broken. (7) **`Deliverer.last_text` is DELETED rather than generalised.** DEC-36 already removed the tag path's feed of Nora's reply, so its only remaining property was being wrong under multi-message — a four-message reply would have fed the last message and dropped three. Structural absence beats a comment. The tag path still feeds exactly ONE Honcho document per tag, carrying the tagger's words only, whatever the message count. (8) **R-25's counter is not optional.** Multi-message's own failure is a turn truncated between messages, and that turn is invisible to everything R-23 built. (9) **`llm.limits.messages` is NOT added**, per DEC-32 and the plan's §5e: `NATIVE_TOOLS["send_reply"].budget` IS the cap, and a config key beside it would be two numbers for one question. **That budget was 1 at ship** — the mechanism shipped inert and turning it on is an operator's `NATIVE_TOOLS` edit plus optionally the `voice.md` edit that decides how talkative she is (neutral doctrine measured at exactly 1 message 6/6; an invitation to chat like a person, 3–5). **ACTIVATED 2026-08-07 — the cap is now 3:** the single `NATIVE_TOOLS["send_reply"]` budget edit DEC-42 anticipated (1→3), and nothing else in the machinery moved (`Deliverer.exhausted`, `_terminal_budget_spent`, the `_spoke`/`_turn_is_over` split all already read this one number — the property DEC-10 exists for). The `send_reply` docstring carries the guidance: multipurpose, not chunking — a distinct message for a distinct person, never one thought split. `voice.md` is deliberately LEFT UNCHANGED: the conservative default holds because `tool_choice` relaxes to `auto` after the first send, so 3 is a backstop and not a quota (DEC-42(2)); if she proves too terse in practice, the talkativeness line is a later operator-only doctrine edit, measured. (10) **`tests/unit/test_engagement_pipeline.py::ScriptedRunner` is now `nora.testing.FakeAgentRunner`.** It was a second, hand-written replay that called `guard.evaluate`/`guard.record` directly and never dispatched `BeforeModelCallEvent` at all — the divergence DEC-33 already removed once, kept alive in a second file, and made actively misleading by this change: a multi-message scenario would have "passed" against a mechanism it never exercised. `nora.testing.FakeAgentRunner` now dispatches a before-model event carrying an agent and a fresh per-invocation `ScriptedModel` whose `update_config` replaces `params` exactly as the SDK's does. | `tests/test_model_per_tag.py` (the per-tag probe: two concurrent tags, B's first model call held until A has been relaxed — share one model and it reads `auto`), `tests/test_guards_terminal.py::test_a_terminal_tool_with_budget_left_does_not_end_the_turn` and `::test_a_raised_terminal_budget_buys_a_second_model_call` (real SDK loop), `tests/test_strands_api_contract.py::test_obligation_7_the_tool_choice_flip_is_reachable_from_a_before_model_hook`, `tests/unit/test_engagement_pipeline.py::test_multi_message_is_active_and_the_default_cap_is_three` (was `..._still_produces_exactly_one_message`, the inert proof, now the active-default proof), `::test_the_cap_tracks_the_send_reply_budget_and_nothing_else` (was `..._raising_the_send_reply_budget_is_all_it_takes`), `::test_the_canary_and_r18_run_on_every_message_not_just_the_first`, `::test_every_message_gets_its_own_audit_row`, `::test_a_multi_message_turn_still_feeds_honcho_exactly_once`, `tests/test_scripted_agent_fidelity.py::test_the_double_relaxes_tool_choice_after_a_terminal_verb_runs` |
| DEC-43 | **Media is bytes Nora HOLDS, keyed by the sha256 of its content — and the placeholder taxonomy is driven by the registered media type, not by prefix matching.** `design/18-media-custody.md`. (1) **The violation.** A9 says SQLite is THE RECORD; for media it held `[image]` plus an implicit pointer into signal-cli's own store, under signal-cli's own retention. Prune that daemon or re-link the device and the row claims an image nothing can produce. (2) **Custody.** `nora.ingest.stages.media` copies the bytes at ingest into `nora.infra.blobs.FilesystemBlobStore` (`data/media/<first2>/<rest>`), and the hash lands in `messages.media_json` in the SAME INSERT as the body naming it — INLINE and ahead of the audit write, deliberately NOT spawned like `memory_feed`, because a spawned copy would write the promise first and keep it second, which is the same violation at a smaller size. Hashing the CONTENT rather than the operator's original filename-plus-metadata sketch is what buys dedup, verifiability and rename-stability; metadata rides alongside the key and never produces it. (3) **Filesystem behind a port, not RustFS** — because NO MEDIA HAS ARRIVED YET (signal-cli's store has no `attachments/` directory at all), so an object store would be sized against zero observed data, and it would put a container that can be DOWN on the ingest write path. Its lifecycle rules are the one feature not needed: media expiry is the prune loop Nora already runs plus one path. `BlobStore` is `put`/`get` — S3's shape — so the swap is one constructor when there is evidence. (4) **Bytes never reach a prompt**: type + key only, and no filename is rendered any more (it was sender-supplied text of unbounded shape going straight into the body she reasons over). (5) **The taxonomy fix, which is a live defect**: `contentType.startswith("image/")` sent everything else to `[attachment: <filename>]`, so a voice note arrived as `[attachment: 1754521883.aac]` and Nora could not tell that a human had SPOKEN. RFC 6838 top-level types now decide, with `mimetypes` as the filename fallback and `voiceNote` as a separate axis from `audio/*`. (6) **Degrades honestly**: no bytes means no handle in the body, the message still recorded in full, and `nora_media_custody_total{status}` naming the reason — with `nora_media_attachments_total` as the companion that can measure ABSENCE. (7) **RETENTION IS AN OPEN OPERATOR DECISION**, stated as such in design/18 §8 rather than defaulted: nothing deletes blobs yet, `FilesystemBlobStore.delete` and `[media] enabled = false` are the mechanism and the off switch, and the five questions the operator has to answer are listed there. | `tests/contract/test_blob_store_contract.py`, `tests/contract/test_media_source_contract.py` (one suite over both implementations each), `tests/unit/test_media_custody.py`, `tests/unit/test_ingest_pipeline.py::test_custody_runs_before_the_audit_write_so_one_row_carries_both`, `tests/test_signal_parser.py::test_a_voice_note_says_that_somebody_spoke`, `tests/infra/test_alembic.py::test_0004_adds_media_json_without_disturbing_the_ts_desc_index`, `tests/infra/test_schema_parity.py::test_media_json_is_the_last_declared_column` |

Its only parameter was a raw platform `sender_id` the model is never shown — DEC-24's inert parameter in pure form, a value the model can only INVENT — and unlike the three room-scoped tools it was not scoped to the conversation at all: its own comment admitted a model that already held (or guessed) another person's platform id could pull that person's card ACROSS conversation boundaries. The tool read a card by a model-supplied id; the block is fed by Nora from the resolved tagger. Deleting the tool does not touch the block or its feed. (4) **`_context_header` needed no change** — DEC-29 already removed every tool name from it, so there was no `read_peer_card` line left to drop; the `peer_card:` block description it carries is about the prefill block, which remains. | DEC-45 | **Adopt RFC/ISO standards where they genuinely fit; skip them where they do not, and say why.** DEC-44 is the tools agent's; this is the standards-adoption pass. (1) **BCP 47 (RFC 5646) language tag — a LIVE bug fixed.** `DoctrineStore` already took a `language_hint`, defaulted `""`, and rendered it into every tag's `runtime_context` as `language: <hint>` — but the composition root never passed it, so the line shipped BLANK and the model was never told, from outside, what to speak. The value is now `doctrine.language_hint = "yue-Hant-HK"` (Cantonese, Traditional Han, Hong Kong), wired through `nora.app.composition` and `evals/harness.py` for fidelity. It REPLACES the invented `config` string `chinese_script = "traditional"`, which `find_referencing_symbols` confirmed **nothing read** — a two-value enum that could not carry script AND region, standing beside a real bug it did not cause and did not fix. A standard tag beats a convention only this repo understands. Probe: `test_the_runtime_context_carries_the_configured_language_tag` (drop the wiring → `language:` blank → red). (2) **RFC 3339 timestamps — audited, one machine-read site fixed.** The audit's rule: a timestamp Nora EMITS into a tool return or a machine-read log must be RFC 3339; human-facing prose must not be forced into it. `nora.infra.transit.arrivals` already normalises every emitted instant to RFC 3339 with a `+08:00` offset (clean, unchanged). `prefill._now_line`'s "Thursday 6 August 2026, 15:06 (HKT)" and `nora.app.report._when`'s operator paste-output are HUMAN-FACING and stay prose. The one defect: `nora.obs.logging`'s structured `ts` was `%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S` — naive, no offset — while the JSON lines are ingested by a log store that sorts and windows on it. Now `%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ` with `utc=True`: second precision preserved, ambiguity removed. Probe: `test_the_ts_is_an_rfc_3339_instant_not_a_naive_local_string` (`fromisoformat(ts).tzinfo is None` → red before the fix). (3) **RFC 2119 register — the loader GATE was the win; the wording rewrites were SKIPPED, measured-or-not.** design/17 §4's kinds map onto RFC 2119: `constraint` → MUST, `conduct` → SHOULD, `identity`/`voice` → descriptive/neither. `check_normative_register` refuses the contradiction — an advisory keyword weakening a hard rule, an absolute one hardening a habit, or any normative keyword in a descriptive file — the mechanical companion to `check_kind_boundaries`. It is UPPERCASE-scoped (RFC 2119 §8: force lives in the capitalisation), so lowercase "should"/"must" in prose never trips it and the shipped doctrine passes UNREWRITTEN (grep confirms zero uppercase keywords in `nora/doctrine/*.md`). **No doctrine WORDING was changed.** DEC-39's discipline says a wording change must be justified by the live-gateway selection harness (`tool_choice=required`, n≥16); that harness returned **401 with no `OMNIROUTE_API_KEY` in this environment**, so the measurement could not run — and the operator's deliberately natural register (§the hard line: never flatten `voice.md`/`identity.md` into normative language) is not churned on an unmeasured hunch. Gate yes, rewrites no. Probes: `test_a_constraint_written_in_should_is_a_category_error`, `test_a_conduct_line_written_in_must_is_a_category_error`, `test_a_descriptive_file_carrying_any_normative_keyword_is_rejected`, `test_the_register_gate_ignores_lowercase_keywords_in_ordinary_prose`. (4) **RFC 6838 media types — ALREADY DONE (DEC-43), verified, untouched.** The media-custody merge drove the attachment taxonomy off RFC 6838 top-level types; nothing to do. (5) **ISO 4217 currency in `calculate` — deliberately NOT adopted.** The operator asked for a GENERIC calculator (DEC-35: a tool is a capability, not a scenario). Currency is presentation, not arithmetic; a currency code would make a capability into a scenario, the exact shape DEC-35 deleted from other tools. Recorded as a non-adoption, not an omission. | `tests/test_composition.py::test_the_runtime_context_carries_the_configured_language_tag`, `tests/test_logging.py::test_the_ts_is_an_rfc_3339_instant_not_a_naive_local_string`, `tests/test_doctrine_loader.py::test_a_constraint_written_in_should_is_a_category_error`, `::test_a_conduct_line_written_in_must_is_a_category_error`, `::test_a_descriptive_file_carrying_any_normative_keyword_is_rejected`, `::test_the_register_gate_ignores_lowercase_keywords_in_ordinary_prose`. | DEC-46 | **`route(origin, destination, mode, arrive_by)` — Google routing + a Places resolver, the third grounding capability and the first `time_shiftable=True` tool (`design/proposed/14-grounding.md` §3, §5.1, §5.2).** (1) **Paid, external, keyed — the opposite of `transit` on every axis.** `POST routes.googleapis.com/directions/v2:computeRoutes` with `X-Goog-Api-Key` and a field mask, verified live 2026-08-06 (天水圍站 → Yoho, TRANSIT, 358 s). Three states (`ok` / `no_route` / `unavailable`) with a `{tool,status}` counter; `no_route` (Google answered, no way to connect) and `unavailable` (the call failed) are held apart by a sentinel, the same ok/nothing/down split R-27 draws for transit. (2) **`arrive_by` is the point — the FIRST `time_shiftable=True` tool** (DEC-40 reserved the field for exactly this). Google Routes accepts `arrivalTime`, so `route` can answer about a departure hours out; it is set only for TRANSIT (a transit-only field on this API) and rendered into the capability block as NOT live-only, beside `now`/`transit` which are. (3) **The place resolver is a COMPONENT, not a tool** (§5.2, operator-confirmed): no `place_lookup` verb — `route` takes place NAMES and `nora.infra.routes.places` (Google Places Text Search New, `zh-HK`/`HK`) turns each into a coordinate that never appears in any schema (DEC-24). Ambiguity is an answer: results are proximity-clustered (one mall's entrances are one place; 打邊爐 2 km apart are two), and a genuinely ambiguous name comes back as `no_route` with `*_could_be` candidates to ask about, never a guessed point sent to Google. (4) **THE RESOLVER IS A SCOPE BOUNDARY — places only, never a person.** Its module docstring says so and it consults no store that knows a human — the lower half of §2.2's guarantee (the upper half is R-28). (5) **The field mask is a bill.** `X-Goog-FieldMask` names ONLY the rendered fields (duration, distance, per-leg transit details — line/vehicle/agency/stop/time/headsign/stop-count) and deliberately omits the polyline and `navigationInstruction` (Google step prose, free text, dearer SKU) and place `reviews` (stranger-written) — the two free-text surfaces this API returns. So the typed envelope is NOT wrapped `untrusted="true"` (§2.1) and there is nothing to wrap; the dining bonus (rating, priceLevel, openNow) is typed and unwrapped. (6) **Attribution / no-caching honoured for free by DEC-36:** a route result rides in Nora's REPLY, and her reply is never fed to Honcho (only the tagger's own words are), so no Maps content enters the semantic store as her conclusion. (7) **Config `[route]` is optional and OFF by default** (`api_key_env = "GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY"`, DEC-8 — the value never in config); the registry holds no `RouteClient` until the composition root wires one, and the tool answers `unavailable` (never silent-empty) while unwired. R-28 is the egress guard. | `tests/test_routes.py` (three states + counter, field-mask discipline, `arrive_by` sent for transit and not for driving, resolver ambiguity/not-found/unavailable, dining fields typed-unwrapped, never-raises); `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py::test_every_tool_declares_whether_it_can_be_asked_about_another_moment`. | DEC-47 | **Media is encrypted at rest, and image EXIF is stripped BEFORE the content is hashed.** Operator decision 2026-08-07; extends DEC-43, whose custody shipped merged-but-inert. Turns custody ON via `nora.app.composition`, and adds the two protections the operator required. `design/18-media-custody.md`. (1) **EXIF strip (images only).** A photo's EXIF carries GPS coordinates and a device id — worse PII than the image — and content addressing would otherwise dedup on EXIF *differences*, making the same picture with two GPS tags two blobs. `nora.ingest.exif.strip_exif` (Pillow: open → re-encode with no metadata) runs in `nora.ingest.stages.media._take` BEFORE `blobs.put`, so the content key is the key of the PICTURE and the location PII is gone from what Nora holds. It is images-only and never raises: an image Pillow cannot decode falls back to the original bytes (logged, still encrypted). NON-IMAGES (audio/video/documents) have NO metadata stripped — there is no cheap universal stripper — and that is stated, not hidden. (2) **Encryption at rest.** `FilesystemBlobStore` seals every blob under AES-256-GCM (`nora.infra.blobs.cipher.BlobCipher`) and writes CIPHERTEXT; a JPEG's `FF D8` never appears on disk. The key hashed for the content address is of the PLAINTEXT (dedup and the `[image a3f2c1]` handle unchanged); a fresh 12-byte nonce per blob is stored alongside the ciphertext (not secret — reuse is what is fatal, and content addressing writes a blob at most once, so a nonce never seals a second plaintext under one key). `get` opens the ciphertext and RAISES `InvalidTag` on a wrong/corrupt key rather than returning garbage — the same "unreadable is not absence" split `get` already drew for a bad disk. Nothing on the ingest hot path decrypts (the text model never reads bytes); a future `media_fetch` tool is `open`'s only caller. (3) **Degrade honestly, never plaintext-by-accident.** The cipher is REQUIRED on the store — there is no plaintext-on-disk mode. Custody is ON only when `[media] enabled` AND `media.encryption_key_env` names an env var holding a valid base64 32-byte key; a missing/malformed key is custody OFF (`_build_media_custody` returns `(None, None)`, warned), which is the `not_configured` state `nora.ingest.stages.media` counts — the deliberate asymmetry with the LLM key (`config.load` refuses to start without that; media is a bonus and must never take the daemon down or write plaintext). DEC-8: name the env var, never inline the secret. (4) **WHERE is co-equal with WHO, and rode the message row — NO new table, NO new index.** `media_json` sits on `messages`, which already carries `conversation_id` (WHERE), `sender_id`/`person_id` (WHO) and `ts` (WHEN), and `idx_messages_conv_ts` already indexes `(conversation_id, ts DESC)` — so "what media in THIS group, by whom, when" is answerable through the existing row and index with NO schema change. Metadata (`sha256`/`media_type`/`size`/`filename`) stays CLEAR; only the bytes are encrypted, which is what makes 「呢個group尋日啲相」 answerable without decrypting. (5) **Fixture-only.** No real attachment has ever arrived on this deployment — every EXIF/encryption probe runs on Pillow-built images, and the `getAttachment` success path stays as unverified as DEC-43 left it. | `tests/unit/test_media_encryption.py` (ciphertext-on-disk, `FF D8` absent, round-trip byte-identical, per-blob nonce, wrong-key raises, EXIF absent after strip, same-picture-different-EXIF dedups to one blob, non-image unstripped), `tests/test_composition.py::test_media_custody_is_off_when_the_key_env_is_unset`, `::test_a_valid_key_turns_media_custody_on_with_an_encrypting_store`, `::test_a_malformed_key_degrades_to_off_rather_than_crashing`, `::test_media_disabled_is_off_even_with_a_valid_key`; DEC-43's suites extended (`tests/contract/test_blob_store_contract.py` runs the encrypting store) |
| DEC-48 | **Nora's FIRST skill — `navigation` — and the on-demand skill layer it needed, shipped INERT.** design/12 §1 deferred skills until a domain existed ("Nothing qualifies yet"); route + transit (DEC-46, R-27) made wayfinding the first that does — combining a schedule and a live wait is instructions, not capability, which is what a skill is (design/14 §1). (1) **The skill is JUDGEMENT, not mechanism.** Which endpoint to call and how to fan out is the tool's job; ask-the-person-who-knows and read-the-age-on-a-fact are universal doctrine. What is left is true of moving around and nothing else, and it is genuinely small — four points: a journey's TIME decides which answer is real (a schedule far out, a live countdown only near departure, the live wait refining the plan's first leg, never replacing it — the `time_shiftable` split of DEC-40 read as judgement rather than as a field); an arrival time is a plan run BACKWARDS (「八點到」 → a leave-by moment, refined only as it nears); one destination from several origins is SEVERAL plans, each addressed to its person, plus a question to whoever's departure — which is intent, not position (design/14 §5.3) — is unknown; and a driver (wants parking near the destination) and a passenger (wants the way there) are not asking the same question. It names NO scenario (design/17, operator-insisted: a skill that names the dinner run fails the airport run), restates no tool docstring and no doctrine, and states when to load AND when not to (a bare 「K73 幾時到」 is one tool call, not a plan, and does not earn the turn a skill costs — design/12 §4). (2) **Why a NEW loader and not the doctrine loader.** Doctrine goes into `system_prompt=` on every turn; a skill's body must NOT (design/12 §1's placement rule), arriving only on activation, with just its name and when-to-load line in the prompt (progressive disclosure). design/17 §4 names five doctrine kinds and "skill" is not one. So `nora.skills.loader.SkillStore` is a separate, framework-free module (no `strands`, no `config` import — `tests/test_boundaries.py` boundaries hold), reading the AgentSkills.io SKILL.md shape (`name` + `description` frontmatter) so the files load unchanged under `strands.vended_plugins.skills.AgentSkills` the day activation is wired. (3) **The authority boundary is R-29** — skill sources are operator config only; `resolve` is a pure name lookup that cannot turn message content into a loaded skill. (4) **INERT, the DEC-42 shape.** Nothing constructs a `SkillStore` in `nora.app.composition` and no body reaches the model, because activation needs a model-facing tool with an R-7 budget and that table is `nora.agent.tools.NATIVE_TOOLS`. Turning it on is the operator's edits (a `skills` budget there + `plugins=[AgentSkills("nora/skills")]` in `nora.agent.factory`); until then the skill is loaded, validated and boundary-guarded but un-invokable. **ACTIVATED 2026-08-07 — see DEC-50, which is those two edits, the measurement this caveat deferred, and the R-29-survives-activation verification.** **Honest caveat:** because it ships inert and un-injected, its effect on tool selection is unchanged BY CONSTRUCTION (the live prompt is byte-identical) — no ADR-0004 measurement gates this, and none is claimed; the measurement belongs to the activation edit, not here. | `tests/test_skill_loader.py` (parsing, scanning, dir-name match, progressive-disclosure metadata carries name+when but no body, and R-29's boundary probe), `tests/test_skill_content.py` (the shipped skill loads, names no scenario, says when-to-load and when-not, stays small), `tests/test_acceptance_rules.py::RULE_TESTS["R-29"]` |
| DEC-49 | **A tag for a busy conversation is QUEUED, not dropped, and the 👀 acknowledgement moves to admission (R-30).** The old admission dropped a second concurrent same-conversation tag with `nora_tags_dropped_total{reason="in_flight"}` — so three people tagging at once produced one reply and two silences, counted but indistinguishable in the room from being ignored, which is R-5's shape aimed at people. Two changes. (1) **The 👀 fires at ADMISSION, before queueing or handling**, for both the run-now and the queued tag — moved out of `_run_admitted` into `EngagementPipeline.run`/`_begin_indicator`. `indicate.begin` only spawns, so it adds no latency; the effect is that persons 2 and 3 see they were SEEN while they wait, and the ambiguous silence stops. A REJECTED (self-tag/debounce) or QUEUE_FULL (flood) tag still gets no 👀 — neither is a person waiting, and a 👀 that never resolves is the stuck-forever bug DEC-17 exists to avoid. (2) **A per-conversation FIFO queue** (`nora.engagement.queue.ConversationQueues`, keyed by `(platform, conversation_id)`), drained one turn at a time from the pipeline's `finally` (`_dispatch_next`): it releases R-10's lock, re-acquires it for the next dequeued tag, and spawns that turn — with NO await between release and re-acquire, so no incoming tag races into the run-now path. **Why serialise a conversation (correctness, not latency):** each turn's context is assembled at DEQUEUE time, so tag 2 sees tag 1's reply already in the transcript; two concurrent same-conversation turns would each reason from a stale room. **Why it is not a latency concern:** nobody in a group chat expects instant replies — a person answering three messages takes over a minute, so 45s for person 3 is normal; the queue exists to NOT LOSE PEOPLE, not to be fast. **Bound, not staleness:** `engagement.queue.max_depth` (default 16, `nora.config.QueueConfig`) guards unbounded growth (a flood) by shedding the NEWEST arrival past the bound as `nora_tags_dropped_total{reason="queue_full"}` — a counter distinct from debounce and from the now-removed `in_flight` reason; a new `nora_tags_queued_total` observes queue use. There is deliberately NO staleness rule: elapsed time is a weak proxy for "the group moved on", and dropping a tag someone still waits on is the exact failure removed here. Composes with R-9 (debounced tags never enqueue) and R-10 (the lock still guards the single in-flight turn). Per-CONVERSATION state only, deleted the moment a conversation's queue drains — `tests/architecture/test_no_retained_per_tag_state.py` still holds. R-30, after the skill took R-29 (R-26 is reserved by `design/proposed/18` for unshipped context-budget work). | `tests/unit/test_engagement_pipeline.py` (FIFO order, queued tag sees the earlier reply, different conversations still concurrent, flood shed with its own counter, queued tag gets its 👀 promptly), `tests/unit/test_engagement_admission.py` (queued/queue-full/rejected verdicts), `tests/unit/test_engagement_queue.py` (FIFO/isolation/drain-retains-nothing/bound) |
| DEC-51 | **The record store is PostgreSQL, and the "just a driver swap" framing hid three non-portable defects the migration actually fixed** (`design/proposed/20-postgres-migration.md`, §§1–2, §4). (1) **`ts`/`quoted_ts` were `sa.Integer` → INT4; Signal's millisecond timestamps (~1.78e12) overflow INT4's ~2.1e9 ceiling.** On SQLite `INTEGER` is 8-byte so it never showed; on Postgres every message write would raise `NumericValueOutOfRange`, silently under R-5. Now `sa.BigInteger`; `id` stays INT4 (bounded forever by retention) and `is_nora` 0/1, so the INT8/INT4 asymmetry is deliberate and complete. (2) **The SQLite Alembic chain (0001…0004) cannot replay on Postgres** — `0001_baseline` emitted `DEFAULT (datetime('now'))`, type-checked at `CREATE TABLE`, which Postgres rejects. Squashed to ONE Postgres-native baseline (`0001_pg_baseline`) carrying BigInteger `ts`, `id GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY` (BY DEFAULT so the §5 id-preserving load is legal), and `created_at DEFAULT now()::text` (a bare `now()` is timestamptz with no implicit cast into TEXT — itself a DDL error the review caught). `idx_messages_conv_ts` is hand-written raw DDL, autogenerate-excluded. (3) **asyncpg has no `lastrowid`** — `append` uses `insert(...).returning(id).scalar_one()`. The SQLite WAL apparatus (`_note_busy`, `nora_store_busy_total`, the `OperationalError` handling) is deleted: MVCC removes the single-writer contention it existed to soften, proved by a 50-way concurrent-append test. DB URL is `DATABASE_URL` (DEC-8, never inlined), wired through the composition root; media custody's on-disk root moved from `db_path.parent` to `store.data_dir`. `AuditStore`→`RecordStore` renamed (GLOSSARY). | `tests/test_migration.py` (Signal-magnitude ts round-trips through the store; reverting `ts` to Integer turns it red; only messages+alembic_version tables), `tests/infra/test_engine.py` (asyncpg write/read; read-only engine refused a write at the server), `tests/infra/test_audit_store.py` (RETURNING id, concurrent appends land), `tests/contract/test_audit_store_contract.py` (the Postgres store passes the SAME suite as `FakeRecordStore`), `tests/infra/test_alembic.py` (single baseline builds + `alembic check` clean) |
| DEC-52 | **Every Nora-owned container shares one private `nora` network; external services are reached via `host.docker.internal`** (`design/proposed/20` §3). The record store's URL is `DATABASE_URL` and the read-only control plane is a Postgres read-only engine (`default_transaction_read_only=on`, server-enforced) pointed at a `GRANT SELECT`-only role — replacing SQLite's `mode=ro`; the operator CLI runs via `docker exec` into a `nora`-network container. **CODE HALF DONE, COMPOSE HALF DEFERRED to the operator (constraint-sensitive):** the `docker-compose.yml` networking change, moving signal-cli off host networking, the read-only role/`nora-db` service, and the live cutover are left as reviewed additions (`deploy/compose/nora-db.compose.yml`, `deploy/postgres/load_messages.sql`), not applied — CLAUDE.md constraint 3 makes the signal-cli move a live-verified ship-condition. | `tests/infra/test_engine.py::test_the_readonly_engine_refuses_a_write_at_the_server`, `tests/test_cli_control_plane.py::test_the_store_the_cli_actually_opens_refuses_a_write` (read-only engine refuses `set_person_id`); compose/cutover verified live by the operator, not in CI |
| DEC-53 | **DEC-11's schema-parity gate is rebuilt on PostgreSQL catalogues** (`design/proposed/20` §2). It reads the `ts DESC` flag from `pg_index.indoption`'s DESC **bit** (`indoption[i] & 1`, NOT `== 1` — a `DESC NULLS FIRST` column is `1|2=3`, and `information_schema` cannot see ordering at all), asserts the `conversation_type` CHECK from `pg_constraint`, and diffs a normalised `pg_dump --schema-only --no-owner --no-privileges` against a checked-in baseline. **Oracle scope is stated honestly: metadata+baseline, not live-production** — a frozen dump catches drift-from-baseline; the `nora` database publishes no host port, so the live-production half is a `docker exec nora-db pg_dump` job, named not dropped. | DEC-50 | **The navigation skill is turned ON — the two activation edits DEC-48 named, measured, with R-29 verified to survive.** DEC-48 shipped the skill and its loader INERT and named exactly what activation needed; this is that activation. (1) **Edit one — a `skills` budget in `nora.agent.tools.NATIVE_TOOLS`.** `AgentSkills` registers a model-facing `skills(skill_name)` activation tool, and R-7 default-denies any tool absent from `budgets()` (`guards.py`) — so without a budget `ReplyGuard` vetoes every activation call and the skill can never load, the same DEC-42 inert shape. The entry carries a new fifth `NativeTool` field, `plugin=True`: it is present ONLY so R-7 admits the tool, and is FILTERED OUT of everything the registry BUILDS or RENDERS — `ToolRegistry.capability()` and `tool_names()` skip it — because the offline/eval compositions build the capability block from this same table but supply their own runner and never wire the plugin, so a `- skills — …` line there would promise a tool they do not have. The budget is the one thing that must be shared; the description is the one thing that must not. **budget 1**, sized like `group_members`: re-activating a skill re-injects a body already in context (the `read_buffer`-clamp cost) and cannot improve the answer; at 1, R-7's repeat veto is exactly right, and the day a second skill ships and one message spans both, it is a one-line raise (DEC-10). `terminal=False` (activating is the START of using judgement, never the end); `time_shiftable=None` (no time dimension, like `calculate`). (2) **Edit two — `plugins=[AgentSkills(skills_dir)]` in `nora.agent.factory`.** `create_agent` gains `skills_dir`; when set it builds ONE `AgentSkills` plugin PER TAG — the same per-tag isolation the model (`ModelSource`) and guard already have, its `WeakKeyDictionary` skill-state never spanning two tags — which registers the activation tool and injects an `<available_skills>` block (each skill's NAME + when-to-load line, NOT its body — progressive disclosure, design/12 §1) into the system prompt before each invocation. The path is threaded composition → `LiveAgentRunner(skills_dir=)` → `create_agent`, resolved as `cfg.doctrine.dir.parent / "skills"` (skills sit beside doctrine under `nora/`). `skills_dir=None` leaves the layer inert (offline/eval, and any un-wired deployment): no plugin, no block, no tool — the pre-DEC-50 prompt byte-identical. **NO compose mount needed:** `nora/skills` is baked into the image (`Dockerfile COPY nora/`) and loads per tag, so — unlike the mtime-hot-reloaded `nora/doctrine` bind mount — it needs no host mount. (3) **R-29 SURVIVES activation, verified.** After wiring, the runtime activation path is `AgentSkills.skills`, not `SkillStore.resolve` — but it is a PURE DICT LOOKUP against the operator-loaded set (`_skills_for(agent)`), no more permissive than `SkillStore`: filesystem/URL loading happens ONLY from `skills_dir` (operator config) at `init_agent`, never from the `skill_name` a message supplies. Probed live: with `navigation` loaded from the operator dir, an absolute path, a `..` traversal, an `https://` URL, an inline `---` blob and an unknown bare name ALL return "not found" and load nothing; only `navigation` ever activates. Item 6 can name a shipped skill, never introduce a source. (4) **MEASURED (ADR-0004), the measurement DEC-48 deferred.** Real assembled doctrine + real tool schemas + real `ReplyGuard`, live `opencode-zen/deepseek-v4-flash` at the tier's `reasoning_effort=low`, the REAL agent loop (not `tool_choice=required` first-tool — the skills tool is auto-selected mid-loop), n=12, BEFORE=`skills_dir=None` (inert) vs AFTER=wired. **Non-navigation did NOT regress** — the key risk (prompt weight pulling routing onto a new tool) did not materialise: `skills` fired 0/12 on a greeting, on 「你係邊個」 and on a person question in AFTER, and the person question selected `lookup_person` 12/12 + `about` 12/12 identically in both conditions. **Plan questions activate reliably**: `skills` 0→12/12 (「幫我計劃八點去 Yoho」) and 0→9/12 (arrival-time plan). **Edge, honest**: 「K73 頭班車幾點」 reaches `transit` 12/12 and never `route` or `skills` in both conditions — a first-bus schedule question the live-only `transit` tool cannot answer and the skill correctly declines to load (a bare live check is not a plan); a clean first-bus answer is beyond today's toolset, stated not hidden. **Honest cost**: on the arrival-time plan the extra `skills` turn competed with `route` inside `turns=8` (`route` 11→6); activation delivers JUDGEMENT (arrival-run-backwards, schedule-vs-live), which is reply QUALITY the tool-count trajectory cannot see, not more tool calls — so the plan-quality benefit is asserted by DEC-48's design review, not by this selection measurement, which shows only that activation is reliable and non-regressing. **Verdict: ship** — no non-nav regression, R-29 holds, all four gates green; not a STOP. | `tests/test_factory.py` (`test_skills_dir_none_leaves_the_on_demand_layer_inert`, `test_wiring_skills_registers_the_activation_tool`, `test_wiring_skills_injects_metadata_not_bodies`, `test_r29_survives_activation_only_the_operator_dir_loads`), `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py` (`test_skills_is_budgeted_so_r7_admits_it_but_the_registry_does_not_build_it`, `test_skills_declares_terminal_false_and_no_time_dimension`, budget/spec tables updated), `tests/test_skill_loader.py`/`::RULE_TESTS["R-29"]` still hold (the framework-free loader is unchanged). ADR-0004 numbers above are a live-gateway measurement, not a pinned test |
| DEC-54 | **Presence is a PLAINTEXT table in the `nora` database, not a separate encrypted store — and its disclosure guard is a NEW fail-closed seam, not existing R-21 (`design/proposed/19-presence-collector.md` §3, §5; R-31).** The first draft's «separate `presence.db`, R-tree, `BlobCipher`-encrypted» was withdrawn by the operator: it predated the Postgres decision and its encryption story was incoherent for a queried relation (you cannot index coordinates you have encrypted, and every presence query is keyed on `circle_id`/`person_id`/`ts`, never on a coordinate — no PostGIS, no R-tree). So `presence_fixes` joins `messages` and identity on `person_id` in one database (design/20), `ts` is `BIGINT` (Life360 fix timestamps are epoch ms — the same INT4 overflow `messages.ts` fixes), and only media *bytes* stay encrypted (DEC-47) — presence is queried, media is write-once. (1) **The residual liability is NAMED, not glossed:** `presence_fixes` is the most sensitive data Nora holds; the compensating controls are that the `nora` DB publishes no host port and disclosure is subset-of-circle checked. (2) **The disclosure seam is the INVERSE of the room tools' R-21, and nothing else enforced it** — both reviews flagged «the same rule the roster uses» as an overstatement. The room tools *resolve within scope* (can't name someone not in the room); presence must *refuse to disclose a circle member into a room containing someone outside the circle*. `nora.agent.whereis.DisclosureGuard` intersects the conversation's `RoomView.people` with the circle roster and **refuses (not omits)** on any member outside it — fail-closed, and fail-closed too on a degraded/unverifiable room. `check_disclosure_invariant` is the startup check that alarms the day «group ⊄ circle» (`nora_presence_circle_subset_ok` → 0). (3) **`whereis` carries the fix's AGE and degrades to `unavailable`, never null-island** (§7.6); outbound position stays hard-blocked (design/14 §2.2, R-28 untouched). (4) **Shipped as a tested SEAM, INERT** — `[presence] enabled=false`, and `whereis` is not yet in `NATIVE_TOOLS`, the same inert shape the navigation skill shipped in (DEC-48); wiring it live is a later measured step. | `tests/test_whereis.py` (`test_whereis_refuses_to_disclose_outside_the_circle` = R-31, `::test_whereis_is_unavailable_and_never_null_island_without_a_fix`, `::test_whereis_refuses_on_a_degraded_room_it_cannot_verify`, `::test_whereis_returns_the_latest_fix_with_its_age`, the two startup-invariant probes), `tests/infra/test_schema_parity.py` (the `(circle_id, person_id, ts DESC)` index covered by the parameterised gate), `tests/test_config.py` (`[presence]` optional + inert default), `RULE_TESTS["R-31"]` |
| DEC-55 | **The presence collector is a CHANGE-RECORDER in a SEPARATE process, with a bounded 403 path and in-daemon liveness (`design/proposed/19` §1, §4; R-32).** (1) **Change-recorder, not sampler.** The study measured that polling buys ZERO freshness — a stationary phone returns the identical fix for many minutes — so `PresenceStore.record_fix` writes a row ONLY when the member's `ts` strictly advances past the last stored `ts`. Two reads of an unmoved member produce ONE row; the write metric (`nora_presence_fixes_total`) counts DISTINCT fixes, not polls. (2) **The 403-loop is the ban risk, and the client will not hammer.** `Life360Client` caches the token in memory, re-auths at most ONCE per read, and a 403 that a FRESH token also gets returns `banned` (alarmed via `nora_presence_auth_alarms_total`) rather than looping — the exponential back-off (the WAITING) lives in the collector loop, which lengthens its cadence with `auth_failures` (`cadence * 2^failures`, capped hourly). Auth is HTTP Basic + `grant_type=password` + the Life360 app **User-Agent** (the study's missing ingredient), all named-via-env (DEC-8). (3) **The canary lives in the daemon** (`nora_presence_canary_total`): a non-JSON challenge page and an unexpectedly-empty circle both alarm; Nora never sees this fragility, she reads the local table. `wire_state` (live/stale/sharing_off/disconnected/no_fix/unavailable) is counted per member per cycle, so «sharing off» is a KNOWN state, not a gap. (4) **Liveness is the daemon's** (`nora_presence_last_collection_age_seconds`): the heartbeat climbs when up-but-not-collecting, which a frozen fix count cannot show (R-5). (5) **A separate process** (`python -m nora.presence`, its own composition root and metrics endpoint) sharing only the `nora` DB and the client library — either can be down without the other; prune (180 days) is a periodic sweep off the write path. The compose service and live cutover are DEFERRED review artifacts (`deploy/compose/presence.compose.yml`), not applied. | `tests/infra/test_presence_store.py` (`test_an_unmoved_member_is_recorded_once` = R-32, out-of-order/per-member dedup, time-window prune), `tests/test_life360_client.py` (fake Life360 server incl. 403 + challenge shapes; `test_a_persistent_403_backs_off_and_never_loops`, token cached, challenge is its own status), `tests/test_presence_collector.py` (change-recorder end-to-end, empty/non-JSON canary, per-member state counting, heartbeat climbs, back-off grows/caps), `RULE_TESTS["R-32"]` |
| DEC-56 | **`media_fetch` — reading a stored image back through MiMo-V2.5, the READ half of media custody (`design/proposed/21-media-fetch.md`, R-33).** Custody (DEC-43/47) is the WRITE half: inbound attachments are content-addressed, sealed and recorded as `media_json`; nothing read them back. This is that read — the operator-idea (2026-08-07) that Nora should be able to LOOK at an image the group sent, via `opencode-go/mimo-v2.5` through the process-wide OmniRoute client (probe-verified end to end 2026-08-07: a red PNG → "Red", prompt_tokens 159). (1) **New collaborators, no ingest change.** The OmniRoute `AsyncOpenAI` client, the blob store and the vision config were NOT on the tool-construction path; `media_fetch` threads them onto `ToolRegistry` exactly like `transit`/`route` (`vision`/`blobs` params, a per-process `VisionCache`), and the write path is untouched. (2) **THE BLOCKER — its own timeout and byte ceiling.** The whole agent loop runs inside one `asyncio.wait_for(llm.timeout_seconds)`; a MiMo reasoning call with a generous `max_tokens` is seconds, so two of them plus the text turns would overrun it as the loop's silent `invocation_timeout` (R-5). `nora.infra.vision.VisionClient` is a RAW `chat.completions.create` (none of `OpenAIModel`'s retry/timeout/trace) under a timeout DERIVED from `llm.timeout_seconds` (`derive_vision_timeout` = `min(configured, loop × VISION_TIMEOUT_FRACTION=0.5)`, provably shorter — the way `factory.build_retry_policy` derives its backoff), one bounded retry inside that single budget, and a `vision_max_bytes` ceiling well below `media.max_bytes` (100 MiB → a ~133 MiB base64 body); over either → `unavailable`, never the loop's timeout. (3) **`store.get` wrapped:** None (absent) AND a raised `InvalidTag`/`ValueError` (corrupt / wrong key) both map to `unavailable`; the tool never raises into the loop. (4) **Cache by content hash, screened on every serve.** The sha256 IS the content, so a bounded in-memory LRU (`VisionCache`, on the registry) keyed by it is valid forever; it stores the RAW description and `scan_identifiers` runs on every SERVE — a second `media_fetch` of the same hash makes NO MiMo call, and a cache hit can still not bypass the screen. (5) **States:** `ok` / `nothing_found` (no such image in view — R-21 scope, resolved within `bound_conversation_id`'s `media_json` over a window ≥ prefill + `read_buffer` reach) / `ambiguous` (two in-scope blobs share the 6-hex prefix — 24 bits, never pick-first) / `not_an_image` (a voice note / video / file, gated BEFORE any MiMo call) / `unavailable`. (6) **The description is UNTRUSTED egress** (design/14 §2.1) — identifier-screened like `about`, with the HONEST caveat that `scan_identifiers` catches an ACI/UUID/key/`+`E.164 but NOT a bare local number (a residual risk EQUAL to R-18's, stated in code and tests, not papered over). (7) **Config, not literals:** `vision_model` / `vision_max_tokens` / `vision_reasoning_effort` (`low`) / `vision_max_bytes` / `vision_timeout_seconds` are real `MediaConfig` fields (`extra="forbid"`). (8) **Gated behind `[media] enabled` + key + a reachable OmniRoute client** (any absent → `unavailable`, the keyless-`route` shape); a `NATIVE_TOOLS` budget of 2, `terminal=False`, `time_shiftable=None`. `[media] enabled=false` today, so it ships INERT-by-config. Image only; audio/video are a later table entry. **DEC number reconciled at merge:** this branch's index runs to DEC-49, and 50–55 are consumed by sibling branches (`21-media-fetch.md` §7 reserved DEC-56 to avoid the collision), so the gap here is deliberate. | `tests/test_media_fetch.py` (scope, cache-no-second-call, screen-on-every-serve incl. the bare-local-number caveat, ambiguous/not_an_image/absent/wrong-key/inert states), `tests/test_vision.py` (derived timeout < loop, byte ceiling never sent, hung→timeout, one bounded retry, never-raises), `tests/test_acceptance_rules.py::RULE_TESTS["R-33"]` |
| DEC-57 | **RETIRED by DEC-73 (2026-08-08): SearXNG + Firecrawl + `web_fetch` replaced by Tavily; the SSRF apparatus and egress.sh deleted. Historical text below.** **`search(query)` + `fetch(url)` — SearXNG search and Firecrawl page fetch, the fourth grounding capability, wrapped untrusted and shipped INERT (`design/proposed/22-search-fetch.md`, design/14 §2.1/§3).** (1) **Two verbs, one inert capability.** `search` is `GET {searxng_url}/search?q=&format=json` (SearXNG, a metasearch PROXY — «the query never leaves the host» was false, design/14 §2.3); `fetch` is `POST {firecrawl_url}/v1/scrape` with `formats:["markdown"]`. Both live behind one `[search]` config section that defaults `enabled=false` and stays so until the operator turns it on AND Firecrawl's egress isolation is in place — while off the registry (`nora.agent.registry.ToolRegistry`) holds no `SearchClient` and both answer `unavailable`, never a silent empty (R-5), the `route`-with-no-key shape. (2) **The SSRF control is Firecrawl's egress, not Nora's precheck** — the v2 revision's whole point: Firecrawl follows redirects and re-resolves DNS itself, so a public URL that `302`s to `127.0.0.1:18080` or DNS-rebinds is fetched against Nora's internals and Nora never sees it. R-34 is the defence-in-depth Nora's own code holds (per-tag `allowed_urls` fence + `is_global`/`getaddrinfo` precheck); the packet-layer egress isolation is the operator ship gate (§0/§5.1), and a green unit suite means «Nora refuses naive URLs», NOT «SSRF closed». (3) **Retrieved bytes are wrapped `untrusted`, reusing `nora.infra.transit.arrivals._wrap`** (R-13, escape-safe): a page controls its own snippet/body, so a snippet AND a page are `<untrusted source="URL">escape(text)</untrusted>`, and a body of `</untrusted>` becomes `&lt;/untrusted&gt;` and cannot forge the boundary. Retrieved text activates no skill (R-29 — `SkillStore.resolve` is a pure name lookup) and enters only as a tool RESULT, never a prompt position. (4) **The query is an egress surface** (design/14 §2.2): `scan_identifiers` (NOT `scan_reply` — 「搵首 haiku」 must work, DEC-30) blocks an identifier before it reaches SearXNG; a person alias is COUNTED not blocked (`nora_search_alias_total`, over `_known_names(persons)`), because a block would fail closed on legitimate use before the query shape is known. (5) **`fetch` deliberately does NOT `scan_identifiers` the page** (unlike `about`): a public web page is not Nora's people-data and stripping a whole page is impractical; the identifier boundary for anything RELAYED is `scan_reply` at `Deliverer` (design/22 §3, finding 5). (6) **Bounded by constants, not a component** (design/14 §4): `search_max_results` (~6) and a per-snippet byte clamp on `search`, `fetch_max_chars` on `fetch`, truncation STATED in the return value — retrieved text lands in the INPUT and is re-sent every subsequent call (`tokens × turns-remaining` against `total_tokens=64000`). (7) **Three states** (R-27) `ok`/`nothing_found`/`unavailable`; `fetch` adds `refused` (not in the fence, or non-`is_global`). Budgets `search` 2 / `fetch` 2, `terminal=False`, `time_shiftable=None`. (8) **Firecrawl API version PINNED to the deployed instance** — verified `v1` on the running `firecrawl-api` container (`127.0.0.1:3002`); both `/v1/scrape` and `/v2/scrape` answer with an identical `{success, data:{markdown, metadata:{url, sourceURL}}}` envelope today, and `v1` matches design/14's measurement. SearXNG `format=json` disabled → 403 → `unavailable` (the fake returns it; a ship-time check must confirm a live search returns `ok`, not merely reachable — the 61-day-silent shape). **Remaining operator gate (design/22 §5.1–§5.2, NOT in this build):** Firecrawl container egress network-isolation, and the real-Firecrawl redirect-to-localhost SSRF integration probe (no fake can prove it — the bypass runs inside Firecrawl). See R-34. **SUPERSEDED at runtime by DEC-69 (2026-08-08): the operator ENABLED `fetch_enabled` WITHOUT the egress-isolation ship gate, accepting the SSRF residual — «shipped INERT» is now the original design only; live state is on, R-34's defence-in-depth holding alone.** | `tests/test_search.py` (SearXNG json shape + 403-disabled → unavailable; Firecrawl real envelope + failed-scrape wrong-shape; the `allowed_urls` fence, the `is_global`/numeric/rebind gate, the `</untrusted>` forge, the result/byte/char caps, alias counted-not-blocked, identifier blocked, model-name allowed, never-raises, unavailable-when-unwired), `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py` (the two tools + budgets), `tests/test_acceptance_rules.py::RULE_TESTS["R-34"]`, `tests/test_env_template.py` (the `[search]` section) |
| DEC-58 | **The MEASURED tool-rename pass on the full 13-tool set — one rename APPLIED, two DECLINED, and the two the task expected were both overturned by the number (DEC-39's methodology, verbatim).** The harness: the real assembled `applies_to=tag` doctrine as system prompt (generated capability block AND the live `<available_skills>` block), the real 13 tool schemas from `build_tool_specs` + the `AgentSkills` `skills` tool, `opencode-zen/deepseek-v4-flash` via OmniRoute at `reasoning_effort=low`, `tool_choice="required"` first-tool selection. Each candidate holds the docstring BYTE-IDENTICAL and changes ONLY the identifier — the schema `name` and the capability block's `- <name> —` line. Baseline (n=16) is 16/16 on each tool's own question: `read_buffer`←「頭先講咗啲咩」, `group_members`←「邊個喺度」, `lookup_person`←「阿豹係邊個」. (1) **`read_buffer` → `load_transcript`: APPLIED.** GLOSSARY's pending honest rename (nothing is buffered; it reads durable rows off disk). Self 16/16 → 16/16, and it pulled NOTHING off the others — 「邊個喺度」 stayed `group_members` 16/16 and 「阿豹係邊個」 stayed `lookup_person` 16/16 under the new name. Renamed everywhere it is the tool's identity (the `NATIVE_TOOLS` key, the closure, `_spec`, the `_record`/`_log_call` label, `CHAT_EARLIER_MAX`/`DEFAULT`, `about`'s docstring pointer, and every `nora/`+`tests/`+`evals/` reference); the `nora_tool_calls_total{tool}` LABEL moves with it (it must agree with the `_log_call` grep — DEC-39's rule), the metric NAME is unchanged (append-only). The R-7 named test moved `test_tool_call_budget_enforced_for_read_buffer` → `…_for_load_transcript` (RULE_TESTS updated). (2) **`group_members` → `room_members`: DECLINED, on a measured regression.** Self 16/16 and 「頭先講咗啲咩」 clean, BUT it REGRESSED a DIFFERENT tool: the person-identity question 「阿豹係邊個」 (whom `lookup_person` answers 48/48 at baseline: 16/16 + a 32-run confirm 32/32) mis-routed to `room_members` **7/48 (14.6%)** — z≈2.75, essentially DEC-39's own rejection threshold. The mechanism is DEC-39's exactly: 「邊個」 (who) is shared between "who is X" (identify) and "who is here" (membership), and the membership-shaped name **room**_members bleeds the identify question onto itself where **group**_members took 0/48. A name is the feature the router matches on; this one competes with its sibling. Recorded as a non-rename, the way DEC-39 recorded `person_profile` 0/16. (3) **`lookup_person` → `who_is`: DECLINED, though it did NOT regress.** The task flagged this SUSPECT expecting risk; the number said the opposite — `who_is` was clean on every axis (self 「阿豹係邊個」 16/16, 「邊個喺度」 stayed `group_members` 32/32-confirm so it stole nothing, 「頭先講咗啲咩」 clean). Declined anyway for MOTIVE, not measurement: `lookup_person` is not misnamed (no GLOSSARY entry, unlike `read_buffer`/`recall`), the task named no honest target, and DEC-39 established this tool as the person-axis ANCHOR — moving the anchor's name on one clean candidate at n=16, when a sibling's rename (2) just showed how fragile the person question's routing is, is unmotivated risk. The clean numbers are recorded so the decline is transparent (declined for lack of a defect to fix, not for measured harm). **The lesson, twice over:** the task predicted `room_members` safe and `lookup_person` risky; measurement inverted both. Measure, do not assume. | The applied rename keeps the existing suite green under the new name (`tests/test_tools.py`, `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py::test_the_registry_exposes_every_declared_tool` and `::test_budgets_match_todays_table`, `tests/test_guards_replyguard.py::test_tool_call_budget_enforced_for_load_transcript` (R-7), `tests/test_tool_logging.py`, `tests/test_turn_budget.py`). The selection numbers are not a test — they need a live gateway and cost ~256 model calls a run; they are recorded here and in `nora/agent/tools.py` beside the `load_transcript` name. The two declines change no code |
| DEC-59 | **`whereis` goes LIVE — wired into `NATIVE_TOOLS` over DEC-54's seam, with the R-21/R-31 pre-flight on Nora's side, the same day the collector deployed (2026-08-07).** The activation DEC-54(4) deferred, taken now that its two prerequisites hold: the R-28 coordinate barrier landed FIRST (`_looks_like_coordinate`, council #4), and the collector is live and recording (compose `presence` service, verified against `presence_fixes`). (1) **The registry holds the collaborators the way it holds `route`:** a `PresenceStore` READER sharing the audit engine (one pool; the collector process owns writes/prune) plus `circles_from_persons(persons)` — identity.toml grouped by `circle_id`, LENIENT where the collector is strict (an unplaced block is skipped, an empty result leaves `whereis` answering `unavailable`; Nora must not refuse to START over a tool that can honestly say «I can't check»). (3) **Multi-circle composes without weakening R-31:** one `Whereis` per circle, first `ok` wins, a non-`refused` answer outranks a refusal (the refusal is about the ROOM/circle pair, not the person) — and no circle the room is not a verified subset of ever discloses, because every circle's answer still comes through `Whereis.locate`'s own guard. (4) **The startup invariant runs on Nora's side at last** (design/19 §7.7): `_presence_preflight` is a BACKGROUND task (never a startup gate — an unreadable roster must not stop Nora) that resolves every `policy.toml` conversation through `Room` and publishes `nora_presence_circle_subset_ok` per circle; unmapped accounts are WARNED as what the gauge cannot see, the per-call guard staying authoritative. | `tests/test_whereis.py::test_the_whereis_tool_is_unavailable_when_presence_is_not_wired`, `::test_the_whereis_tool_answers_through_the_wired_seam`, `::test_the_whereis_tool_never_discloses_via_a_circle_the_room_is_not_in`, `::test_the_whereis_tool_refuses_when_no_wired_circle_contains_the_room`, `::test_circles_from_persons_groups_by_circle_and_skips_unplaced`, `tests/test_tools.py::test_every_declared_tool_is_built`, `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py` (tool set, budgets, live-only + capability clock line) |
| DEC-60 | **Named places resolve on-host, and a resolved place's position may leave to Google ONLY as the direct execution of the person's own trip request (`design/proposed/23-named-places.md`, R-35).** The operator directed the build 2026-08-07, with §1's boundary map as his precondition and «caution on the circle ID» as his instruction. (1) **The governing rule: coordinates and geometry stay server-side; the model handles names.** `whereis` gains a `place` field — a fix inside a saved place's geofence answers with the household's own name («喺屋企»), computed by a pure haversine-vs-`radius_m` check at answer assembly; place AND address ride together so the model never guesses between them, and raw lat/lng remain the LAST fallback only. `calculate` is deliberately NOT extended and no geometry tool exists at the model surface — the model never holds two coordinates to compare, and a geometry tool would create the exact coordinate-handling surface everything above avoids. (2) **Resolution is hybrid, ordered, LOCAL:** the `route` tool builds a per-room casefolded name→`Place` map (the guard needs the room, so the map is built in the tool layer, not the process-wide client) from `presence_places`, `RouteClient` consults it BEFORE Google Places — a hit means the NAME never reaches Google and the stored coordinate travels only as the Routes waypoint the request itself requires; a seam is left for later sources (operator aliases, Honcho). The R-28 model-facing coordinate barrier is untouched: only the server-side resolver may place a coordinate in an outbound call. (3) **R-35 — subset-scoped, fall-through, never a refusal:** a room that is not a verified subset of the circle (or holds an unmapped account, or could not be read) resolves NOTHING and the name goes to the public lookup as an ordinary string — deliberately the INVERSE of R-31's loud refusal, because a hard refusal would CONFIRM to an outsider that the household has a place by that name, while fall-through makes a guarded name indistinguishable from an unsaved one (R-21's «not being here ≡ not existing», applied to a dictionary entry whose existence is the secret). (4) **Capture is the collector's** (phase 1, same commit family): `GET /v3/circles/{id}/places` (probed live 2026-08-07) behind the same one-re-auth-per-read discipline, `presence_places` replaced wholesale at most hourly riding the member cycle AFTER a clean read, and `parse_places` trusts the ASKED circle over the body's `circleId` echo — the operator's circle-id caution made mechanical. Circle ids stay in identity.toml-derived flow, the presence tables and metric labels; never in config, replies, tool results or log messages. | `RULE_TESTS["R-35"]` (`tests/test_whereis.py::test_a_non_subset_room_resolves_no_saved_place`), `tests/test_whereis.py::test_whereis_names_the_saved_place_the_fix_sits_inside`, `::test_whereis_outside_every_geofence_answers_without_a_place`, `::test_an_unreadable_roster_resolves_no_saved_place`, `tests/test_routes.py::test_a_saved_place_hit_skips_google_places_entirely`, `::test_an_unsaved_name_falls_through_to_the_public_lookup`, `tests/test_life360_client.py` (places reads: typed wire, 403-bounded, challenge), `tests/test_presence_collector.py` (hourly refresh, failed-read keeps rows, no places call on a failed member read), `tests/infra/test_presence_store.py::test_replace_places_round_trips_and_removes_what_the_wire_dropped` |

Three honesty rules: (1) one fix has no direction, so the keys are ABSENT — never a guessed boolean; (2) inside a geofence, containment WINS and the movement read is skipped («at Home» and «moving» cannot both be the headline); (3) the docstring tells the model these are measurements, not intentions — hedge («好似向緊屋企方向»), never promise a destination or an ETA. | DEC-66 | **Honcho bedrock — Nora's OWN model of each person (observer/observed), `ask_memory`, and the clean workspace topology (design/proposed/24 §S, operator-settled 2026-08-08).** Honcho keys memory by **(observer, observed)** peer pairs; Nora joins every session with `observe_others=True` (humans `observe_me=True`, `HonchoClient._configure_peer`), so the deriver builds `(nora → person)` — her model of someone from what she witnessed, distinct from their self-model. All recall reads `(observer=ai_peer, observed=person)`, workspace-wide; the room roster gates the SUBJECT (R-21/R-31), not the query. Live-proven: a probe peer's job+hobby returned from one observed message; empty without the config. (1) **The clean topology (operator drilled):** **workspace = a group of PEOPLE** (a social unit), **session = a CHANNEL** that same unit uses (Signal/Discord/WhatsApp group), **peer = a person**. Cross-session blend within a workspace is safe by construction — same humans, different apps, one social context; two social units never share a workspace. Two orthogonal scopes: workspace = what she may KNOW together, roster = whom she may SAY. (2) **`ask_memory(person, depth)`** — the dialectic verb (`HonchoClient.chat`, `target`=observed), Nora's escalation from `about` (retrieve→reason); `depth` is the MODEL's per-question choice (minimal→max), slow (~14-19s), budget 1, roster-gated, identifier-bearing answer withheld whole. `about` rescoped to `(observer=ai_peer, target=person)`. (3) **Session-id sanitised** (`4c9f107`): Honcho requires `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$`, a Signal group id is base64 (`+/=`) → 422 → the WHOLE feed path was silently dead on real groups (offline used clean ids); `_safe_conversation_key` maps base64→url-safe, hash fallback. (4) **C1 (review):** the ai_peer observe-config re-fires on the cache-hit path, so a transient failure retries not disables. (5) **`[agent].name`** (`0fcad88`) makes the agent's machine identity config (Strands/trace id), so a clone ("delia") is config + doctrine files. (6) **Granularity RESOLVED:** one workspace for alpha; future per-social-unit `workspace_id` via identity.toml → `workspaces.toml` (the real phase 1). `search_memory` (Honcho raw-message search) designed not built — workspace-scoped is safe under the topology, roster-speaker-filtered + R-18 when built. (7) **GRADUATED disclosure by fidelity (Kimi audit 2026-08-08, made explicit):** the recall/presence verbs deliberately gate differently by how sensitive their output is — `about`/`ask_memory` return a DISTILLED model and answer even in a degraded/partial room (carrying a `partial_view` flag); `where_is` returns a LOCATION and refuses fail-closed in a degraded or `unnamed>0` room (R-31). Distilled < raw < location was three sensitivities, three gates, one intentional model; DEC-68's row records why `search_memory` (RAW words) moved off the room-wide gate onto a per-message one (operator direction 2026-08-19) rather than staying grouped with `where_is` here. | `tests/test_honcho.py` (target on the wire, observer-config retry, base64→legal session id, chat target+depth), `tests/test_honcho_representation.py` (target rides the body / absent when omitted), `tests/contract/test_memory_store_contract.py` (chat in the shared contract), `tests/test_room_tools.py` (ask_memory reasons from Nora's model, roster-blind, depth fallback, identifier withheld), `tests/test_factory.py::test_agent_name_defaults_to_nora_and_flows_to_the_trace_identity`, `tests/test_tools.py` + `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py` (set + budgets) |

The operator: "nora is asking WHERE IS, not where they used to be." So before it reads, `where_is` now forces a fresh reading. (1) **The COLLECTOR owns it** (operator's chosen boundary): a new `POST /refresh?person_id=` on the collector's HTTP server (`nora/presence/__main__.py`) calls `PresenceCollector.refresh_member` → `Life360Client.request_location` (the app's SILENT refresh button — `POST .../members/{id}/request` with `type=location`; the person is NOT notified) → a BOUNDED poll of `read_circle` until a fix newer than stored lands or `refresh_timeout_seconds` (8s) → records it through the SAME `_record` path, so the on-demand reading PERSISTS to the DB. So no Life360 credential lands in Nora and `where_is` stays a reader. (2) **Nora side:** `HttpLocationRefresher` (`nora/infra/presence_refresh.py`) POSTs `/refresh` before `Whereis.locate` reads — only after disclosure passed and the person resolved (never for someone the room may not ask about). (3) **NEVER load-bearing:** a timeout / unreachable collector / refused ping returns False and `where_is` reads the last stored fix — the on-demand path can be down without `where_is` breaking. `_record` now returns whether it wrote so `refresh_member` knows the fresh fix landed. Wired by `[presence].refresh_url` (empty → old read-only behaviour). R-28 intact: a location REQUEST sends a member-id, not a coordinate. | `tests/test_life360_client.py` (request_location pings with type=location, false on refusal), `tests/test_presence_collector.py` (refresh_member pings+records the fresh fix, unknown person, bounded timeout), `tests/test_presence_refresh.py` (HttpLocationRefresher 200→true, non-200/unreachable→false never raises), `tests/test_whereis.py` (forces a live refresh before reading, falls back to the stored fix on failure) |
| DEC-68 | **`search_memory(query)` — semantic search over the raw MESSAGES, the tightest-gated recall verb (design/24 §S.3, operator 2026-08-08).** The third Honcho recall verb, distinct from `about` (retrieve a distilled model) and `ask_memory` (reason): it finds the exact WORDS somebody said, further back than `load_transcript` reaches. `HonchoClient.search` → `POST /workspaces/{ws}/search` `{query, limit}` → a LIST of message objects (its own parse, not `_post`'s dict), returning `(peer_id, content)` pairs and dropping media-only `￼` lines. WORKSPACE-scoped, which is SAFE under the one-social-unit-per-workspace topology (§S.1). Because it surfaces RAW words (higher-fidelity than a distilled model), the tool is the tightest-gated: (1) it keeps ONLY messages from the room's roster speakers (never Nora's own peer, never an off-room peer); (2) R-18 drops any line carrying an identifier. Budget 4 (fast, ~0.33s). On the `MemoryStore` port with the shared contract suite (both backends). *Loosened by operator direction 2026-08-19: originally also REFUSED the whole search — before ever querying Honcho — in a room with anyone Nora can't place (`view.unnamed > 0` or degraded), the same room-wide fail-closed shape as `whereis` (R-31). In practice every real group has at least one unmapped account, so this refused everywhere and the tool was effectively dead. The guard is now PER MESSAGE, not per room: an unmapped account's own words never come back (same roster-speaker filter as (1) above, which made the room-wide check redundant with it), but a NAMED person's words surface regardless of who else is in the room, and a DEGRADED view (roster unreadable) still searches — narrowed to just the tagger's own words, since `roster` then holds only them. `search_memory` no longer records a `refused` status at all (`nora_room_lookups_total` dropped from six values to five).* | `tests/test_honcho.py` (search parses the list, drops media placeholders, empty on failure never raises), `tests/contract/test_memory_store_contract.py` (search on the port, both backends), `tests/test_room_tools.py` (roster-speaker-only + R-18 screen, nothing_found, an unmapped account present doesn't block a named person's hit, a degraded view still searches the tagger's own words), `tests/test_tools.py` + `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py` (set + budgets) |
| DEC-69 | **Governance reconciliation — three operator-accepted egress/risk decisions the code shipped but the record didn't (operator 2026-08-08, Kimi audit).** Housekeeping DEC: the code already does these; this is where they become auditable. (1) **`web_fetch`/`web_search` ENABLED accepting the blind-SSRF residual.** `config.toml [search] fetch_enabled = true` was flipped WITHOUT the packet-layer egress isolation DEC-57/R-34 named as the ship gate. `deploy/egress.sh` is written but NOT persistently applied — its `raw/PREROUTING` rules match Firecrawl by container IP and silently reopen after every `docker compose up` of Firecrawl or Nora (DEC-69.3 below), with no canary to signal they're gone. So the live SSRF control is R-34's defence-in-depth ALONE (the `allowed_urls` fence + `is_global`/`getaddrinfo` precheck + `sourceURL` re-check) — bypassable at the connection Firecrawl actually makes. The operator accepts this residual for the alpha; the «shipped INERT» framing in DEC-57 is the original design, superseded here. (2) **The presence collector egresses a member COORDINATE to Google (or HK-gov) to reverse-geocode it** (`nora/infra/geocode.py`, resolving that module's flagged follow-up). This is a real coordinate egress and does NOT violate R-28/DEC-24: R-28 governs what leaves toward the MODEL/GROUP, and the collector is neither — the coordinate goes host→Google, the resolved NAME is all that reaches Nora, and `where_is`/the model never see a coordinate. Accepted and now recorded; the geocoder is fail-soft (a geocode outage costs a name, never a cycle). (3) **`POST /refresh` (DEC-67) has no rate-limit or coalescing, accepted because the surface is INTERNAL** — compose-network only, sole caller `where_is` (budget 2/tag), `auth_failures` back-off is the real ban guard. A second internal caller reopens it (per-person cooldown + concurrent coalescing the fix). See the handler docstring in `nora/presence/__main__.py`. **egress.sh persistence + a reopen canary — BUILT (operator "go" 2026-08-08), pending a one-time root install.** `deploy/systemd/` adds `nora-egress.timer` (reapply every 2 min), `nora-egress-watch.service` (docker-event reapply the instant a Firecrawl surface container starts — IPs change on `compose up`), and `egress.sh reapply`/`canary` subcommands; `canary` probes firecrawl→host (TIMEOUT=caged, REACHED/refused=open), exit-coded + a `nora_egress_cage_intact` textfile metric. The root gate is PRESERVED — reapplication runs as root via systemd, NOT a NOPASSWD sudoers (which would let a compromised container rewrite the very firewall this cages). Install once: `sudo deploy/install-egress-units.sh`. Canary confirmed live detecting the currently-open cage (REACHED 307). | Governance record; the behaviours are tested at their sites (`tests/test_search.py` R-34 fence/precheck, `tests/test_geocode.py` bilingual fail-soft, `tests/test_presence_collector.py` refresh). No new test — this DEC records accepted operator decisions, not a new rule |
| DEC-70 | **Reminders + the heartbeat — Nora's first UNPROMPTED-SPEECH path (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-08-reminders-heartbeat-design.md`, R-1b, R-36…R-39, operator 2026-08-08).** A `reminders` table of ONE-SHOT rows, a `HeartbeatLoop` on a fixed tick, and a `FirePath` that reuses the tag pipeline's stages without pretending to be a tag. Seven decisions worth the index. (1) **A reminder is a DEFERRED REQUEST, not a stored message**: the row holds the requester's own sentence and at fire time it is put to Nora as a request, so she answers it in her own voice with her own tools rather than replaying a script. (2) **The two senders are enumerated** — R-1's first clause is revised, not merely stretched, and R-1b pins the closed set structurally. (3) **The serialisation slot is SHARED**: R-10's in-flight lock and R-30's FIFO queue hoisted into `ConversationSlots`, because a fire turn and a tag must contend for the same conversation; the tick uses `try_acquire` and never queues — a reminder answered forty minutes late, into a conversation that has moved on, is worse than one answered a tick late. (4) **Policy is re-checked at FIRE time** (§5.1): `policy.toml` loads once at startup and a row may be thirty days old, so trust withdrawn in between must bite; a revoked row is FAILED and counted, never retried, and R-5 keeps it out of the room so the counter is its only trace. (5) **At-most-once, by decision** (R-37b). (6) **No recurrence, and `remind` refuses on a fire turn to keep it that way** (R-39) — the caps cannot substitute for it. *Superseded in part by DEC-85 (2026-08-10): recurrence exists, as a chain of one-shot rows advanced inside `claim`. The fire-turn refusal stays, for the reason R-39's revised text gives.* (7) **`nora_reminders_pending` was DECLARED AND DELETED, not shipped unpublished**: §13 asked for a backlog gauge, but every `ReminderStore` read is a coroutine while `Metrics.gauge_function` binds a synchronous callback that prometheus-client runs inside `collect()` — on the event loop serving `/metrics` — so a scrape-time count cannot be awaited; and publishing from the tick exports `0.0` forever on the shipped default (`[heartbeat] enabled = false`, while `remind` still writes rows), which reads as "no backlog" and means "nobody is measuring". The backlog is a database question and read-only `nora reminders list` answers it, flagging overdue rows; `nora_heartbeat_last_tick_age_seconds` (scrape-time, so it CLIMBS when the loop dies) is the liveness half. | `tests/architecture/test_only_two_paths_can_send.py` (R-1b), `tests/unit/test_heartbeat_loop.py` (R-36, the tick), `tests/contract/test_reminder_store_contract.py` (R-37/R-37b, both stores), `tests/unit/test_fire_path.py` (the turn), `tests/test_reminder_tools.py` (R-38/R-39 and the caps), `tests/test_cli_control_plane.py` (read-only listing, no `cancel` subcommand), `tests/test_doctrine_audiences.py` (the `heartbeat` audience), `tests/unit/test_conversation_slots.py` (the hoisted slot) |
| DEC-71 | MCP tool scope is enforced where `ToolRegistry.build()` is called (assembly), never at egress — `allowed_mcp` comes from `policy.toml`'s per-conversation `tools` field. | `tests/unit/test_engagement_pipeline.py::test_unauthorized_conversation_never_receives_pulse_tools` (+ `::test_authorized_conversation_receives_pulse_tools`), `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py::test_build_includes_only_the_allowed_mcp_servers_tools` (+ `::test_build_omits_mcp_tools_when_allowed_mcp_is_empty`) |
| DEC-72 | **`weather(place=None, forecast=False)` — one tool, one call, the HK Observatory keyless open data (operator 2026-08-08).** The weather answer Nora lacked (a 「今日天水圍咩天氣」 tag blew the token budget flailing on web scrapes, Langfuse). (1) **ONE verb, not three.** Weather is one capability with sub-shapes (current / today / multi-day / warnings); three tools (`weather_now`/`forecast`/`typhoon`) would fight over every weather tag the way DEC-58 measured, so it is one verb with a `forecast` flag. OPEN follow-ups: the corpus is all rank-2/identity-shape (overfitting — needs stat/shape diversity + a non-identity fabrication case + more grounded controls), the injection cases carry no `transcript` so the safety criterion grades them without source data, and the tier claim needs a controlled frozen-reply run. **AND — measured, the deeper one — the judge's own fabrication-detection was NOISY: a temp-0 re-run caught 1/3 of the same cases an earlier run caught 3/3, because the replies are stochastic AND the judge passed a fabricated ATTRIBUTION that carried a grounded VALUE («你排第一，52死» — 52 IS in the data, but nothing links «你» to that entry). Still n=4 controlled — the diversified corpus (C1) + a ≥10-reply controlled run is the confirmation. **C1 DONE, and it corrected the picture (as Kimi predicted): on a DIVERSIFIED corpus (varied rank + a NON-identity «invented time window» fabrication + 2 grounded controls) the judge catches only 3/5 fabrications, not 3/3 — the narrow all-rank-2 corpus OVERSTATED it. Controls pass 2/2 (low false-positive). So the gate MECHANISM is sound and controls are clean, but judge DETECTION on diverse fabrications (esp. invented-data, not just misattribution) is the real open frontier — the next iteration is a judge prompt that also catches fabricated STRUCTURE/values, measured on frozen diverse replies.
