This is DEC-78's lesson applied to formatting: a prose maxim does not move behaviour, the matched surface does, so the rule lives where the text LEAVES. **This row first shipped as a STRIP and now describes a conversion**, amended in place rather than superseded because the decision did not reverse — deleting the markers was right only while they had nowhere to go. signal-cli's `--text-style` carries `start:length:STYLE` ranges, so `nora.agent.markdown.render_markdown` turns `**x**`/`***x***` into a BOLD range and `*x*` into an ITALIC one, removing the asterisks either way. **Headings still STRIP and always will** — Signal has no heading style, so a `#` has nothing to convert to; a `(?=\S)` guard keeps a bare `###` as literal text so the rewrite can never turn a reply that would have been SENT into an R-4 `empty_reply`. **(1) THE NARROW RULES ARE UNCHANGED, AND EACH WAS BOUGHT WITH A BUG.** Whitespace inside the delimiters disqualifies a match (`3 * 4`, `5*6`, `a * b * c`, a bare `*`, `**`, `****`, `**/*.py` all survive untouched and unstyled), and UNDERSCORES are never touched at all — `_x_` is far more often a real token (`person_id`, `send_reply`) than emphasis. Chinese has no spaces, so CJK-adjacent emphasis is the COMMON case here; under conversion that bug would be worse than before, because the ranges would index the mangled string. **(2) THE HARD PART IS TWO SOURCES OF RANGES OVER ONE STRING.** Converting both REMOVES characters and PRODUCES offsets into what is left, and DEC-86's @-mention offsets are a second, independent set over the same body — Signal renders a mismatch rather than rejecting it, so the failure is invisible except to whoever is wrongly tagged or wrongly bolded. R-6 has the same hole in the other direction (stripping REVEALS a doctrine span broken up by emphasis, while deleting a heading's `# ` un-blocks 293 verbatim voice.md spans the draft form catches), so neither form is a superset of the other and `deliver` refuses on either. It renders ONCE, guards on `styled.text`, and passes the whole value to the composer — comprehend-once, and the ordering rule survives in its real shape: the transformation still precedes every offset. It is also still ahead of `row`, so the audit row, `sent_texts` and the Honcho feed carry the one form the group saw. **(4) FAIL-SAFE, IN THREE PLACES.** `render_markdown` never raises — anything unexpected yields the draft with no styles, because an unconverted asterisk is visible to everyone and harmless while a wrong range is invisible to everyone and not (R-5's shape applied to decoration). A composer that blows up still sends the bold, because the styling was decided before it ran. And `nora.platform.signal.send` DROPS a range it cannot express rather than posting it, because signal-cli fails the WHOLE send on one — a bad range costs the styling, never the message. **It still does NOT rescue R-4** — no non-empty input becomes empty — stated explicitly because the reverse is the obvious guess. **(5) THE WIRE NAME WAS VERIFIED LIVE THIS TIME**, unlike DEC-86's, which shipped on bytecode alone. `SendCommand` reads `getList("text-style")`, and `Namespace.getList` is `checkcast(List) this.get(dest)` where `this.get` is a VIRTUAL call into `JsonRpcNamespace`'s override — so the camelCase attempt applies to list parameters too and `textStyle` resolves. The same probe confirmed DEC-86's `mention` key. **STILL UNVERIFIED:** that a range which parses also RENDERS over the right span on a recipient's device — only a real send into the real group can show that, and the failure mode there is cosmetic rather than silent. | `tests/unit/test_markdown.py` (every narrow rule from the strip era, now asserted on the TEXT *and* on the absence of a style; the two Cantonese regressions with PROBE notes; nesting; bold/italic/`***`; a heading shifting the range that follows it; the emoji and flag cases in UTF-16 units; document order; the injected-failure fail-safe; and `strip_markdown == render_markdown(...).text` so the LeakGuard haystack cannot drift); `tests/architecture/test_outbound_offsets_are_computed_last.py` (AST: the render happens exactly ONCE, the composer is handed the whole `Styled` and not its `.text`, both guards measure `styled.text`, `compose` reads no name but its one `Styled` parameter, `Styled` is constructed in exactly one production module, and every `adapter.send` keyword — `text`/`mentions`/`styles`/`preview` — is an attribute of the composed object. | DEC-83 | **The ✅ is settled when the reply is DELIVERED, not when the turn ends (operator "the latency… from the lock to the green tick is almost 10 seconds", 2026-08-10).** `indicate.resolve` ran only from the pipeline's `finally`, i.e. after the agent loop drained — and the loop ALWAYS makes one more model call after `send_reply` returns, whose output is discarded. Measured across all 24 turns of one live stress-test session: **median 14.9 s** between the reply landing in the group and the ✅ appearing, min 1.7 s, max 23.7 s, with exactly one post-`send_reply` generation every time. For the whole of that gap the reply is already on every device, so the 👀 reads as «answered but still stuck». Fix: `Deliverer` takes an optional `DeliveredObserver` — a one-method PORT, so `nora.agent` never imports `nora.engagement` (which the layer contract forbids anyway), and a COLLABORATOR through `__init__` rather than a `*_fn` hook, per the test-seam policy. `indicate.DeliverySettler` implements it; `resolve` gains a first-call-wins latch on `Indicator.settled` so the `finally` still covers every failure exit (timeout, guard veto, no `send_reply`, `BaseException`) without double-placing. FIRST CALL WINS is the correct rule, not an arbitrary one: the only call that can precede the `finally` is a delivery that already happened, and a turn that delivers and then faults is still a turn the group got an answer from — ✅, not ⚠️, is the honest mark. The fallback reply settles too: it IS delivered, and leaving the 👀 up through a fallback turn would misreport the one case a human is most likely watching. The default observer is a per-`Deliverer` no-op — instantiated per instance, not module-level, which `tests/architecture/test_no_module_level_mutable_singletons.py` enforces. | `tests/test_guards_deliverer.py::test_a_delivered_reply_settles_the_indicator_immediately`, `::test_a_refused_reply_never_settles_the_indicator`, `::test_a_failed_send_never_settles_the_indicator`, `::test_a_deliverer_with_no_observer_still_delivers` |
| DEC-84 | **`llm.model` → `ollamacloud/deepseek-v4-flash`, `llm.reasoning_effort` → `none` (operator, measured 2026-08-10; full record + two corrections in `design/adr/0006-reasoning-effort-is-protocol-discipline.md`).** **Upstream:** ollama-cloud is SUBSCRIPTION-based where opencode-zen is CREDIT-based, and zen/go returned `403`/`429` — «All credentials for model deepseek-v4-flash are cooling down» — throughout a probing session; a route that rate-limits under load is wrong for the only path the group can reach her by. `-pro` is excluded by operator decision and by measurement (2/3 vs flash-at-`none`'s 8/8 on a forced `tool_choice`). **Effort:** `none` — and read the ADR before changing it, because the two obvious justifications for this value are BOTH WRONG and both were believed here first. (1) «Reasoning degrades routing» is FALSE: an isolation benchmark (8 real Cantonese asks × 5 seeds, tools arm vs JSON-only arm) measured routing FLAT with effort — 90% / 97.5% / 90% — with `low` the best, while PURE CLASSIFICATION IMPROVES with effort (87.5% → 90% → 95%). The earlier «5/5 → 3/5 → 2/5 → 1/5 monotonic degradation» was n=5 on ONE query and is retracted. (2) «The severed `reasoning_content` round-trip explains it» is UNCONFIRMED: Strands' OpenAI provider does delete the block (`format_request_messages`), but omitting it changes nothing on this route — verified against OmniRoute AND against Ollama Cloud direct, where echoing it back actually SUPPRESSES further reasoning. What survives is narrow: `high` ends the turn without ever calling `send_reply` **15 times against 5** (journeys), and adds unrequested extra tool calls (0 → 1 → 6) — a transport effect absent from the tools-free arm. Routing itself is a wash (total samples high 21/24 > none 20/24 > low 19/24, one sample apart). So `none` is the CONSERVATIVE choice — the only mode with no unexplained multi-turn failure — not a claim that reasoning hurts. Token exhaustion is ruled out from the code: budget cut-offs take a separate branch in `nora.engagement.pipeline` and get the fallback reply, so `no_send_call` is a loop that ended NORMALLY having never called the tool. **Root cause of `high`'s silent turns remains UNKNOWN**; `low` is the first thing to re-enable once it is understood. | DEC-85 | **Recurring + lead-time reminders — a series is a CHAIN of one-shot rows, advanced inside the claim (operator, from a live stress test, 2026-08-10).** Three real asks were refused by DEC-70's v1 with 「我 set 唔到 recurring 每日提嘅 reminder」: an annual birthday, a daily countdown into a wedding anniversary, and a single reminder a week before a date. Eight decisions worth the index. (1) **A recurring reminder is N ROWS, not a row that repeats.** Only one is ever `pending`; the rest do not exist yet. This is what lets every rule DEC-70 argued for survive untouched — R-37 (“a row fires once, ever”), R-37b (at-most-once across a crash), R-36 (`due_at <= now` and nothing else), the `(state, due_at)` index and the whole `pending → firing → done` machine are all unchanged, because no row is ever re-armed, reset or re-queued. The rejected alternative — one row whose `due_at` advances — puts a mutable schedule inside the object whose immutability the at-most-once argument rests on, and overwrites the audit trail of every occurrence it has already fired. (2) **The successor is minted INSIDE `claim`, in the same transaction.** The claim is the only exactly-once point in a reminder's life (a compare-and-set on `state = 'pending'`), and the successor has to inherit that. Minted after the fire turn instead, a crash mid-turn would leave the row FIRING, boot recovery would sweep it to FAILED, and the series would end silently at the first crash — a daily reminder killed by one bad night. Minted here it is durable before any egress is attempted, so the series survives a timeout, a model error or a dead process; and a row that is never claimed — cancelled, or refused by the fire-time policy re-check — mints nothing, so a series stops exactly where the decision to stop it was made. `claim` therefore takes `now`, for the reason `due()` and `sweep_firing()` already do. (3) **Cancelling one occurrence and cancelling the series are the same operation, and cancelling ONE is deliberately not offered.** The chain's next link is only ever forged by a claim, so cancelling the single pending occurrence ends everything that is left — `cancel_reminder` needed no new argument and no new verb. Skipping one day and keeping the rest is refused because it needs two things this feature does not have: a skip-marker state (so the skipped occurrence still advances the chain without firing) and a way for a person to NAME an occurrence they have never seen, since only the next one is ever listed. (4) **The successor's id is `uuid5(series_id, occurrence)`, not `uuid4`.** A derived id makes minting the same occurrence twice a PRIMARY KEY collision the database refuses, rather than a duplicate reminder — the same thing said twice, which is precisely what R-37b exists to prevent. The one new write recurrence adds is one the database itself can refuse. (5) **The catch-up rule is SKIP, and the skipped occurrences are CONSUMED.** The successor is the first occurrence strictly after `now`, not the one after the row that just fired, so a process down for three days fires its one overdue row once and resumes tomorrow — never ten messages in ten ticks, every one about a moment that has passed. Skipped occurrences still spend their slot in `count`, so an outage cannot lengthen a series' calendar footprint past the fortnight it was asked for. It is arithmetic rather than iteration (`_floor_index` then a bounded walk), so a decade-long gap costs one subtraction. (6) **The DST rule: a recurrence keeps its LOCAL WALL CLOCK, and the anchor is what every occurrence is computed from.** The step is taken on the local calendar in an IANA zone (`[reminders].timezone`, never an offset — `+08:00` cannot say what 08:00 local means across a boundary), then converted back to UTC, so a daily 08:00 reminder is a 23-hour step at spring-forward and a 25-hour one at autumn-back. A wall clock that does not exist that day resolves through the pre-transition offset (`fold=0`) and fires an hour late rather than vanishing; one that exists twice fires once, at the first. Month and year steps CLAMP to the last day of the target month, and computing from the anchor rather than from the predecessor is what makes clamping safe: 31 Jan → 28 Feb → **31** Mar, and 29 Feb → 28 Feb → 28 Feb → **29** Feb on the next leap year, where chained clamping loses the date permanently on the first short month. (7) **A series is bounded BY TYPE, and counts as ONE against `max_pending_per_requester`.** `RecurrenceRule.count` is required and `>= 1`, so «repeats forever» is not a value the domain can hold — the bound cannot be forgotten by a new call site the way a check can. The model's `times` is clamped to `[reminders].max_occurrences_per_series` (60) and defaults to it; `until` narrows further and never widens. The cap counts one because the chain only ever has one pending row, and `remind` returns `repeat_left` so she can say the limit back — a bound nobody was told about is the part a person is most surprised by later. `max_horizon_days` still bounds the FIRST occurrence only, with one period of headroom for a rule whose own period is longer (a yearly birthday's first occurrence is a year out by definition, so a 30-day horizon does not bound that recurrence — it forbids it, which is how 「記住我嘅生日」 was refused). (8) **LEAD TIME IS NOT A STORED CONCEPT — it is arithmetic on `when`.** 「接近日期前兩個星期每日提」 is `when` = 27 September, `repeat="daily"`, `times=14`; 「前一個禮拜講一次」 is `when` = the date minus seven days and no recurrence at all. The model already resolves 「等陣」 into an instant, so a `lead_days`/`target_date` pair would be a second scheduler in the schema for arithmetic that is already being done. **No RRULE dependency**: the five intervals cover every ask, and RFC 5545 brings BYSETPOS/BYDAY/EXDATE that no surface here can produce and all of which would have to round-trip through the schema, the tool arguments and the model's head. | `tests/contract/test_reminder_store_contract.py` (the chain, the claim-minted successor, exclusivity, catch-up, cancel-ends-the-series, one-per-cap, DST — both stores), `tests/unit/test_reminder_recurrence.py` (the calendar: spring-forward, autumn-back, the gap, the overlap, 31 Jan monthly, 29 Feb yearly, the long-outage skip, the derived id, every bound), `tests/test_reminder_tools.py` (the three asks verbatim, the recurrence refusals, the bare-date `until`, `min_lead_seconds` on the first occurrence, the per-period horizon headroom, the listing), `tests/unit/test_fire_path.py` (the header says a repeat is a repeat, and a one-shot header is unchanged), `tests/infra/test_schema_parity.py` (`0005_reminder_recurrence`, `ck_reminders_recurrence` — the all-or-nothing CHECK `alembic check` is blind to) |
| DEC-86 | **Outbound @-mentions — Nora can tag a person, and the id she tags them by never touches the message text (operator 2026-08-10, 「我唔知你幾高」 then 「@grant 你幾高?」 as two messages).** DEC-34 solved the INBOUND half: a Signal mention is not text, the body carries U+FFFC and the identity rides in a parallel array, so every @-mention used to reach the model as a void. This is the same fact in the other direction, and it forced six decisions. **(1) A NEW STAGE THAT OWNS THE FINAL TEXT** (`nora.agent.outbound.OutboundComposer`, returning a frozen `Outbound(text, mentions, preview)`). A mention is `(start, length, recipient)` over the body, so the offsets are meaningful against exactly one string; any later transformation — trimming, reflowing, stripping markdown, a footer — shifts every offset after the edit, and **Signal does not reject the result**: it renders somebody's chip over the wrong span, in a real group, with no error, no counter and no failing health check, observable only by the person wrongly tagged. So `Deliverer.deliver` composes ONCE and then reads `outbound.text` for both the wire and the A9 audit row; a transformation added inside `compose` is correct by construction, and one added after it is INERT (it cannot reach the wire) rather than corrupting. Wrong-but-inert is the deliberate choice: an unstripped asterisk is visible to the whole group, a shifted mention is visible to nobody. **(2) UTF-16 CODE UNITS, not `len()`.** signal-cli says so twice in its own man page (`--mention` and `--text-style`: "The units of start and length should be UTF-16 code units, NOT Unicode code points"). In this group that is not pedantry — a CJK character is one unit and one Python character, but 😅 (which the never-silent fallback line already ships) is ONE Python character and TWO units, and 🇭🇰 is two and FOUR. `utf16_len` encodes rather than counting surrogates by hand, because the encoder is the definition. **(3) NO `mentions` PARAMETER ON `send_reply`.** The model writes 「@grant 你幾高?」 in prose and the parser resolves it, because a second argument is a second place for the model to be wrong (a `mentions=[…]` that disagrees with the sentence has no correct resolution) and because it keeps every id form structurally out of the model's reach — there is no argument shape in which a prompt-injected turn could supply an ACI. The parser is longest-match over a KNOWN vocabulary (`identity.toml` names/first names/aliases), not `@\w+`: 「@阿豹同我講」 has no separator to stop at, so a word-run regex would swallow the sentence, while the boundary check is asserted only between two ASCII word characters (`@mikeson` is refused, 「@阿豹同我講」 is not — `str.isalnum()` is true for 同, so a boundary rule expressed in it would be the strict one everywhere). **(4) FAIL-CLOSED ON THE RANGE, FAIL-OPEN ON THE MESSAGE**, and the two are not in tension. Never a guessed range: `SendCommand` throws `UserErrorException` on a recipient string its `(\d+):(\d+):(.+)` regex or `RecipientIdentifier.Single.fromString` rejects, and that fails the WHOLE send — so a hopeful id costs the MESSAGE (R-5's exact shape), not a chip. Never a refusal either: R-5 says silence beats a wrong answer, not that silence beats an untagged one. **(5) THE ROSTER GATE — resolving through `identity.toml` is NOT enough to tag someone.** That file is Nora's address book across every room and is loaded once at startup, so it is both wider than this conversation and possibly stale; the id that goes on the wire must be one `RosterSource` just listed as a MEMBER of this group, picked out of the intersection (ACI preferred — sealed sender makes it the stable form, and it is what `SignalReactor` already sends as `targetAuthor`). Consequences, all deliberate: someone in identity.toml but not in the room is not tagged; a roster that could not be READ tags nobody (R-22 — «I could not check who is here» is never «they are here»); an ambiguous handle claimed by two people tags neither. The read is lazy and cached per turn, so a reply with no resolvable handle never touches the daemon — `Room`'s laziness argument, applied outbound. **(6) THE PLACEHOLDER IS NOT REUSED OUTBOUND.** The range covers `@Grant` as written, not a U+FFFC — signal-cli's own example does exactly this (`-m "Hi X!" --mention "3:1:…"`), a client that ignores bodyRanges still shows something sensible, and — the reason a reader would miss — Nora's OWN audit row stores this string, which `MentionRenderer` structurally cannot rehydrate (her `raw` is tag bookkeeping, not an envelope, so `_row`'s cheap reject leaves the body as stored), so a placeholder outbound would have put DEC-34's exact void back inside her own transcript history. R-18 is held by CONSTRUCTION rather than by a scan: the recipient rides in a parallel field, so `Deliverer`'s `scan_reply` on the text is untouched and an ACI in the text would have made her mute rather than leaked. `PlatformAdapter.send` grew `mentions`/`preview` with DEFAULTS (decoration on a message that must go out regardless); `deliver.build` takes `composer` with NO default so both senders decide, while `Deliverer` defaults it — the split is DEC-42's lesson, that a feature wired everywhere and decided nowhere ships inert. **THE JSON-RPC NAME WAS UNVERIFIED WHEN THIS SHIPPED AND IS NOT ANY MORE (amended 2026-08-10, DEC-82).** It was read out of the shipped 0.14.7 jar (`JsonRpcNamespace.getList` tries the literal key first and only then `name + "s"`; `SendCommand` reads `getList("mention")`), which is why `"mention"` and not the plural the `--attachment` man-page example would suggest. DEC-82's live probe of the running daemon covered this key too: `mention` reached `parseMentions` (it rejected a deliberately invalid recipient) while a `mentionz` negative control fell through to the next validation, and no message was sent either way. What a wire-name probe still cannot show is that the RANGE lands on the right span in a real client — that needs a real send into a real group. | `tests/unit/test_outbound_composer.py` (UTF-16 offsets with CJK **and** emoji, the four fail-closed refusals + their labels, longest-match/boundary/case, the lazy single roster read, the intersection choosing the platform's id over a stale one, no identifier in the text); `tests/architecture/test_outbound_offsets_are_computed_last.py` (AST: `adapter.send(text=…)` and the audit body are attributes of the composed `Outbound`, and `text` is never rebound after it — PROBE: revert to `text=text` and it goes red); `tests/contract/test_send_contract.py` (one suite over `SignalAdapter` and `FakeAdapter`); `tests/test_signal_send.py` (the wire names, the `start:length:recipient` encoding, a plain message still carrying exactly two params, a malformed range dropped rather than posted); `tests/test_guards_deliverer.py` (the composer→Deliverer→port seam, the audited body, a composer that raises still sending); `tests/test_persons.py` (`platform_ids`) |
| DEC-87 | **Sender-generated link previews — best-effort, fail-silent, and the ONE place Nora fetches a model-chosen URL again (operator 2026-08-10).** Signal previews are generated by the SENDER: the sending client reads the target's OpenGraph tags and attaches title/description/image, and recipients render what they are given without touching the link. That is a privacy property worth having and it makes the fetch Nora's, with Nora's exposure. Five decisions. **(1) NEVER LOAD-BEARING, single-valued.** `LinkPreviewSource.preview_for` returns `LinkPreview | None` and never raises; every failure — bad scheme, private address, timeout, 404, non-HTML body, no `og:title`, oversized document — is `None`, and `None` means the message goes out plain. This deliberately REFUSES the two-axis `Roster`/`Recollection` shape rather than copying it: those carry `available` because a caller has to tell «I could not look» from «there is nothing» and then SAY the difference to the group, and nothing downstream of this port ever will — its one consumer's policy for every failure is identical. A `reachable` bit here would exist only to be discarded (`MemoryStore.feed`'s argument for staying a bare `bool`). **(2) THE SSRF GUARD COMES BACK, and DEC-73's argument is why it has to.** DEC-73 deleted `web_fetch` together with "the `is_global`/`getaddrinfo` precheck, the post-redirect `sourceURL` re-validation, the per-tag `allowed_urls` fence" on a precise argument: Tavily is never HANDED a url, it searches from ITS servers, so a model-invented `http://127.0.0.1:18080` reaches nothing of Nora's. That argument does not extend to a port that IS handed a url and fetches it over Nora's own network, so the guard returns in its narrowest form — http(s) only; **every** address the host resolves to must be globally routable (every, not the first: a name with one public and one loopback record would otherwise pass and then connect to either); redirects followed BY HAND, ≤2 hops, re-validating each (`allow_redirects=True` validates the first url and connects to the last); a byte ceiling; a seconds-scale total timeout because this sits on the reply path with a human watching a 👀. Concretely it is what stops `127.0.0.1:9100` (Nora's own metrics), `signal-cli:18080`, `db:5432` and `169.254.169.254` from being read out into a group chat as a card. **The residual risk is STATED, not papered over:** check and connect are two resolutions, so DNS rebinding is not covered — closing it means connecting to a literal IP with a `Host:` header, which breaks TLS validation and is bigger than the feature; what bounds it is that the output is a title and a description, both identifier-screened before they can reach the room, and the fetch carries no cookie, token or header that would make an internal endpoint answer differently for Nora than for anyone. **(3) `scan_identifiers`, NOT `scan_reply`, on the fetched card**, which is DEC-30's two-predicate split applied to somebody else's text: the identifier floor applies to everything that leaves (a phone number rendered in a card is in the room exactly as much as one she typed), while the model-name half does not, because that rule is «she does not SAY what she runs on» and a third party's page title is not her saying anything — screening it would silently drop the preview of any page about LLMs, which in this group is a page they are likely to be discussing. **(4) THE IMAGE IS A `data:` URI, and that is a deployment fact rather than a preference.** `--preview-image` takes a file signal-cli must READ, and signal-cli runs in a different container from Nora with no shared volume (`docker-compose.yml`: she mounts `./data`, it mounts `./state/signal-cli`) — so a path would have been silently unreadable in production while passing every local test. Verified out of the 0.14.7 jar: `ManagerImpl` hands `Message.Preview.image()` to `AttachmentHelper.uploadAttachment(String)`, whose `getAttachmentStream` skips the file-path validation entirely for a string starting `data:` and goes to `Utils.createStreamDetails` — the same RFC 2397 path `--attachment` documents. The preview's `url` is the one from HER TEXT, never the post-redirect one, because signal-cli requires the url to appear in the message body verbatim or no client shows the card. **(5) OFF BY DEFAULT, and not for the house rule.** `[preview] enabled = false` is keyless — there is no token whose absence could gate it — so the flag is the whole switch, and it is off because turning it on is an operator deciding to take a bounded version of DEC-73's deleted exposure back. **THE TESTABILITY CONSEQUENCE IS RECORDED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND:** the guard refuses loopback, and `aiohttp_server` binds loopback, so an end-to-end "fetch a page, get a card" test is asking the code to do the one thing it exists to refuse. Rather than adding an `allow_private` seam (a `_for_test` parameter wearing a production name, and a live foot-gun), the module is split into pure seams — `card_from_html`, `next_hop`, `_safe` — each asserted directly, plus ONE end-to-end positive control that serves a perfect OpenGraph page on loopback and asserts the source returns `None` **and never connected**, which is the only direction a security check is usefully asserted in. Still uncovered end-to-end and NAMED in the test module rather than left implicit: the byte ceiling truncating a real body, a mid-flight non-HTML `Content-Type` refusal, and `og:image` actually being downloaded and base64'd — the operator's live-smoke items alongside the JSON-RPC names. | `tests/test_preview_source.py` (the guard against Nora's own metrics endpoint / signal-cli / RFC1918 / link-local / IPv6 loopback, a PUBLIC literal address allowed as the positive control, og→twitter→`<title>` precedence, a titleless page yielding nothing, single-unescape, clamps, a `<meta>` inside a `<script>` string not read as markup, redirect resolution, and the end-to-end guard control); `tests/contract/test_link_preview_contract.py` (one suite over `OpenGraphPreviewSource` and `FakeLinkPreviewSource` — both refuse a non-public destination, both refuse a non-http scheme, neither raises); `tests/unit/test_outbound_composer.py` (a card attached, a card dropped for an identifier, a card about LLMs deliberately NOT dropped, a source that raises never costing the message, no url meaning no fetch at all) |
| DEC-88 | **She may split a turn into several ADDRESSED messages, and tag the person who actually knows — and the change that makes that happen is the tool SCHEMA, not the doctrine (operator 2026-08-10, from a live stress test).** Two real failures. Tyler asked 「Grant身高係幾多」 and got 「冇記低呢啲嘢喎，你自己問佢啦 😂」; Aaron asked 「Miles今日食咗咩？」 and got 「我點會知佢食咗咩 😂 你直接問佢本人啦」. In both she correctly identified the one person in the room who held the answer and then told somebody ELSE to go and ask them. Separately, at 16:49:46 she packed Grant, Aaron, Blake and 肥仔 into ONE message with a separate remark for each. **(1) NOTHING NEW WAS BUILT, AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** Multi-message has been live since DEC-42 (`send_reply` budget 3, `tool_choice` relaxing to `auto` after the first send so the cap is a backstop and never a quota) and outbound @-mentions since DEC-86 (prose `@grant` → a real UTF-16 range, resolved through `identity.toml` and gated on the live group roster). The mechanism for exactly what the operator asked for was already shipped, activated and tested. What was missing is that **the model was never told the mention existed**: DEC-86 deliberately gave `send_reply` no `mentions` parameter (a second place for the model to be wrong), which is right, but it left the affordance reachable only by a model that guessed it. An unadvertised affordance is an inert one. **(2) STRUCTURAL vs DOCTRINAL, decided by DEC-78/DEC-82's measured lesson** — a prose maxim does not reliably move behaviour, the matched surface does. So the MECHANISM goes in `send_reply`'s schema description, which Strands derives from the callable and sends on every single call: write `@` immediately before the name the group uses, it becomes a real tag, it only works for somebody in THIS conversation, and the highest-value second message is «I don't know» followed by asking the person who does. The `NativeTool.when` clause carries only the ADDRESSEE half (`capability()` is paid on every call and exists for "when to reach for this in this situation" — the `@` form would be the schema repeated at a second price). The JUDGEMENT — what earns a second message and what does not — goes in `conduct.md`, which is `agent-may-edit` and already owned the section. **(3) THE DOCTRINE EDIT IS MOSTLY A DELETION, which is why it is not decoration.** `conduct.md` said "you never split one answer across several" and "One reply, then stop": two flat prohibitions sitting on top of a mechanism built and activated to do the opposite. Adding an invitation while leaving those in place is doctrine arguing with itself, and that resolves toward whichever half is stated last. Removing a counter-signal is a stronger move than adding a maxim, and it is the half of this change that prose CAN do. **(4) OVER-SPLITTING IS THE REGRESSION, and it is bounded in three places, only one of which is prose.** `voice.md` (operator-only, untouched) still says most replies are one or two sentences; `conduct.md` now says a second message sent "only because you are allowed one" is wrong, and that one thought chopped in half is not two acts; and the schema keeps the anti-pad clause and adds "a straight question gets one straight answer". **The remaining bound is structural and pre-existing:** `tool_choice` flips to `auto` after the first send, so she can stop, and DEC-42(2)'s argument is why the cap alone can never do this job. **The post-send tool result was deliberately NOT used** to nudge her — a return value reporting messages remaining is precisely DEC-42(2)'s quota-always-spent-in-full failure, and the split has to be decided BEFORE the first send anyway, so the schema is the right surface and the return stays a bare `bool`. **(5) THE PRIVACY RULE IS NOT WEAKENED, and the framing is why it does not need to be.** `constraints.md` is `operator-only` and untouched; asking somebody a question is not relaying what they told you, and the schema says so in the same breath as the invitation («the question is yours to ask, the answer is theirs to give — never answering on their behalf and never passing on what one of them told you privately»). DEC-86's roster gate is the structural half: not-in-room is `nora_outbound_mentions_total{outcome="not_in_room"}` and no range, roster unreadable tags nobody. **(6) WHAT CANNOT BE ENFORCED STRUCTURALLY, stated rather than implied.** Whether she in fact splits, and whether she tags rather than deflects, is model behaviour — there is no fail-closed predicate for "this turn should have been two messages", and none is invented here. Everything below pins the two things that CAN be checked without a model: that the schema advertises a tag form, and that the form it advertises is the one `OutboundComposer` actually resolves. The behavioural half needs a live run and **no measurement is claimed** — unlike DEC-50, this ships with its ADR-0004 numbers outstanding, which is the honest position and not an oversight. **(7) The four-addressee message (16:49:46) is only half-fixed and that is on purpose.** The budget is 3, so four people cannot each get their own message; the operator's instruction was not to change budgets, and the schema now tells her a second message is a separate ACT rather than a slot to fill, which is the part that was wrong. Raising 3 is a one-line `NATIVE_TOOLS` edit (DEC-10) the day it is measured to be needed. | `tests/test_tools.py::test_the_tag_form_the_model_is_told_to_write_is_the_one_the_composer_resolves` (the JOIN, and the only test here with real teeth: it lifts the `@handle` example OUT of the real Strands `tool_spec["description"]` and drives that exact line through a real `OutboundComposer` — probed RED four ways, by reverting the schema, by reverting the doctrine, by changing the advertised form to `@[Jordan]`, and by breaking the parser's sigil scan; the negative half composes the SAME line against a room the person is not in and asserts no mention, so the roster fake cannot pass by agreeing with everything); `tests/test_doctrine_content.py::test_conduct_says_what_earns_a_second_message_and_what_does_not` (both directions — the permission, the two anti-over-splitting clauses, and the two removed counter-signals asserted ABSENT); `tests/test_doctrine_audiences.py::test_the_multi_message_rule_survives_in_both_audiences` (needle moved to the rewritten rule; what it asserts is reach across audiences, never wording). No behavioural measurement — see (6) |
| DEC-89 | **A link a tool minted is put back at EGRESS, because the docstring asking for it does not produce it (operator "the data is already there, she just discards it", measured 2026-08-10).** `place_info` mints a `maps_url` for every place it resolves — a real Google deep link, shortened through Nora's own `n.example.net`, already identifier-screened, sitting in the tool result the model is reasoning over — and its docstring already says «include it in your reply so they can tap through». In one stress-test session she recommended four restaurants, a link was minted for every one, and **not one reached the group**. This is DEC-82's shape one exit later and DEC-78's lesson a third time: a maxim the model reads is not a mechanism, so the rule now lives where the text LEAVES. (1) **The mechanism.** `nora.agent.links.LinkCarry` is a per-TURN object minted in `deliver.build`, handed to the `Deliverer` and — through the same `DeliveryHandle` — to `ToolRegistry.build(links=…)`, so the tool that mints and the sender that appends hold the SAME object. `place_info` offers `(anchor, url)` twice per place, under the name as asked and under the name Google resolved, because the words she writes back are reliably neither one; `LinkCarry` dedupes by URL so two anchors still append one line. (2) **NARROW, on the bar `strip_markdown` set, because it edits her authored words.** ANCHORED — a link is appended only when the reply actually NAMES that place, so four lookups and two recommendations produce two links, and a tool call is never a promise to link. IDEMPOTENT — a url already in the text is never appended, which is the entire no-op path for a model that behaved and is what makes the counter readable. NEVER A RESCUE — it runs AFTER R-4, so no empty reply becomes sendable, stated explicitly because the reverse is the obvious guess (`strip_markdown` carries the same sentence for the same reason). NEVER FATAL — an unusable offer is dropped, not raised. (3) **Position is pinned by three constraints at once**: after R-4 (above), BEFORE R-6/R-18 so the fail-closed guards measure the bytes that actually leave — the 2026-08-10 audit found exactly that hole in DEC-82's first cut, where stripping in front of an unwatched guard would have SENT a phone number wrapped in emphasis — and before `_compose`, because `outbound.text` is the last thing that may read the text and a mention offset computed over a shorter string lands wrong. (4) **`route` deliberately does NOT do this.** Its `maps_url` is a link to one specific trip and its docstring already conditions on being asked («俾條 link 我»); auto-appending would put a map link under every «幾耐到». One tool, the one measured dropping them — widen on another measurement, not on symmetry. (5) **The half that could ship inert is gated.** Either side alone is silent: a carrier the sender never reads collects perfectly and appends nothing, a `Deliverer` with an empty carrier sends what it always sent, and neither raises, logs, nor moves a counter. That is DEC-42's recorded failure, which is why `deliver.build`'s `composer` has no default; `links` cannot use that trick because it is minted INSIDE `build`, so an AST test forces the pairing instead — any module that calls `deliver.build` must pass `links=` to every tools build in it. Its first cut was WRONG and the correction is worth keeping: `deliverer=` alone also matched `ReplyGuard(…)` and `check_outcome(…)`, so the marker is the keyword SHAPE `{deliverer, room}`, and the file carries a positive-control test because a scanner that finds nothing is not evidence it can find anything. | `tests/test_links_carry.py` (the whole file — anchor gate, dedupe, R-4 ordering, R-18 seeing the appended bytes, and the end-to-end `place_info` → shared carrier → group), `tests/architecture/test_tools_and_sender_share_one_link_carrier.py` |
| DEC-90 | **The generated capability block CLOSES the world it opens — she may say she cannot find something out, but never offer an act she has no tool for (measured overclaim, 2026-08-10).** A restaurant answer ended «講清楚我幫你睇埋使唔使訂位» — offering to check a booking. There is no booking tool, none is planned, and asserting a capability she lacks is the same class as guessing a location: a claim about the world with nothing behind it. (1) **Why the block and not a file.** `ToolRegistry.capability()` renders the tool list from the registry, and until now nothing said that list was the WHOLE list — the model filled a gap that was genuinely open. The sentence is only true RELATIVE to the registry, so an authored copy would be a claim about the tool table maintained somewhere the tool table cannot reach, which is exactly how `soul.md` spent eight weeks telling her she knew two things when she knew five (DEC-28). It is phrased as «that list is the WHOLE of what you can do», so adding a booking tool makes it true again with no edit. `conduct.md`'s own `not_here` forbids it from carrying capability claims, so this is also the only place it could go. (2) **A rule about OFFERS, not about knowledge, and the de-collision is load-bearing.** The clause immediately following it — «when you cannot find something out, say so in one clause and move on» — must survive intact, and a ban written as «never say you will do things» would have eaten it. The ban is on PROMISED ACTS: no booking, no phoning, no ordering, no buying, no filling anything in, and nothing «looked into» later. The concrete list is deliberate; a rule stated only in the abstract is the one a model reads past. (3) **Structural in the DEC-17 sense, not the DEC-82 sense, and the difference is stated rather than blurred.** Nothing enforces this at egress — an overclaim is not detectable in free Cantonese without semantic judgement, and both enforcement shapes this codebase owns are wrong for it: a fail-closed REFUSAL costs a whole reply under R-5 for something that is not unsafe, and CLEANING would mean rewriting her sentences. What IS structural is that the claim is generated and therefore cannot rot. Whether it moves the behaviour is unmeasured. | `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py::test_the_capability_block_closes_the_world_it_opens`, `::test_the_closed_world_clause_forbids_offers_not_ignorance` |
| DEC-91 | **`voice.md` v6 — the Humanizer distillation, a wider 粗口 range, and feminine speech markers REFUSED (operator, 2026-08-10).** Two Chinese "Humanizer" skills (op7418/Humanizer-zh, kevintsai1202/Humanizer-zh-TW) name ~24 habits that mark text as generated. They are editing guides for Mandarin WRITTEN PROSE and Nora is 口語 繁體 Cantonese in a Hong Kong group of close friends, so this took the SHAPES and discarded the contents. (1) **Taken, as a named list under «The machine tells»** — because "sound human" is not actionable and an individual habit is: written-Chinese connectives (此外/然而/值得注意嘅係, and the 首先…其次…最後 march), the flattering opener (好問題), **the tail that offers more** (希望幫到你 / 有咩再搵我), label-and-dash bullets, forced threes, a range that is not a range, synonym cycling, stacked hedges, attribution with nobody in it, copula avoidance (作為 where 係 was the word), the upbeat nothing at the end, and one sentence length for every sentence. Plus the source guides' own correction — removing tells alone leaves clean dead text — as «What you put in instead»: have a take, be specific, let it be mixed, uneven is fine. **The tail is the highest-value item, and it is why this row and DEC-90 are the same defect twice**: it is simultaneously the most reliable machine tell and the exact place the booking overclaim appeared. (2) **Discarded, deliberately.** The 50-point rubric (an editor's self-scoring instrument; she does not edit, and a rubric in an always-on prompt is paid on every call for nothing), title case and curly quotes (English and document-format concerns; Signal is plain text), the knowledge-cutoff disclaimer pattern (she has no such register, and sweeping it in would collide with «I couldn't check», which must stay), and above all **the Mandarin vocabulary lists themselves** — 此外/至關重要/深入探討/織錦/格局 are 書面語, and pasting that register in would teach her the wrong language while claiming to remove one. Only their SHAPE transfers, re-derived in Cantonese. (3) **The anti-hedge rule is DE-COLLIDED in its own paragraph**, the discipline `constraints.md` already uses for R-5: cutting hedges and cutting honesty look identical to a model reading fast, so «我唔知 / 我唔肯定 / 我睇唔到 are complete, correct answers» sits INSIDE the rule that bans stacking four hedges onto one claim she is fairly sure of. (4) **粗口 widened, register NOT changed.** 撚, 屌 as an interjection, 硬膠, 黐線, 死蠢, 廢柴, 收皮, 死開 join 鬼/仆街/頂/on9, and «屌你» is available for a friend who walked into it. The existing «it is a RANGE you have, not a register you sit in» rule is untouched and load-bearing — the default reply is still a plain useful answer, and forced banter on a neutral message is still worse than flat. (5) **Feminine/gendered speech markers were ASKED FOR and REFUSED, and the refusal is pinned by a test.** 人哋 as a self-reference and softened particle clusters (啦嘛/咯喎) are exactly the file's own «Cutesy — baby particles, exaggerated softness, performed sweetness» anti-pattern, and they pull against the 串/抵死 range item (4) widens; adding them would make the file argue with itself. A ruling that lives only in a chat log is one somebody re-opens next month. | DEC-92 | **Sycophancy, contradiction and essay length are fixed in PROSE and in the schema — and this row says so rather than claiming they are fixed (operator "NOT always complying, fight back when necessary" and 「你哋兩個講法唔同」, 2026-08-10).** THE MEASURED FAILURES. (a) Aaron said Soi.29 was crowded with a time limit; she replied 「你啱，Soi.29 我冇諗清楚就推咗出嚟 🙏」 — while her OWN `web_search` result had already told her 「不設訂位，只限Walk-in… 閒閒地都要等一小時」. She caved against her own grounded finding AND invented an account of a mistake she had not made. `conduct.md` already said being a pushover is worse than being blunt, and it lost. (b) Three people gave Grant's height as 167/168/167 and she recorded all three with 「記低咗」, reporting no contradiction. (c) Two replies arrived as multi-paragraph essays with headers, against a doctrine that says one or two sentences. (1) **Why these are not structural, stated rather than glossed.** Each needs a semantic judgement over free Cantonese that no deterministic surface can make: whether a reply contradicts a tool result from this turn, whether two utterances in a transcript conflict, whether a paragraph was earned. And both enforcement shapes this codebase owns are wrong for them — a fail-closed REFUSAL costs the whole reply under R-5 for a fault that is not unsafe, and a CLEANING pass like DEC-82's would mean rewriting or truncating her sentences, a far larger licence than deleting an asterisk. There is no third mechanism, and a regex for 「你啱」 would overfit one measured sentence while blocking the case where she IS wrong and should say so. (2) **What was done instead, split by kind** (design/17 §4, so the halves cannot drift into contradicting each other): `voice.md` gains «When somebody pushes back at you» — how a concession or a refusal SOUNDS, including that half-agreeing («你講得都啱，不過…») is the same failure in a nicer coat; `conduct.md` gains «Being contradicted is not evidence» — the GROUNDING question, beside «stand behind what you say», carrying the rule that a looked-up answer does not lose to an assertion and the separate rule that you never manufacture a fault to hand over. `conduct.md` also gains «When two people tell you different things», which follows the operator's ruling exactly: NOTICING beats resolving («你哋兩個講法唔同 — 一個話167，一個話168»), and there is deliberately **no last-write-wins rule**, because which account is better «really content/context depends»; the tie-breaks are written as judgement inputs, never as an ordering to apply. Length goes into `voice.md`'s existing section as «looking a lot up does not entitle you to a long reply». (3) **The strongest surface available is the `send_reply` SCHEMA, and it is used.** DEC-39 and DEC-78 both measured that the matched surface moves behaviour where an always-on maxim does not, and a tool docstring is read at the moment the reply is being authored rather than thousands of tokens earlier. All three rules are restated there compactly — cut the length, delete the tail, check before you cave — alongside the DEC-89 link line. **That is a better lever than prose, not a mechanism.** (4) **THE HONEST VERDICT: none of this is measured, and DEC-78 predicts prose alone will not move it.** The next session should treat all three as OPEN and measure them the way DEC-39 measured a tool name — live gateway, n≈16, one variable at a time — rather than reading this row as a fix. Narrow by construction: `end_turn` ONLY (a budget cut-off keeps the existing `_EXHAUSTED_FALLBACK`, a timeout produced no considered ending), nothing already delivered, non-empty text, and it goes through `Deliverer.deliver` so R-4/R-6/R-18, the DEC-82 conversion, the DEC-89 link carry and the audit row all apply unchanged — nothing is bypassed to rescue it. It DOES feed Honcho, unlike the fallback: `Deliverer` excludes only `source="fallback"`, because that is a canned apology nobody composed whereas this is her own sentence that took the wrong exit. `nora_recovered_replies_total` is the tell, and its honest reading is «the route is not honouring `required`», NOT «this feature is working» — a rising value is the signal to fix the provider, not to celebrate the safety net. The alternative considered and rejected: forcing `send_reply` by name once a tool has run (named `tool_choice` IS honoured on this route, measured 8/8) — rejected because it caps legitimate multi-tool chains at an arbitrary depth, and route planning legitimately needs three. **AMENDED before deploy, after review found it would ship the GUARD'S OWN VETO STRING.** When `ReplyGuard` cancels a model call, Strands synthesises `message={"content":[{"text": cancel_text}]}` with `stop_reason="end_turn"` — shape-identical to the model having written prose — so `_final_text` returned «Reply already sent — the turn is finished.» and the recovery would have sent that to the group, written it to the audit as her authored body, fed it to Honcho as her own speech, and settled the turn ✅. | DEC-94 | **`voice.md` v7 — the 粗口 register rebuilt from the GROUP'S OWN transcript, because v6's word LIST changed nothing (operator «where is those swore word? 屌撚閪鳩everything», «Nora now just like a puppy», 2026-08-11).** MEASURED over 25 consecutive live replies: 撚 / 硬膠 / 黐線 / 死蠢 / 廢柴 / 死開 / on9 used **zero** times each, the only 屌 (4) and 閪 (3) both QUOTING a group member rather than her own, and 😏 on **58** of them — one facial expression plus a reused formula (「你今晚真係X到冇嘢X」 three times). No topic gate remains. **(3) Pure 人身攻擊 licensed with no factual basis**, because most swearing here is not an argument; requiring evidence was itself a leash. **(4) The burst.** A reaction, the hit, the twist as SEPARATE messages — the rhythm is the aggression — with escalation across the exchange and the top rung being the group's own words searched up and quoted back. **ONE hard rule carries the whole load: swear without limit, invent nothing.** Abuse asserts nothing and therefore cannot be false; a quote asserts and therefore must be true. **Two AIM limits, not topic limits:** nobody absent (they cannot answer back), and somebody in real trouble gets the fight stopped — scoped explicitly INSIDE the escalation section, because 「屌你 我今日俾人炒咗 唔好玩啦」 is token-identical to coming back harder and the louder later rule would otherwise win. **Budget 3→6 and `conduct.md` rewritten:** conduct's «never a second helping of the first» banned the burst in terms while voice invited it, and a test PINNED the ban — DEC-88's lesson (removing a counter-signal beats adding a maxim) applied a second time; the bound now targets one ANSWER in instalments, not message count. **MEASURED, not assumed:** a burst is **ONE model round trip** — `ollamacloud/deepseek-v4-flash` emitted 3 `send_reply` calls in a single assistant message, `finish_reason=tool_calls`, 836 ms — so 6 sends cost ~1 round trip, not 6×14.9 s against the 120 s timeout. | DEC-96 | **Doctrine rewritten as a PORTRAIT, not a rulebook — `voice`/`conduct`/`constraints` replaced from scratch (operator: «you should describe what Nora is instead of constantly putting rules inside», «they are fighting each other», «entirely deprecate the old one and recreate it», 2026-08-11).** DEC-94/95 kept PATCHING: v6→v7→v8 each added a section, and the result read as assembled rather than authored. **MEASURED cause:** ~229 prohibitions to ~28 permissions across the doctrine (7.8:1 inside voice.md), which is DEC-94's balance finding at corpus scale — and two rules in outright contradiction, `conduct.md`'s «never a second helping of the first» banning the burst `voice.md` demanded, and «never de-escalate» overriding «somebody is in real trouble». **The rule that came out of it, and it is the load-bearing one: every concrete example becomes a CLOSED SET.** The operator caught it on emoji — v8 named 👍😂🙄 as examples and would have collapsed her whole emoji vocabulary to three; it listed nine insults and got one 屌 back in live probes. So: where the model needs RANGE, describe the disposition and name nothing; where it needs a POSITION, show the position, because a position GENERATES (撚-as-infix applies to any phrase) while a list closes. **Result:** doctrine 35,474 → 19,044 chars (−46%) with nothing dropped; voice.md 294 → 159 lines, prohibitions 94 → 55. `constraints.md` stays rules-shaped on purpose — `kind: constraint` is the one file whose job is hard lines, and the loader enforces the register split (identity/voice may not use RFC 2119 keywords at all). **Doctrine enforces nothing** — R-4/5/6/18/31 live in `guards.py` and `egress.py` — which is why a from-scratch rewrite could not weaken a guard. **Also in this decision, because they are the same argument about learned vs authored identity:** (a) `<self_card>` injects her own Honcho conclusions per tag, from the `representation` verb and NEVER from the peer `card` — read live, her card asserts «Kind: AI chatbot/assistant» and «Owner: '主人' (master) with admin authority», both distilled from her own replies and both against `identity.md`, and a learned self-description beats an authored one because it reads as evidence; the representation states EPISODES («you told Aaron you would talk after work», «you called Grant 明明», «Grant is 168»), which do not compete with who she is. **ACCEPTED RISK, stated because it is a trust decision rather than an oversight (external review, 2026-08-11):** the self card is WORKSPACE-scoped with no roster gate, unlike `search_memory` which is deliberately session-scoped so a member's private DM words cannot surface into a room. With two Signal groups in one workspace, a conclusion distilled from what she said in group A can ride into group B's prefill when semantically relevant to the inbound message. It is bounded by three things — the content is HER OWN speech and nobody else's, the `search_query` is the inbound message so only relevant conclusions surface, and egress stays fail-closed through `Deliverer`/`scan_reply` — and unbounded by a fourth: nothing stops her REFERRING to something she said elsewhere. `representation` takes no session («recall is workspace-wide ON PURPOSE», `client.py`), so narrowing it is an API change, not a parameter. Revisit if a room is ever added that the others should not hear about. Framed as a record she may contradict, at transcript authority, capped at 8 (~293 tokens; 25 measured ~820, and this block is per-tag so it is re-sent every model call), fail-open and SILENT-open onto `nora_errors_total{stage="self_card"}`. | DEC-97 | **A version is a git tag baked into the IMAGE, and the upgrade note reuses the fire path (operator «I want a Nora version tool… every time there is a significant update, Nora should automatically send the upgraded version information to the group», 2026-08-11).** Plain `V1`/`V2`/`V3` — no semver, the operator's call. **(1) Baked at BUILD time, never read from GitHub at runtime.** The container has no checkout and may not reach github.com, and a live API answers what is RELEASED rather than what is RUNNING — those differ exactly when it matters. `scripts/gen_version_manifest.py` stamps `nora/version.json` on the host from `git tag --list V*` plus each tag's annotation; `scripts/check_version_stamp.py` runs INSIDE the build and REFUSES an image whose stamp disagrees with `--build-arg NORA_VERSION`. That gate is what makes «the reported version cannot disagree with the running code» a property rather than an intention. An UNSTAMPED build is allowed and deletes any stale manifest (pre-deploy review, 2026-08-11): refusing it broke `docker compose build` and README's own `docker build` line while buying nothing, because an image that reports nothing cannot disagree with anything. **(2) ONE verb, `version(which)`** — DEC-72's argument again: «what are you running» and «what was in V2» are one capability with two shapes, and two tools would fight over every version question. R-27 throughout: a missing manifest is `unavailable` at BOTH `current()` and `lookup()`, never `nothing_found`, and an unknown version returns `known` so she offers real ones instead of inventing one. **(3) NO second egress path.** R-1b forbids a third caller of `deliver.build`, so what differs between fire occasions was factored into a frozen `Occasion` (conversation, `person_id`, body, speaker, header, kind); `FirePath.speak(occasion)` is the turn and the heartbeat's reminder adapter sits on top. An announcement names nobody, so §12's «requester left identity.toml» refusal is now scoped to occasions that DO name one. **(4) She relays the tag ANNOTATION in her own voice** — a version with no note announces nothing rather than reading commit subjects at the group. Where she announces is not configured: it is every room `policy.toml` already allows (DEC-8 — trust lives there). `[version] announce` defaults TRUE, unlike every other optional capability, because an unstamped image already announces nothing, so the capability is gated by something stronger than a flag. **MERGE NOTE:** authored as DEC-79, which collides with the room-level `location` switch; renumbered here, and the first renumber was a blind find-and-replace that rewrote seven unrelated location-trust references before the pre-deploy review caught it. | `tests/integration/test_version.py::test_she_answers_which_version_she_is_running` (drives a REAL inbound Signal event), `tests/unit/test_version_announcer.py::test_a_restart_never_re_announces_the_same_version` (three boots, real Postgres), `tests/test_version_stamp_build.py::test_an_unstamped_build_is_allowed_and_leaves_no_stale_stamp`, `tests/infra/test_version_manifest.py`, `tests/test_acceptance_rules.py::RULE_TESTS[R-42]` |
