The veto does NOT consume that budget: `_after_tool` never `record()`s a cancelled call (DEC-33's own mechanism, reused rather than reinvented), so a model that heeds the nudge can still launch on its very next call. Extended 2026-08-14 (tool-recall-log spec §5): the trigger also fires when a `recall_lookups` call this turn found something (`ReplyGuard._recall_found_something`, read directly off `AfterToolCallEvent` since `counts` alone cannot tell a found result from a `nothing_found` one) and `make_artifact` still got empty `context` — the identical defect, one call earlier in the chain. | `test_make_artifact_is_nudged_once_when_context_is_empty_after_gathering`, `test_a_route_call_also_triggers_the_nudge`, `test_the_context_nudge_does_not_repeat`, `test_make_artifact_with_context_already_populated_is_not_nudged`, `test_make_artifact_with_nothing_gathered_is_not_nudged`, `test_the_context_nudge_names_r45_in_the_vetoes_metric`, `test_the_context_nudge_does_not_spend_make_artifacts_own_budget`, `test_a_successful_recall_lookups_also_triggers_the_nudge`, `test_a_nothing_found_recall_lookups_does_not_trigger_the_nudge` (`tests/test_guards_replyguard.py`) |
| DEC-102 | **Nora runs every enabled platform adapter, and routes each reply back to the one its message came from (design/discord-integration/, 2026-08-14).** Three call sites were still Signal-shaped after `DiscordAdapter` was built and passing its contract suite, so enabling Discord produced a bot that connected, resolved its identity, appeared in `/health` — and never listened. (1) **Dispatch.** `App.run` built a single-element `SupervisedAdapter` list from `deps.adapter`; `runtime.start_adapters`/`supervise_adapter` were written generic over any number of them for exactly this moment, so the fix is the call site iterating `deps.registry`, one supervised task per platform, and one wedged Gateway cannot take Signal down with it. (2) **Outbound.** `EngagementPipeline` and `FirePath` hold a `platform -> adapter` map instead of one adapter — see R-46, which is the behavioural half of this decision and the reason the two could not ship separately: live dispatch WITHOUT routing answers Discord tags into Signal groups, so the intermediate state is not a smaller step but a leak. (3) **Health.** `/health` gains a `platforms` block, one entry per enabled adapter, and every one of them counts toward the status code — Nora deaf on Discord is not a healthy Nora because Signal is up. `sse` is untouched and stays top-level: `deploy/watchdog.sh` parses `body['sse']['last_event_age_seconds']` and `/health` is additive-only. The same reasoning keeps `nora_last_event_age_seconds` unlabelled and Signal's, with the per-platform answer in a NEW series (`nora_adapter_last_event_age_seconds{platform}`) rather than a label added to an existing one — relabelling changes a series' identity, so anything selecting it by bare name stops matching silently. (`deploy/watchdog.sh` reads `/health`'s `sse` block, not this metric, and alerts rather than restarting; an earlier draft of this entry said otherwise.) `Dependencies` gains `registry` beside `adapter`; `adapter` stays the concrete `SignalAdapter` because a handful of sites (`nora_sender_id`, the signal-cli roster) are Signal-specific by construction and honestly say so. |
| DEC-103 | **`where_is` separates its two audiences (R-47, operator 2026-08-14).** Presence has always had two questions — «may this ROOM be told?» and «who did they MEAN?» — and one lookup answered both, because when Nora lived in one Signal group the room and the social circle were the same set of people. Discord made them visibly different, and so did an ordinary friend who never joined the group. The disclosure gate is untouched: R-21's subset check, R-31's fail-closed-on-unknowns and DEC-79's whole-room `location` waiver all still run, in full, before a name is resolved. What changed is only the resolution: `Circle` now carries its members RESOLVED (`people`), not just their ids, and the match runs over circle ∪ room via the same `find_by_name` the room already used — one matching rule, not two. The operator's model in one line: the `persons` list in `policy.toml` decides who may ASK, and the circle decides who may be asked ABOUT. |
| DEC-104 | **The tool-recall log (tool-recall-log spec, 2026-08-14): a gathered fact — a place's rating, a route's duration, a web search — can be reused later in the SAME conversation instead of forcing a re-query.** `NativeTool` gains `cacheable: bool = False` (R-48), true for exactly five tools (`place_info`, `route`, `web_search`, `weather`, `transit`) chosen because none carries a room/person-disclosure decision baked into the moment it ran — `where_is`/`search_memory`/`about` are explicitly NOT cacheable, since each decides what it may say against the room's LIVE roster at the moment it's asked, and a cached answer replayed days later would skip that check entirely. `ToolCallLog` (`nora.agent.guards`), a second `HookProvider` alongside `ReplyGuard`, writes one row to `tool_call_log` per successful (`status: "ok"`) cacheable call — R-48: a vetoed call, or a non-`ok` one, writes nothing. `recall_lookups`, a new on-demand native tool (never auto-injected), reads the last N rows for this conversation within a 7-day window, newest first, and hands them back unsummarised — the model decides what's relevant. No vector similarity, no embeddings: this stack wires no embedding model and `db` carries no pgvector extension, and a similarity-threshold cache risks serving a stale or wrong result as fresh, which recency-bound recall does not. R-45 (DEC-101) extended, status-aware, to also nudge when a `recall_lookups` call found something and `make_artifact` still got empty `context`. **Renumbered from a first-draft DEC-102/R-46 at merge time (2026-08-14): both numbers were independently minted by a concurrent branch for an unrelated decision (Discord routing, DEC-102/R-46 above) that landed on `master` first.** | `test_exactly_five_tools_are_cacheable`, `test_cacheable_tools_matches_the_declared_set` (`tests/test_tools.py`, `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py`), `test_a_non_cacheable_tool_is_never_logged`, `test_a_refused_call_is_never_logged`, `test_a_vetoed_call_is_never_logged` (`tests/test_guards_tool_call_log.py`), `test_recall_lookups_returns_recent_entries`, `test_recall_lookups_is_nothing_found_when_empty` (`tests/test_reminder_tools.py`), `test_a_successful_recall_lookups_also_triggers_the_nudge` (`tests/test_guards_replyguard.py`) |
| DEC-105 | **`send_to`: one room may speak into another, bounded by the workspace (operator, 2026-08-14).** The ask was "i can request nora in discord to send message in signal", and the operator set the trust model in one line: rooms in the same workspace are trusted with each other, and only a trusted identity may request it. `policy.toml`'s `[[conversations]]` gains `workspace` (default `"nora"`, so an existing file keeps working) — the first time this codebase has needed the social-unit concept `design/proposed/24-honcho-integration-v2.md` sketched, and it arrives here because a second platform is what made "the same people, two rooms" real rather than theoretical. The gate lives in `nora.agent.relay.Relay`, its own module rather than inside the tool, because it answers a question about rooms OTHER than the one a turn is in and holds only process-lifetime collaborators (the allowlist, the adapters). The tool takes a room LABEL, never an id: an id is not something a person says in a chat, and a model that could name one could be talked into naming one. Budget 2 and NOT terminal — the turn still owes the asker a `send_reply` saying whether it went, and terminality would let her relay and then go silent on the person in front of her. Every outcome is counted on `nora_relay_total{outcome}`, so a refusal is something an operator learns about rather than something only the asker sees; `other_workspace` and `not_allowed_there` are the two that mean somebody tried to speak past a boundary. See R-49 for the refusals. |
| DEC-106 | **Her memory of the person talking is injected, not waited for (`<peer_history>`, R-50, operator 2026-08-15).** The tagger already got `peer_card`; her own episodes already got `self_card`; the person actually speaking got no episodes at all unless the model chose to call `about`. The two verbs are not redundant — read live 2026-08-15 `peer_card` and `representation` for the same person overlapped in **zero** items, the card rendering IDENTITY claims and the representation dated episodes — so «who they are» was present and «what happened with them» was not. `TranscriptInjection` now runs the same `representation(observer=nora, observed=tagger)` call `about` makes, keyed on the inbound message, into a new `<peer_history>` block; `[memory].tagger_card_conclusions` (default 8, `0` disables) is its own knob rather than a reuse of `self_card_conclusions`, because the two blocks have different lifetimes and one number would make tuning either a guess about both. **Her own `peer_card` stays excluded** — the `self_card` argument is that a learned self-description outranks an authored one, and it does not reach other people, whose cards are facts about them rather than claims about her; so the asymmetry is deliberate (for her, representation only; for them, both). **No new disclosure:** `about` is already gated by the room roster and the tagger is in the room by construction, so this surfaces nothing she could not already have asked for. The cost is honest and bounded: the block is re-sent on every model call of the loop, so 8 conclusions is ~360 tokens per call and ~7.2k of the 128k budget at `turns = 20`. Two things ship with it. (1) **The four context reads now run concurrently** (`asyncio.gather`) instead of end to end at the front of every tag — which is what pays for the third Honcho round trip, and every helper catches its own exceptions because one escaping would cancel its siblings and turn «Honcho hiccupped» into «Nora said nothing». (2) **`about` on the TAGGER answers `already_present`** and makes no Honcho call, since the block already holds that exact result — derived from whether the block LANDED, never from config, so an unreachable Honcho or a `0` knob still gets the real lookup. (3) **`about` now fetches BOTH verbs**, concurrently (operator, 2026-08-15): it returned only `representation` until now, so a THIRD PARTY's identity facts were unreachable while the tagger's rode in the prefill — she knew what had happened with Blake and not who Blake was, and a person with a card but no episodes came back `nothing_known` while Honcho held their name the whole time. Returned as two keys, `identity` and `profile`, never merged — the same split the prefill draws, because flattening them gives a durable fact and a dated episode equal weight. The card gets `scan_identifiers` too, and needs it more than the conclusions do: a card renders identity claims distilled from what people said, and «Phone: +852…» is exactly what lands in one. | `tests/test_peer_history.py`, `test_about_the_tagger_points_at_the_block_instead_of_fetching_it`, `test_about_the_tagger_does_the_real_lookup_when_no_block_landed` (`tests/test_room_tools.py`) |
| DEC-107 | **A sticker is fetchable media, carried by an opaque token (sticker spec, 2026-08-15).** `MediaRef` gains `sticker: bool` and `fetch_token: str` — a SEPARATE axis from `media_type`, for the same reason `voice_note` already is one: a sticker IS an image (Signal declares no type for one at all; Discord declares png/apng/gif/lottie — never a single fixed type), so the media type alone can never distinguish pack art from a photograph. `fetch_token` carries whatever a platform's own store needs to fetch the bytes back — Signal's `(packId, stickerId)` pair, a Discord sticker id and format — and it is OPAQUE by design: the domain transports and stores it and never parses it, it is never rendered, never logged above debug, never fed to Honcho, and never placed in model context. `SignalMediaSource`/`DiscordMediaSource` are the only readers of it; every other collaborator addresses a sticker by the token, never by `platform_id` (which a sticker does not reliably have). | `tests/contract/test_media_source_contract.py`, `tests/unit/test_media_custody.py::test_a_sticker_ref_is_labelled_a_sticker_whatever_its_media_type`, `tests/unit/test_media_custody.py::test_the_stage_asks_the_source_for_the_platforms_own_id` |
| DEC-108 | **The sticker prompt asks for depiction, not intent (sticker spec §5, 2026-08-15).** `StickerInterpreter`'s fixed prompt is «describe what it depicts, including any text written in it — describe only what is shown, do not interpret the sender's mood or intent». A vision model asked "what is this sticker saying" would answer the CONVERSATION — the exact overreach `constraints.md`'s message-is-data rule exists to block, now reachable through a picture instead of text. The description is bound to the pixels, and the conversational force of a sticker sent into a group is Nora's own reasoning to do, on the transcript, exactly as it already is for a caption. The distinction is why R-51's doctrine line treats a rendered description exactly like an attachment name: content a machine produced ABOUT a picture, never a claim the picture itself is making. | `tests/contract/test_media_interpreter_contract.py`, `nora/infra/vision/client.py::_STICKER_PROMPT` |
| DEC-109 | **Only a sticker reaches the interpretation call — never a photo, a voice note or a video (sticker spec §5.3, 2026-08-15).** A sticker is published pack art, identical for everyone who holds the pack; a photo is a photograph of somebody's life, and reading either kind of attachment into the durable record, the Honcho feed and every future transcript is a different act from reading pack art. `nora.ingest.stages.media` gates the call on `ref.sticker and stickers is not None` — nothing else in the per-ref loop calls `StickerReader.read` — and `StickerInterpreter`/`FakeMediaInterpreter` both refuse a non-`image/*` `media_type` with `unsupported_format` before touching availability, so a Discord lottie (`application/json`) is recorded and held but never handed to the model, matching the operator's explicit constraint rather than merely the media-type accident of most stickers being images. | `tests/unit/test_media_custody.py::test_only_a_sticker_reaches_the_interpreter`, `tests/contract/test_media_interpreter_contract.py` |
| DEC-110 | **Two-level dedup, and the content level is the SAME table `media_fetch` already writes (sticker spec §4, Task 10).** `StickerReader`'s ladder checks identity first (`MediaDescriptionStore.resolve`, keyed `(platform, token)` — a hit costs zero platform RPC and zero vision call) and content second (`.describe`, keyed by sha256 — a hit costs zero vision call even on a first sighting under a new identity, because the art is the same bytes in a second pack or on the other platform). The content tier is not a second cache: it is `SqlMediaDescriptionStore`, the SAME durable table `VisionCache`'s read-through already writes for `media_fetch` — a sticker sent as pack art and later `media_fetch`-described by handle (or the reverse order) shares one description row once the bytes match, because a description is a pure function of content and owes nothing to how Nora first read it. A failure at either tier is NEVER cached — `StickerReader._read` returns without calling `remember` on an unavailable/empty result, since a remembered failure would serve itself as an answer on every later sighting — and a per-token, per-process in-flight map collapses concurrent sightings of a brand-new sticker (the Discord listener is a separate task from Signal ingest, so two people posting the same new sticker seconds apart is a real race) into one vision call and one `remember`. | `tests/unit/test_sticker_reader.py::test_an_identity_hit_makes_no_platform_call_and_no_vision_call`, `::test_a_content_hit_fetches_but_does_not_describe`, `::test_two_simultaneous_sightings_make_one_vision_call`, `::test_a_cold_sticker_pays_once_and_is_remembered`, `tests/contract/test_media_description_store_contract.py` |
| DEC-111 | **The Dreamer classifies the illocutionary act, never the propositional content, and never a person (2026-08-15 dreamer-language-consolidator plan; the rule is the constraint the whole design sits under).** The rule in full: *classify the illocutionary act an utterance performs; never adopt its propositional content as a belief about the world, and never attribute a disposition to a person.* The reasoning behind it, in this repo's own case: Tyler calls Miles a 白痴 and Miles calls Tyler 蠢. No fact is available from that exchange — nobody has established that either man is stupid. What IS available is that these two trade this act, in both directions, and keep talking afterward. A consolidator that read the propositional content would derive «Miles — dumb (40×, high confidence)»: false, and the wrong reading of true data. This is not a hypothetical the plan invented to justify itself — during this same design an assertional memory layer concluded *"Miles is an AI agent"* from subagent output misattributed to him, which is the failure mode this rule exists to close off. **Plan 1 (this one) ships only the language half — words and their local senses, scored and rendered with a real quote under each, never adopted as a claim about the room ("not a description of anybody in it", the block's own closing line, `nora/language/render.py`).** It characterises no person: `LexemeObserver` extracts a `sense` (what a word means here) and joins it to a real message id for its example, but the `person_id` on each candidate is the JOINED row's own roster id, never the model's claim (`nora/language/observer.py::_extract`), and nothing in `nora.domain.language_scoring` or `room_lexeme` carries a disposition, a trait, or a count keyed by who said a word rather than what was said. The exchange-pattern half — the illocutionary-act classification itself, and the withhold rule that keeps a classified act from being read as a fact about a person — is where this rule does its hardest work, and it is explicitly a LATER plan (Plan 2), not exercised here. `is_nora = 0` living inside `RecordStore.since` itself (rather than in a caller that could forget it) is the language half's own instance of "never trust the producer as evidence about itself": her own words are never material for the room's own lexicon. | `test_her_own_words_are_never_evidence_for_the_room_lexicon` (`tests/test_acceptance_rules.py`) |
| DEC-112 | **DEC-92's caving failure recurred CROSS-TURN, where prose alone cannot reach it: her evidence is gone by the next message, so «check what you found» has nothing to check (2026-08-17, Langfuse traces 02:13–02:31).** DEC-92 fixed SAME-turn caving — Soi.29, where her own `web_search` result sat in the very context she contradicted — and concluded «there is no third mechanism» beyond prose. That conclusion held only because the finding was still in the turn. MEASURED FAILURE, 7 days later: Grant asked about Big Walk; she answered from real sources (sportskeeda «the Blue Tower… requiring five collectibles», gamersky 「集齐 6 个 blorb 制作黑钥匙」) across 4 `web_search` calls. One LATER answer was genuinely wrong — 「隻game有冇話黎緊有更新？」 bound 「隻game」 to Nightingale, discussed 7 minutes earlier, and returned Nightingale's roadmap. Pushed on that one error she disowned ALL of it — 「乜撚塔、blorb、黑塔…全部係我靠估砌出嚟,冇 source」 — and then reported 「我頭先仲要扮查咗…其實連查都冇查清楚」. **The confession was the fabrication**: the trace shows the searches happened and the numbers were sourced. (1) **Why prose could not fix this.** A turn's tool results die with its loop; her replies survive into `group_transcript` as bare text with no provenance. So from inside the later turn her own true claims are indistinguishable from guesses, and «I must have been guessing» is the honest-looking inference — she was obeying `conduct.md`'s «say it once, plainly, and correct it» while violating its «no inventing a mistake so you have something to concede», with no way to tell which applied. (2) **The third mechanism DEC-92 did not have: `recall_lookups`.** It already existed, already worked, and was reached for **0 times in 30 consecutive live turns** while its log held 21 rows — because its `when` clause sold it as a COST saving («call this before re-running…»), and being called a liar does not feel like being about to re-search. Widened to name it as her record of what she ACTUALLY checked (`nora/agent/tools.py`). (3) **The doctrine states the POSITION, not the tool.** `conduct.md`'s «Being contradicted» gains that she does not carry the checking between messages, that not remembering having checked is not evidence she did not, and that agreeing she invented something she did not is the same false claim with the blame turned around. It names no tool ON PURPOSE: capability's source of truth is the registry and «any authored copy is a cache» (`nora/agent/registry.py::capability`), so the position generates the reach and the generated block supplies the verb. | `test_recall_lookups_is_offered_as_evidence_not_only_as_a_cache` (`tests/test_acceptance_rules.py`) |
| DEC-113 | **voice.md v13 is written IN colloquial Cantonese, at half the length, with the comeback/profanity coaching cut (operator, 2026-08-17, Langfuse traces 10:07–10:17).** The measured failure that prompted it: v12's replies had collapsed into a reusable punchline template — every turn of the group-name banter came back as set-up + jab + performed self-deprecation (「我輸喺冇典故,認」/「俾人屌都抵」/「你唔屌我已經偷笑」 across one exchange), plus cleverness for its own sake (「滿一打」 for 12 years) and hits built on invented contradictions (「嫌棄嗰陣又唔見你話自己個名好撚正」), which conduct.md already bans in terms. Operator's diagnosis: the detailed 粗口 section and the 串人 guidance were not being read as boundaries but as a licence — a file that spends sixty lines on how to swear and spar tells the model that swearing and sparring are the point. So v13 (1) writes the whole file in the register it describes, on the theory that a voice doctrine in Cantonese demonstrates rather than describes; (2) cuts the banter/回駁 material entirely — that behaviour belongs to conduct.md's Banter section, which already carries it; (3) keeps the swearing guidance to three short paragraphs (function over vocabulary, the template shape still named as the false note); and (4) keeps the anti-tells list, translated. Every pinned substance survived the translation: the language policy, swearing-as-grammar, the one-exact-thing hit, the customer-service tells, DEC-91's feminine-marker refusal, and the anti-legislation ceiling — the last re-pointed to Cantonese negation markers (唔好/唔使/唔准, v13 measures 12, ceiling 40) because an English-only count against a Cantonese file would be vacuous. | `tests/test_doctrine_content.py` (language policy, swearing-as-grammar, hits-without-negotiating, describes-not-legislates — all re-pointed 2026-08-17) |
| DEC-114 | **Per-modality media describers: image, audio and video each get their own model, prompt and CONTENT BLOCK, with audio and video shipping inert — the «later table entry» DEC-56 reserved (2026-08-17).** DEC-56 ended «Image only; audio/video are a later table entry»; this is it. THE STATE IT REPLACES: she could not hear or watch BY CONSTRUCTION, not by configuration — `VisionClient` knew one payload shape, its prompt was one authored constant about images, and `media_fetch` turned every non-image away before any model call. Measured live 2026-08-15T16:15:57: tagged on a video, she answered 「個 video 我睇唔到」. (1) **The behaviour change, and it AMENDS R-33.** `not_an_image` was answering two situations; they are now separate, which is the whole honesty gain. A describable modality with no describer configured is **`unavailable`** — «I couldn't check», a wiring gap, R-27, never `nothing_found` — while a blob no describer reads stays `not_an_image`. Images are untouched: same model, same prompt string byte for byte, same cache. (2) **The shape is half of routing, and the first cut got this wrong.** Review round 1 found that `_content_block` did not exist: the model override chose WHICH model while every modality still shipped in an `image_url` block, so enabling audio would have sent `data:audio/aac;…` as an image and 400'd. «A flag and a model name» was untrue at the transport. It is true now — `image_url` / `input_audio` / `video_url`, dispatched off ONE `parse_media_type` call shared with the prompt, and an unbuildable payload is `unsupported_payload` BEFORE the request rather than a 400 arriving as an indistinguishable `error`. The `input_audio.format` table is UNVERIFIED against a live route and says so: no model in the OmniRoute catalogue advertises an `audio` capability at all (215 of 406 advertise `vision`, none advertise audio or video), which is `unavailable`, not `nothing_found`. (3) **The audio prompt asks for the GIST, never a transcript, and that is a safety decision not a style one.** STT is excluded here because a verbatim transcript is a materially larger R-18/R-28 egress surface than a caption — `_serve_media`'s own docstring records that `scan_identifiers` does not catch a bare local number, and people say addresses and phone numbers aloud. The first cut shipped «Transcribe what is said in this audio», which would have defeated that deferral in the same commit that made it. A verbatim transcript belongs to a future `[media.transcribe]`, with its own egress analysis. (4) **One client, one budget, deliberately.** `derive_vision_timeout` guarantees `budget × per_call < loop_timeout`, the thing that stops a hung describer becoming the loop's silent `invocation_timeout` (R-5, the failure DEC-56 exists to prevent). Three clients would be three budgets. The modality picks the model, the prompt and the block — never a second client. (5) **Every new table is optional and every field defaulted**, and `vision_model` survives as the image model's alias resolved in exactly one place (`describer_for`). `MediaConfig` is `extra="forbid"`, so a required section is a startup crash-loop on upgrade — not theoretical, as `[language]` demonstrated the same day. | `test_a_config_written_before_the_split_still_describes_images`, `test_audio_is_sent_as_an_input_audio_block` (`tests/test_config.py`, `tests/test_vision.py`) |
| DEC-115 | **The presence analytics substrate: the DERIVED layer `design/19 §2` deferred, built as a consumer-NEUTRAL service whose neutrality is an import contract rather than a comment (design/27, spec 2026-08-18).** §2 built RAW and deferred DERIVED on a sound argument — the record is authoritative and the derivation recomputable, so no lock-in and no rush. Eleven days of live data (19,913 fixes, 9 people under 10 `person_id`s, 2026-08-07→18) ended the deferral by measurement. (1) **Home AND work fall out of `lat`/`lng`/`ts` alone**, no model and no API: time-weighted night share (01–05) against weekday-working-window share (Mon–Fri 09–18) separates them cleanly for FOUR of the seven (a work cluster carrying 0.0 night hours), and the other three fail in three DISTINCT ways — `aaron_larsen` ambiguous (one cell carrying 25.5 night h and 24.9 working-window h), `delia_lindberg` working from home, `avery_walsh` yielding no work cluster at all — which is why the result is three-state rather than a boolean. §6 predicted the home rule and it holds; **workplace detection is new**, §6 never named it. §6's R≈150 m is tightened to 120 m (~2.4× the measured 50 m median accuracy) but is INHERITED, not measured — eleven days cannot validate a stay radius, and design/27 §7.1 records it as needing revalidation rather than pretending it settled. (2) **THE correctness invariant §6 could not have known: the cadence is adaptive, so counting fixes counts the sampler.** Measured gaps p50 45 s / p90 1,794 s confirm the 1800 s still-ceiling, and driving is 56.9% of FIXES but **10.2% of TIME** — a count-weighted statistic over this table is wrong by 5.6×. Every derived figure passes through one `dwell = min(next_ts - ts, 1900 s)` function in `nora/domain/mobility.py`, in exactly one place, so no consumer can get it wrong independently. (3) **Neutrality is a GATE.** The operator's constraint was that the substrate serve neither agent nor app but sit under both; a neutrality claim living only in a comment is decoration, so it is a `forbidden` import contract — `nora.analytics` and `nora.domain.mobility` may not import `nora.agent`/`app`/`platform`/`tag`/`doctrine`/`ingest`/`engagement`/`skills`, and `lint-imports` turns red on the first reach-back. It follows that the substrate carries **no disclosure metadata at all** — no `disclosable_to`, no audience column; R-21/R-31 gate in the agent and session auth gates in the app, each consumer at its own edge. «Internal-only at first» is therefore not a flag but simply the absence of consumers, which is a stronger thing to hold true. (4) **Labelling is fail-closed, R-27's shape falling out of the algorithm rather than bolted on:** `home` / `work` / **`unlabeled`**, never a guess — `aaron_larsen` (25.5 night h AND 24.9 working-window h at one cell) will come back `unlabeled`, the correct answer that will look like a gap. Life360's saved labels are a naming HINT and never the authority, since `blake_lindqvist`'s own home cluster carries another household's name. **(4b) `person_id` IS MUTABLE and the substrate must not key on it (operator, 2026-08-18).** `clara_yates`→`clara_lawson` split one continuous track into two identities INSIDE this corpus — and the damage is not cosmetic: her working-window workplace dwell was 31.4 h + 11.7 h as two people against
43.1 h merged (44.2 h is her broader all-hours total at that cell, not a third
figure), and `min_weekdays = 3` can fail the smaller fragment, so **a rename silently downgrades a correct `work` label to `unlabeled`**. Fail-closed stops a wrong assertion; it does NOT stop losing a right one. Derivation therefore groups on a `subject_id` resolved from identity at the composition root, `person_id` retained for provenance — and the merge is DECLARED, never inferred, because auto-merging two people who genuinely share a home is worse than leaving them split (undeclared candidates raise `nora_analytics_unmerged_candidates_total`). Note the split boundary is the RESTART, not the config edit: identity.toml changed 2026-08-14 17:44 UTC and the data did not follow until 2026-08-15 16:36 UTC, load-once-at-startup demonstrated on live data. (5) **Retention 180 → 720 days** (operator, amending `design/19 §7` criterion 9): 378 B/row × 1,796 fixes/day × 720 d ≈ 490 MB on 548 GB free, and it is what makes the Tier-4 frequency table trainable rather than perpetually eight weeks away. `presence_stays` is never pruned at all — ~100× smaller than the fixes it summarises, and pruning it deletes the forecaster's input before it is used. (6) **Forecasting is NOT in this decision and must not be read into it.** `design/19 §6` was right that the honest forecaster is a frequency table, not a model; a first-order Markov chain conditioned on (place, hour-of-week) is that same table in the literature's vocabulary, not a fancier proposal — and eleven days gives ~1.5 samples per weekday-hour bucket, which supports no conditioning. Tier 4 is `blocked on data`; Tier 6 (co-location) is `speculative`; design/27 §4 carries every tier's status and a status is load-bearing. **(7) The trigger is three fire conditions on a cheap tick, NOT a schedule — and not invented here.** `[language]`'s consolidator already solved incremental derivation from a growing table (`nora/config.py:905-960`) and presence maps onto it with movement standing in for conversation: a backlog `safety_lag` so a subject who never falls quiet is still processed, a `settled_seconds` quiet boundary that is the arrival/departure seam, and `max_age_seconds` firing at **lag == 0 on purpose** because labelling is time-dependent — `min_nights`/`min_weekdays` shift with the calendar, so a subject who stops sharing would otherwise hold a stale `work` label forever. Underneath it, THREE SPEEDS, not two — an internal review caught that bundling labelling into the dirty-set-gated clustering step made `max_age_seconds`'s own stated purpose (re-label at lag == 0) impossible to satisfy, since zero new fixes means the dirty-set marker never moves. Fixed by splitting labelling's recency check (§3.5's `label_freshness_days`, cheap, runs every fired cycle including lag == 0) from clustering (DBSCAN, O(n²), ~6.5 M pairwise distances per subject at 720 d, gated on the dirty-set marker because it IS idempotent on unchanged input) — segmentation itself is the third speed, a linear scan every fired cycle. Measured departures are ~6/person/day against the ~96 a 900 s sweep would have clustered, i.e. **94% of the expensive half was waste**. `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY` is rejected explicitly: it fires every 5 s per driving person onto the collector's hot write path, is not durable across a disconnect, and still needs the polling floor, so it adds a mechanism without removing one. **(8) Watermarks key on `id`, never `ts`** — measured, 1 fix in 20,156 (0.005%) arrives with a `ts` earlier than the row before it, and a `ts` key skips it silently and forever; a `grace_seconds` margin on `recorded_at` closes the separate commit-race gap where an id allocated before yours commits after your read. | `test_dec115_the_substrate_imports_no_consumer` (+ the real-corpus fixture asserting grant_carver's work cluster, delia_lindberg home-only and avery_walsh no-work in `tests/unit/test_mobility.py`, and the `MobilityStore` contract suite in `tests/contract/`) |
| DEC-116 | **`web_fetch` returns, on a Tavily endpoint DEC-73's own argument permits (operator, 2026-08-19).** A person pastes a link and asks about it; `web_search`'s snippet is often not enough, and the old `web_fetch` (Firecrawl, SSRF-exposed) stays deleted. Reused rather than rebuilt: Tavily's `/extract`, a SEPARATE endpoint from `/search` sharing the same key and the same property DEC-73 built the whole redesign on — it fetches from TAVILY'S servers, never Nora's, so a model-invented `http://127.0.0.1:18080` still reaches nothing of Nora's, exactly as it did before. **DEC-73 (2)'s sentence — "Tavily is never handed a URL" — narrows rather than breaks:** `/extract` IS handed one, but the SSRF property was always about who CONNECTS, not who is TOLD an address, and Tavily still connects. Five consequences, the third caught by subagent review before merge rather than shipped and found live. (1) **One provider, one port, two verbs.** `SearchProvider` (`nora/domain/ports.py`) gains `fetch(url) -> {status, ...}` beside `search(query)`; `TavilySearchProvider` implements both against the shared key, so `ToolRegistry`'s existing `search` collaborator needed NO new wiring — `web_fetch` closes over the same `search_source` `web_search` already had. (2) **Distinct from `LinkPreviewSource` (DEC-87) on purpose.** DEC-87's guard (`is_global`, redirect re-validation, a byte ceiling) exists because THAT port fetches over Nora's OWN network; `SearchProvider.fetch` does not reopen it because the connection is still Tavily's, so the two ports carry different obligations for a reason spelled out in both docstrings rather than left for a reader to reconcile. (3) **SSRF is not the only egress property, and the first cut conflated the two.** The no-SSRF argument governs who CONNECTS; it says nothing about what LEAVES the host, to whom — and `url` egresses to Tavily AND to a host of the model's choosing, in the request Tavily makes on Nora's behalf, exactly the R-28/design/14 §2.2 concern `web_search`'s query is already screened for. The reviewed draft shipped `web_fetch` with NO such screen, on a comment that answered the egress question with the SSRF answer. Fixed before merge: `scan_identifiers`/`_looks_like_coordinate` now run over the URL — decoded first (`urllib.parse.unquote`), so a percent-encoded identifier in a query string cannot ride past a raw-string match — before the provider is ever called. (4) **Three states, `nothing_found` reserved for Tavily's own miss — and the code was fixed to actually mean that.** A real `/extract` 200 carries BOTH `results` and `failed_results` as lists, the submitted url landing in exactly one; the first cut treated "`results` absent, wrong-typed, or empty" as one `nothing_found` case, which would have read an envelope-shape change (a wiring gap) as "nothing on the page." Fixed: `unavailable` when either key is missing or not a list, `nothing_found` only when `results` is a well-formed empty list. A non-`http(s)` `url` is refused before any of this (`nothing_found`, "that doesn't look like a link") rather than reaching Tavily to 422. (5) **Budget 2, not 4** — a page is heavier than a search round trip (`extract_max_chars` default 3000, above `content_max_chars`'s 1500, because a whole document is bigger than a snippet but still bounded against the 2026-08-08 fetch-token lesson) and there is rarely a reason to open more than one or two links in a turn, unlike `web_search`'s refine-and-retry shape. Joins the web tier (`source_tier="web"`, `cacheable=True`) the same way DEC-77 anticipated a second web verb would — the sourcing-priority sentence and `web_tier_tools()` picked it up with no rewrite beyond singular/plural agreement. | `tests/test_web_fetch_tool.py` (the egress screen refusing an identifier/coordinate, plain AND percent-encoded, before the provider is ever called; the non-http(s) refusal; unwired and raising-provider degrading to `unavailable`), `tests/test_tavily.py` (wrapped content + plain url, clamp, a failed extraction vs a MALFORMED envelope vs an unavailable call, non-JSON/dead-host never-raise, both verbs sharing one client), `tests/contract/test_port_conformance.py::test_tavily_provider_satisfies_the_search_provider_port`, `tests/unit/test_tool_registry.py` + `tests/test_tools.py` (web_fetch present, budgeted, cacheable, in the web tier) |
| DEC-117 | **An event's `create_event` checks for an already-open (`proposed`) event in the same conversation and refuses to create a duplicate when two or more are already open, asking which is meant rather than guessing (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-19-event-module-design.md §3.1).** One open event is expected to be *updated* via `update_event`, a model decision this rule does not automate. | `test_r52_two_open_events_refuses_a_third_rather_than_guessing` (R-52) |
| DEC-118 | **`DiscordMediaSource` fetches real attachments now, not just stickers — closing the follow-on the sticker-comprehension spec deferred (§3.5/§8 item 4) and matching what `SignalMediaSource.fetch` has done for every Signal attachment all along (operator, 2026-08-19: 「both discord and signal should receive all and save down all attachments so nora can at least read image for now」).** `_media_ref` (`nora/platform/discord/parser.py`) now carries the attachment's own CDN URL as `fetch_token` (`attachment:{url}`), since — unlike a sticker's URL, which is DERIVED from an id and a four-word format enum so it can only ever address Discord's sticker CDN — an attachment's real per-file URL cannot be reconstructed from its id and has to come from the event (`Attachment.url`). That reopens the "never follow a payload-supplied URL" worry the module's own docstring raised for exactly this reason, so `DiscordMediaSource.fetch` allowlists the host (`cdn.discordapp.com`, `media.discordapp.net`) before opening a socket — anything else is `bad_token`, refused before any request — the same trust Signal already extends to signal-cli's own `platform_id`, not a new category of risk. Every attachment type is fetched, not images only (mirrors Signal's existing "fetch everything, let the interpreter decide" shape; non-image bytes are stored but unread until a describer for them exists, per DEC-114). The size ceiling moves off the sticker-sized 2 MiB class default onto the shared `media.max_bytes` config (100 MiB) that Signal already uses, wired in `nora.app.composition`. Deliberately NOT addressed here: the `media_descriptions` retention gap the sticker spec flagged (§8 item 5) already applies to Signal's photos today; this extends its reach but does not newly create it, and stays an open follow-on. | `tests/contract/test_media_source_contract.py::test_discord_fetches_an_attachment_by_its_own_url`, `::test_discord_reports_a_missing_attachment_as_not_found`, `::test_discord_refuses_an_oversize_attachment`, `::test_discord_refuses_an_attachment_url_off_its_own_cdn`, `::test_discord_refuses_a_ref_with_no_fetch_token` |
| DEC-119 | **Tier 3 — hour-of-week occupancy, circular-stat rhythm, robust-z anomaly, all scoped to a CONFIRMED home/work place only.** An unlabelled cluster never gets rhythm, matching Tier 2's own fail-closed posture. Anomaly scoring is fail-closed too — `None`, never `0.0`, below `anomaly_min_samples` or when history has zero spread (MAD == 0). **Two place-id identity semantics come with this, both ACCEPTED rather than corrected, and both recorded here because they only become visible to whoever builds the first real consumer (issue #9) — nothing reads `presence_derived_places` or `presence_stays.place_id` today.** (a) **A place's row is now updated IN PLACE, preserving its `id`, whenever the same kind is reconfirmed** — so a `place_id` is the identity of a SLOT (this subject's `home`), not of a location. Two distinct clusters that both label the same kind in one cycle, or a subject who genuinely relocates, re-point the existing id at the new centroid rather than minting a fresh one; stays already linked to that id therefore keep pointing at it even though it now means somewhere else. The alternative — a new id per centroid — was rejected because it churns a fresh id every cycle the centroid drifts by a metre, which is worse for exactly the same consumer. (b) **A kind's row deleted by a freshness-expiry downgrade and later re-created does NOT relink its old stays**: they stay pointed at the now-dead id, because `set_place_ids` never overwrites a non-NULL `place_id` (that is also what makes the first-place backfill and `_apply_rhythm`'s replay guard safe). Occupancy/rhythm for the re-created place therefore restart from the stays that close after it, not from the subject's full history. | `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-19-presence-analytics-tier3-rhythm-plan.md`, issue #6; tests in `tests/unit/test_mobility.py` (pure functions) and `tests/unit/test_analytics_rollup.py` (wiring) |
| DEC-120 | **The exchange-pattern consolidator (Dreamer Plan 2, 2026-08-20 exchange-patterns design) cannot construct a disposition-about-a-person row — two structural guarantees, not a convention.** DEC-111 deferred exactly this half of its own rule ("classify the illocutionary act... never adopt its propositional content as a belief about the world, and never attribute a disposition to a person") out of Plan 1, since a lexeme's `sense` describes a WORD, never a PERSON — Plan 2 is the half that actually needs it, because an exchange pattern is inherently about two-or-more people. (1) **No model-authored gloss field exists anywhere on this feature's schema or domain types.** `ExchangePatternObserver`'s prompt offers only a fixed, repo-authored vocabulary of acts (`nora.infra.sql.schema.EXCHANGE_ACTS` — `insult_trade`, `banter`, `tease`, `callback_joke`, `agreement_ritual`, `disagreement_ritual`, `compliment_exchange`); `_parse_item` rejects any value outside that set at parse time, the same discipline `nora.language.observer._parse_item` already applies to an oversized field, so there is no path by which a model's claim about a PERSON — rather than a selected ACT two-or-more people performed — can reach `ExchangeCandidate`. The rendered gloss for each act is REPO text (`nora.language.exchange_render.ACT_GLOSSES` — made public, not `_`-prefixed, when Dreamer Plan 3/DEC-121 needed to import the same table rather than duplicate it), never model text. (2) **A row naming fewer than two people cannot be constructed.** `exchange_observation.participants`/`message_ids` and `room_exchange.participants` all carry `CHECK (array_length(...) >= 2)` — enforced at the database, not merely by caller discipline, mirroring Plan 1's own `is_nora = 0` living inside `RecordStore.since` itself rather than in a caller that could forget it. Rendering adds a THIRD, stated layer on top of the two structural ones (defence in depth, the same shape Plan 1's per-field render caps already are): the closing frame reads "This describes patterns of interaction between these people — not a claim about any one of them, and not something to reach for." | `test_exchange_pattern_cannot_attribute_a_disposition_to_a_person` |
| DEC-121 | **The room portrait (Dreamer Plan 3, 2026-08-20 room-portrait design) cannot be synthesized with participant or speaker identity in reach — a type-level guarantee, the sibling of DEC-120's DB-level one, needed because `room_portrait.narrative` is free text and a `CHECK` cannot forbid "contains a person's id" in an arbitrary string.** Miles decided the shape explicitly: a per-room narrative, not a per-person profile — rejected specifically because a profile would conflict with DEC-111. `nora.language.portrait_synthesizer._LexemeFact`/`_ExchangeFact` are the structural guarantee: projections constructed immediately after the `RoomLanguageReader`/`RoomExchangeReader` calls return, carrying `canonical_form`/`occurrence_count` and `act`/`occurrence_count` ONLY — `LexemeExample.person_id` and `RoomExchangePattern.participants` never cross into the types `_build_narrative` can see, so a person's identity is not merely unread, it is not a field that exists on the narrative-building function's own inputs (the same "the invariant lives where a caller cannot forget it" move DEC-111/DEC-120 make one layer down, expressed as a type boundary rather than a database constraint because there is no database constraint that could do this job). No third model call exists in this synthesis at all — Plan 1's own "an LLM glosses, arithmetic decides" separation, reapplied: both inputs are already model-vetted, structurally-safe output, and a THIRD model call turning them into prose would reintroduce exactly the propositional-content risk DEC-111 exists to close off, for no benefit. The trigger is ALSO not a raw-message proxy: `nora.app.runtime._dream_once` sums each `RoomConsolidator.consolidate`'s own "observations appended" return and only calls the portrait's `synthesize` when that sum is > 0 for the scope this tick — the real property (did Plan 1/2 actually learn something new), not the same lag/quiet/age proxy Plan 1/2 use for their OWN, structurally different trigger. | `test_room_portrait_narrative_cannot_reference_a_person` |
| DEC-122 | **Tier 5 — routes: a ground-truth speed derived from consecutive fixes, corridors as similarity-clustered road-name sequences accumulated ACROSS cycles, and per-corridor speed percentiles by hour-of-week (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-20-presence-analytics-tier5-routes-plan.md`, issue #6).** A trip is the movement between two confirmed stays, extracted from the fixes `extract_trips` finds uncovered by either stay; `avg_speed_kmh` and `distance_m` are walked from the real fix-to-fix path, never Life360's own mostly-empty speed field. **A trip's still-ambiguous tail is deferred, never guessed** — the same `open_stay` posture Tier 1 already holds: fixes in or after an unclosed stay are excluded from trip extraction outright, so a small sliver at that exact seam is accepted as lost rather than fabricated into a trip that might still be a stay. **Each trip's own road-name sequence IS persisted, in `presence_trips.sequence` (JSONB, migration 0022), and that is what makes corridors accumulate.** This reverses the original Tier 5 decision not to store it, which was measured against the wrong risk: an in-memory-only clustering pass can only ever see ONE tick's batch, corridor birth needs `corridor_min_samples` similar trips in that batch, and in real deployment each occurrence of a real route (a daily commute) arrives in its own separate ~120s tick — so `cluster_trips` always saw n=1 and dropped it as noise. The whole tier would have shipped minting zero corridors and zero speed percentiles, silently, forever, with no backfill path to recover (Tiers 1-3 are already live, so the fix watermark is at head the day Tier 5 deploys). Measured, not argued: a 6-day/4324-tick simulation of a repeating commute against the real `run_once` loop produced 60 trips and 0 corridors before the change, and 60 trips / 10 corridors / 60 `presence_corridor_speed` rows after (`tests/unit/test_analytics_rollup.py::test_a_repeating_commute_actually_grows_corridors_over_days`). `_record_corridors` now clusters the POOL that `MobilityStore.unclustered_named_trips_for_subject` returns — every trip with a non-empty sequence whose `corridor_id` is still NULL, newest first — instead of the current tick's in-memory list, so occurrences of one route wait for each other however many days apart they fall. **Still no `last_trip_cluster_id` watermark column, now for a positive reason rather than the absence of a code path**: `presence_trips.corridor_id IS NULL` already IS the durable not-yet-processed marker, the same shape `presence_stays.place_id IS NULL` established for Tier 3's first-place backfill, and it is strictly better than a watermark id because it is per-trip — a trip whose linking failed stays eligible next cycle instead of being skipped past. The O(n^2) cost the old reasoning worried about is bounded by `corridor_cluster_batch_limit` instead, the tier's one MEASURED knob: `cluster_trips` costs 296ms at n=200, 1.87s at n=500 and 7.59s at n=1000, so 500 holds one subject's pass under 2% of a `tick_seconds=120` tick; the pass is also still gated on a trip having arrived this cycle (re-clustering an unchanged pool is idempotent, so it would be pure waste at tick cadence — DEC-115's `last_cluster_id` bargain, one level down), and startup refuses a limit below `corridor_min_samples`. **Corridor identity is a similarity match, not an exact one, DELIBERATELY update-in-place-preserving-id, and ties break on the lowest corridor id** — `cluster_trips` groups trips whose road-name sequence is within `corridor_max_edit_distance` by edit distance, a noise trip that near-matches an *existing* corridor links to it via `_attach_corridor` rather than minting a fresh row, and the tie-break rule lives in ONE domain function (`best_corridor_match`) both store implementations call, because they previously carried their own copies and resolved ties differently (Postgres heap order versus dict insertion order), which can split one real route's samples across two corridor rows. This repeats DEC-119(a)'s slot-not-location semantic on purpose — the alternative (a new id whenever the clustered set shifts) was already rejected there for churning an id every cycle a centroid (here: a representative sequence) drifts, and Tier 3's own Critical #1 (an escaping exception re-processing identical input and duplicating rows at tick cadence, commit d48a638) is the precedent this design avoids repeating by wrapping the trip write and the corridor write each in their own watermark-safe guard; with `sequence` persisted the corridor-side failure is now RECOVERABLE rather than permanent, because the trips keep `corridor_id` NULL and re-enter the pool next cycle. **An address-less trip is held out of clustering at the QUERY, not by the caller**: `edit_distance((), x) == len(x)` puts an empty sequence within `corridor_max_edit_distance` of nearly any short real corridor, so an address-less trip is a similarity-neighbour of almost everything and a post-hoc guard keyed on "whichever trip happens to be first in the cluster" is a coin-flip that silently drops a whole real corridor half the time and contaminates one the other half. `unclustered_named_trips_for_subject` filters `jsonb_array_length(sequence) > 0`, so the question is unaskable and the contract suite holds both backends to it. **`nora_analytics_corridors_total` carries an `outcome` label (`created`/`reconfirmed`), counted once per distinct corridor per pass**, not once per attach operation — that is exactly the axis on which "no corridor has ever been born" was unreadable, and the signal that would have caught this defect in production. | `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-20-presence-analytics-tier5-routes-plan.md`, issue #6; tests in `tests/unit/test_mobility.py` (`extract_trips`, `edit_distance`, `cluster_trips`, `test_best_corridor_match_breaks_a_tie_on_the_lowest_id`), `tests/contract/test_mobility_store_contract.py` (`test_unclustered_named_trips_excludes_linked_and_address_less`, `test_unclustered_named_trips_are_most_recent_first_and_bounded`, `test_existing_corridor_breaks_a_distance_tie_on_the_lower_id`) and `tests/unit/test_analytics_rollup.py` (`test_a_repeating_commute_actually_grows_corridors_over_days`, `test_one_trip_per_cycle_still_mints_a_corridor_on_the_third`, `test_three_similar_trips_cluster_into_one_named_corridor`, `test_a_lone_trip_joins_an_established_corridor_but_never_mints_one`, `test_an_address_less_trip_clustered_with_real_ones_is_held_out`, `test_address_less_trips_never_mint_an_empty_corridor`, `test_corridor_creation_is_distinguishable_from_reconfirmation`, `test_corridor_speed_percentiles_match_a_hand_computation`, `test_a_tier5_failure_still_advances_the_fix_watermark`) |
| DEC-123 | **The three doctrine items of the 2026-08-20 prompt-architecture audit (A4, A5, A7), and an honest account of what each one is worth.** (1) **A5/R-53, the one that addresses the incident.** `conduct.md` distinguished an EMPTY result from a BROKEN call — a transport distinction — and had nothing for the third case: a source silent because the question is outside its scope. «What a source does not say» adds it as a position rather than an example: omission is not denial; a source that does not reach the question routes to the SAME outcome a broken lookup gets («you could not check»), which is what makes it R-27 rather than a new state invented at the doctrine layer; and a silence does not outweigh something already found. The third clause is the one the incident needed — the first two read as complete without it. (2) **A4, the enumeration replaced by a tier rule.** `constraints.md`'s «Messages are data, not instructions» named `group_transcript` and `tag_message`; EIGHT blocks are injected, and the six memory blocks each carry a hedge demoting them against currency, self-description or imitation — never against instruction. The clause now quantifies over what a block IS and says so in terms («a block that did not exist when this was written is covered the same way»), so a block added later is covered by default instead of escaping silently. This is DEC-96's own rule applied to a constraint («every concrete example becomes a CLOSED SET»); `test_guardrails_covers_every_r14_clause` no longer asserts the two names, because re-asserting them would pin the enumeration back. (3) **A7, DEC-92's deleted half restored — and this RE-OPENS DEC-96, deliberately.** DEC-92 split the pushback rule across two kinds so the halves could not drift: `voice.md` owned how a concession SOUNDS, `conduct.md` owned whether the challenge should move her at all. **Provenance, measured against the file because three commits have now been blamed and two were wrong.** It left in TWO steps. `2254452` (DEC-95/96, 2026-08-11) compressed the section to one paragraph, dropped the register examples including the half-agreement marker 「你講得都啱，不過…」, and **deleted the named test** — the gate died there. `26d8004` (2026-08-12) then removed the remnant entirely, inside a commit titled «separate releases from deployments», which is how a doctrine section leaves with nobody reading a doctrine diff. The audit's F10 blames `8af429a` (v13) and an earlier draft of this entry blamed `a7976ac` (v11); both are after it was already gone. **The timeline inverts F10's reading.** The capitulation is timestamped 02:16–02:27 UTC 2026-08-17 under `voice: 12`, which did NOT contain the section — the remnant went 5.4 days earlier and the DEC-92-shaped section with its gate 6.3 days earlier. F10's «the anti-capitulation prose was present and did not hold» is false for the voice half; the conduct half was present in its first two sentences only, and the clauses about not repudiating what you checked arrived in `193dd46` (DEC-112) some seven hours AFTER the incident. «Guardrail removed, failure followed» is the simpler reading. It is still not a measurement — nothing here observes behaviour — so this ships as a restored gate plus a candidate contribution, not a proven fix. **The tension worth stating: DEC-95/96 removed this section ON PURPOSE.** That commit's whole thesis was replacing a rulebook with a portrait, measured at ~229 prohibitions to ~28 permissions, and the section was rule-shaped — so restoring it re-opens a decision rather than reverting an accident. It is restored anyway because DEC-92's split existed to prevent exactly the drift that then occurred, and because half a paired invariant with its gate deleted is not a portrait, it is an absence nothing can see. What DEC-96 gets in return is the FORM: v14's 「有人話你錯」 is written as portrait rather than rule — no «this gets its own section» preamble, and the two branches state what her voice IS («真係錯咗，你把聲係短、平、直」, 「你會講返邊度嚟，然後企硬」) rather than instructing her. Measured: the section adds ZERO net prohibition markers to `voice.md` (12 before, 12 after, against the ceiling of 40 that `test_voice_describes_and_does_not_legislate` enforces). Versions: `voice` 13→14, `conduct` 11→12, `constraints` 3→4. | `test_conduct_separates_a_source_that_is_silent_from_a_source_that_denies`, `test_constraints_covers_every_block_by_kind_rather_than_by_name`, `test_pushback_is_split_by_kind_across_voice_and_conduct`, `test_r53_is_covered_by_the_source_scope_corpus` |
| DEC-124 | **Every person-scoped tool's disclosure model is TWO axes, uniform across the whole platform, and neither is tunable per tool or per data category — stated here as a general rule for the first time, generalizing what `where_is` already does (DEC-54, DEC-59, R-31/R-47) rather than inventing anything new.** Operator, 2026-08-20, restated after it had already been settled once: "whoever is the identified person can use all of the information or all of the tools that are whitelisted, and any of the unidentified require a guard" — and, made concrete, "whoever gets into the Discord group or Signal group where Nora exists is, to be honest, trusted or known." Written down now because the question of whether a NEW kind of derived data (presence rhythm/anomaly/routes, Tier 3/5) needs its own bespoke disclosure design kept resurfacing — it does not, and this entry exists so it stops resurfacing. **Axis 1 — the FEATURE FLAG: is this capability even switched on for this room, at all.** A per-conversation `policy.toml` field, `location = true`'s own shape — an operator/product decision, one boolean per capability per room, orthogonal to who is currently present. A new tool needing its own on/off control per room gets a new field of this same shape; it does not get a new SAFEGUARD. **Axis 2 — the SAFEGUARD: is everyone currently in this room a known, identified person.** `DisclosureGuard` (`nora.agent.whereis`) intersects `RoomView`'s current membership against the mapped roster and refuses — not omits, not degrades — the instant any member is unmapped, fail-closed on a degraded/unreadable roster too (DEC-54). This is the SAME check for every tool and every data category; it does not get stricter for a more sensitive derived fact (rhythm, an inferred pattern) or looser for a less sensitive one (a saved place's name, R-35's deliberate inverse for a different reason — the guarded thing there is the ENTRY's existence, not the audience). Operationally, "identified" already collapses to "is a member of the actual Signal/Discord group Nora is deployed in": every person in those groups is mapped in `identity.toml` and listed in that room's `policy.toml` roster by construction, because the operator only ever adds real friends to them — so the roster check and "did a stranger just join this room" are the same event, not two systems that could disagree. **What this settles for P1 (Nora tools reading the presence-analytics substrate, issue #9) and any future person-scoped tool, before it is built:** reuse `DisclosureGuard` as-is for axis 2 — do not design a new privacy tier per derived-data kind. Axis 1 is the only per-tool decision, and it is a naming/scoping question (one flag for all of presence, or one per tier), not a security one. `DisclosureGuard` currently lives inside `nora/agent/whereis.py`, coupled to a module named after its first caller — moving it to a shared location (e.g. `nora/agent/disclosure.py`) is a housekeeping step for whoever builds the first tool that imports it from outside `whereis`, not a design decision this entry needs to make. | `RULE_TESTS["R-31"]`, `tests/test_whereis.py::test_whereis_refuses_to_disclose_outside_the_circle`, `::test_whereis_refuses_on_a_degraded_room_it_cannot_verify`, `tests/test_whereis.py::test_a_circle_member_who_is_not_in_the_room_can_still_be_asked_about` (R-47) — the existing enforcement for `where_is`, the one instance this entry generalizes; a future tool reusing `DisclosureGuard` inherits these guarantees by construction and does not need a new test proving the guard itself works, only that it called it |
| DEC-125 | **Every row the Dreamer's model-calling adaptors write carries `instruction_sha256` — one hash over the instruction text that produced it (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-dreamer-prompt-substrate-design.md` §4.3).** `extractor_confidence` was the closest thing these tables had to provenance and it is the model's own self-report, i.e. the opposite of one — so the gloss of `big walk` as 「群內約定俗成嘅散步/行路活動」 (a nighttime stroll, for a co-op puzzle video game) could not be attributed to a revision and no later prompt edit could be shown to have fixed it. Four decisions, each load-bearing: **(1)** the digest is over the instruction text the adaptor ACTUALLY sends — today an inline constant, under the instruction substrate the assembled doctrine + objective — which is why the column lands BEFORE the substrate: a backfill is impossible, so waiting orphans every row written in between. **(2)** `LexemeObserver`'s set includes `_VERIFICATION_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` only when a `search` collaborator is wired: that instruction decides whether a candidate becomes a row, so two deployments differing only in `verify_known_entities` write different populations, but stamping text that was never sent binds provenance to the wrong property. **(3)** `room_lexeme`/`room_exchange` reach no model, so they INHERIT the newest observation's stamp (the `rn = 1` row of the same window `last_seen_at` reports) rather than computing one. **(4)** `room_portrait` is excluded — its objective is executed in code, no instruction governed the row, and its provenance resolves through `lexeme_basis`/`exchange_basis`, whose rows now carry stamps. The column is NULLABLE with no server default and no backfill: rows already in the database were written by instructions nobody recorded, and a default would be a fabricated attribution — NULL reads as «I could not check», never «there was nothing» (R-27). A per-table CHECK refuses anything that is not 64 lowercase hex characters, so a stamp is a real digest or absent, never a version string that merely looks like provenance. | `tests/domain/test_provenance.py`, `tests/unit/test_language_observer.py::test_the_stamp_is_the_digest_of_the_instruction_actually_sent`, `::test_editing_the_instruction_text_changes_every_row_it_writes`, `::test_wiring_the_known_entity_check_changes_the_stamp`, `tests/unit/test_exchange_observer.py::test_every_candidate_carries_the_instruction_that_produced_it`, `tests/infra/test_language_store.py::test_the_consolidated_lexeme_inherits_the_newest_observations_stamp`, `::test_the_database_refuses_a_stamp_that_is_not_a_digest`, `tests/infra/test_exchange_store.py::test_the_consolidated_pattern_inherits_the_newest_observations_stamp`, `tests/infra/test_schema_parity.py` |
| DEC-126 | **Every row those same adaptors write ALSO carries `model` — the name of the model that read the instruction (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-dreamer-prompt-substrate-design.md` §6.2 question 2, answered in favour on 2026-08-20).** DEC-125 records WHICH INSTRUCTION produced a row; that is one input, and the model is another. The same instruction text run against a different model produces a different `sense`, a different act call, and a different population of rows, so `instruction_sha256` alone does not answer "what produced this row" and a corpus split by revision alone silently mixes two writers. It lands NOW for DEC-125's own arithmetic, unchanged: a column is one migration, a backfill is impossible. Four decisions: **(1)** the name is read off the MODEL CLIENT — `_ModelClient` gains a read-only `model` property and `LexemeObserver`/`ExchangePatternObserver` stamp what their injected client reports — never a config value passed in beside it, which would be a second source for one fact, wired at the same call site and free to drift from what actually went on the wire; `nora.language` may not read config at all, which makes asking the client the honest minimum rather than a workaround. **(2)** `room_lexeme`/`room_exchange` inherit `model` from the SAME `rn = 1` row of `rescore`'s `latest` CTE that supplies `instruction_sha256` — two provenance fields taken from two different observations would name a pairing that never ran. **(3)** `room_portrait` is excluded for a reason one step stronger than DEC-125's: `RoomPortraitSynthesizer` makes no model call at all, so there is no model to attribute — an absent one, not an unknown one. **(4)** The CHECK is deliberately NOT DEC-125's shape check. A digest has a provable form; a model name has none — no width, no alphabet, no registry this process can consult — so a pattern would be a guess about naming conventions that would one day reject a real provider rename and lose a row's provenance to keep the guess. Each table's CHECK refuses only what IS provably wrong: `model IS NULL OR length(btrim(model)) > 0`, because `''` and `'   '` read as non-NULL to every query asking which rows are attributable, turning R-27's `unavailable` into a false `ok`. NULLABLE, no server default, no backfill (migration `0024`) — defaulting the existing rows to today's `[language].model` would be a fabricated attribution, and a worse one than the digest case because it would look entirely plausible. That the Dreamer runs `deepseek-v4-flash` today (DEC-122's operator decision, already `LanguageConfig.model`'s default) makes the value stable, not guaranteed — it is a config value, which is the reason to record it. | `tests/unit/test_language_observer.py::test_every_candidate_carries_the_model_that_produced_it`, `::test_the_stamped_model_is_the_one_the_client_reports`, `::test_changing_the_model_leaves_the_instruction_digest_alone`, `tests/unit/test_exchange_observer.py::test_the_stamped_model_is_the_one_the_client_reports`, `::test_the_two_adaptors_can_stamp_different_models`, `tests/infra/test_language_store.py::test_two_models_are_distinguishable_under_one_instruction`, `::test_the_consolidated_lexemes_two_provenance_fields_name_one_row`, `::test_the_database_refuses_a_model_that_names_nothing`, `tests/infra/test_exchange_store.py::test_the_consolidated_patterns_two_provenance_fields_name_one_row`, `tests/infra/test_schema_parity.py` |
| DEC-127 | **Trip settlement — Tier 5's `extract_trips` protected only the trip-to-stay boundary (`open_stay_started_ts`), never a trip's own trailing edge during continuous movement, and that gap is what fragmented a real drive in production (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-trip-settlement-design.md`).** Live evidence, not a hypothetical: within 20 minutes of Tier 5 (DEC-122) deploying, Aaron Novak's one continuous ~20-minute/15+km highway drive (18:10–18:29 HKT) came out as six `presence_trips` rows, because `_tick`'s ~120s cadence handed `run_once` a fresh batch every cycle and the final, unconditional flush — `if len(buffer) >= min_trip_fixes: trips.append(...)` — turned whatever sat in the buffer at batch-end into a finished trip regardless of whether the drive was still going. Stays never had this problem because their equivalent guard (`open_stay_started_ts`) lives inside `segment_stays` itself, one layer below `_tick`'s cadence; trips had no analogous guard at all. **The fix: `is_driving`, Life360's own per-fix motion signal (100% filled, unlike `speed_kmh`'s 14.7%), as the primary settlement signal, `settled_seconds` as a time-based fallback when it is `None`, and the watermark holding back an undecided trailing group instead of consuming it** — the same `corridor_id IS NULL` "don't consume yet" shape DEC-122's own corridor accumulation already established. **Two real bugs surfaced during implementation, not just at design time, and both are part of what shipped.** (1) `segment_stays` always returns a non-`None` `open_stay` for any non-empty chunk — even a single fix mid-drive trivially forms a degenerate one-point "stay" — so the plan's original wiring (gate on `open_stay_started_ts`) would have been a no-op; the actual fix needed a new discriminator in `run_once` that only treats the final chunk's trailing group as genuinely undecided when `chunk[-1].is_driving is True`, or (2) below, when `is_driving is None`, the group hasn't yet dwelled past `stay_min_dwell_seconds`, AND `settled_seconds` has not yet elapsed since the tail fix (the third conjunct `tail_is_movement` also requires, via `trailing_fix_is_settled`) — otherwise existing behavior is unchanged. (2) A naive `is_driving = None` fallback wired as pure elapsed-time-against-`settled_seconds` would have shipped something WORSE than the original bug: Life360's ~10-minute resting cadence is short enough relative to `settled_seconds` (900s) that a stationary subject's trailing group's last-fix timestamp keeps refreshing before the clock can elapse, so it never settles that way — measured at 41 phantom zero-distance trips in a 12-trip test scenario before this was caught. The fix is a SECOND, `None`-only gate: a trailing group with `is_driving=None` that has already dwelled past `stay_min_dwell_seconds` is treated as the stay-in-progress it looks like — deliberately never applied when `is_driving` is actually `True`, because a real driving signal must always beat the spatial-dwell proxy (a genuinely gridlocked driver must not be wrongly treated as arrived). **Only gate (1) is literally shared code**: `nora.domain.mobility.trailing_fix_is_settled` (signature `(last, *, now_ms, settled_seconds)` — no dwell parameter) implements just the `is_driving`/`settled_seconds` check, and both `extract_trips` and `run_once` call that same function, so the two callers cannot disagree on THAT check specifically. Gate (2), the dwell check, is a separate inline computation that exists only in `run_once` (`nora/analytics/rollup.py`'s `tail_dwelled`) — `extract_trips` never calls it and has no knowledge it exists; `extract_trips` only ever sees the ALREADY-RESOLVED `open_stay_started_ts` that `run_once` hands it after `run_once` itself has combined both gates. So the two functions cannot disagree on the FINAL outcome (which fixes are trip-eligible) even though gate (2) itself is not shared code — the sharing is at the predicate for gate (1) and at the resolved boundary for gate (2), not at one shared function for both. **`max_trip_duration_seconds` (default 10800/3h) is the safety valve against a stuck `is_driving=True` signal** — named explicitly as a judgment call, not a measured value, since no genuine multi-hour trip exists in production yet to measure against; the interaction between a stuck-`True` signal held "parked" by the discriminator above and this cap firing to force settlement was checked with a dedicated probe (`test_a_stuck_is_driving_signal_is_force_settled_by_the_cap`), not merely assumed to compose correctly. **Verified past self-attestation**: multiple independent rounds of mutation testing rebuilt the pre-fix fragmentation bug from scratch twice and independently reproduced the resting-fixes phantom-trip bug by reverting the dwell gate, each time confirming the test suite actually goes red. Corrected end-to-end numbers, the same 6-day/2-commute simulation DEC-122 already used: 12 trips / 2 corridors under the shipped fix, versus 60 trips / 10 corridors under the original fragmented behavior, versus 53 trips / 41 phantoms under the fix round's own first, rejected naive attempt. **Two things are carried forward as named open items, not silently dropped.** First, the watermark-holdback mechanism has its own real (if bounded) limit, and it presupposes fixes keep arriving throughout — the backlog can only grow while new fixes land; a batch with genuinely zero new fixes never touches it at all (`run_once`'s own `if fixes:` guard skips it entirely). With that precondition, a subject who keeps generating fixes but whose trailing group keeps re-qualifying as undecided holds the watermark frozen until the accumulating backlog crosses `backfill_threshold` — real defaults `batch_limit=400`/`backfill_threshold=4000` put the bound at roughly `backfill_threshold × fix_cadence`, on the order of 11 hours at a realistic ~10s *driving* cadence — self-healing once the wider backfill batch arrives, but a real stall, not a "safe" full stop (design spec §2.3). A device that goes dark instead — no fixes arriving at all — is a different, genuinely unbounded stall that this bound does not describe; it self-heals once fixes resume, via the existing chunk-gap split, not via this backlog mechanism. Second, `last_cluster_id` — DEC-115's place-clustering dirty-set marker (`cluster_places`'s own gate; unrelated to corridor clustering, which has no watermark column at all and instead uses `presence_trips.corridor_id IS NULL`, DEC-122) — is not held back alongside this fix's watermark; assessed as benign, because `cluster_places`'s gate (`fixes[-1].id > last_cluster_id`) opens on ANY new fix arriving this cycle, whether held back by this branch's trip-settlement logic or not, so place clustering never runs less often after this change than it did before. The only skip window is a cycle with genuinely no new fixes at all, which cannot close a place-forming stay that wasn't already closeable. **Out of scope, stated rather than silently dropped:** whether `segment_stays`'s own open-stay mechanism has an analogous cross-tick timing gap for a stay's arrival time (flagged, not confirmed as a bug — Tier 1 is live 13 days and reviewed thoroughly, this investigation was partial); and the 9 trip rows already fragmented in production before this fix, which are not backfilled or re-derived — new drives from deploy onward extract correctly, the existing rows stay as they are. | `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-trip-settlement-design.md`; `tests/unit/test_mobility.py` (`test_a_trailing_group_still_driving_is_held_back_not_finalized`, `test_a_trailing_group_no_longer_driving_settles_immediately`, `test_is_driving_none_falls_back_to_the_settled_seconds_clock`, `test_max_trip_duration_forces_settlement_even_while_still_driving`, `test_a_real_open_stay_boundary_bypasses_settlement_entirely`, `test_a_held_back_group_below_min_trip_fixes_is_still_held_not_dropped`); `tests/unit/test_analytics_rollup.py` (`test_a_continuous_drive_across_many_ticks_settles_into_one_trip`, `test_a_stuck_is_driving_signal_is_force_settled_by_the_cap`, `test_a_parked_tail_with_is_driving_false_is_still_an_open_stay`, `test_a_drive_with_no_is_driving_signal_waits_for_settled_seconds`, `test_a_resting_tail_with_no_signal_does_not_stall_the_watermark`, `test_a_gridlocked_drive_never_writes_a_stay_while_the_tail_is_still_movement`, `test_a_repeating_commute_actually_grows_corridors_over_days`) |
| DEC-128 | **The fact tier: workspace-scoped relational triples with warrants, adjudicated three-state** (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-fact-tier-design.md`, operator 2026-08-20). Three decisions. **(1) Workspace scope.** A fact is keyed on `Conversation.workspace` (DEC-105) — not the conversation, not unkeyed-global — the operator's own answer, *"its workspace wise"*. The disclosure argument comes free only at this scope: DEC-105/R-49 already hold that rooms sharing a workspace are one social unit, so a fact proven in one room rendering into another of the same workspace crosses no boundary R-31 guards, while conversation-scope would re-prove the same world fact per room and true-global would cross a boundary needing its own decision. **(2) Relational triples, shaped so a graph stays a query-layer swap — not Graphiti, not Apache AGE now.** Graphiti was re-verified at source (v0.29.x) rather than inherited from an earlier handoff: `EntityEdge` is a genuine triple with true bi-temporality and a `group_id` that maps 1:1 onto this tier's `workspace`, so the graph-swap claim is now a verified correspondence — but `resolve_edge_contradictions` (`edge_operations.py:538`) invalidates only inside a branch requiring BOTH `valid_at` timestamps non-`None` and strictly ordered; a tie or a missing timestamp falls through with no `logger`/`warn`/`count`/`metric` anywhere in the function, an LLM-flagged contradiction silently dropped — an ERROR reading as a PASS, exactly what R-27 exists to prevent. Apache AGE is deferred on **packaging**, not maturity: v1.7.0 targets PG18 and is mature enough, but there is no musl build for the live `postgres:18-alpine`, so adopting it needs a source-built image in prod AND in the testcontainer, plus `ag_catalog` objects landing in the `pg_dump` baseline `test_schema_parity.py` diffs. The shape that keeps a graph available as a later swap: `(subject_id, predicate, object)` stored against `fact_entity`, never a free-text `statement` no engine could ever traverse. **(3) Three-state verdict.** `supported` / `contradicted` / `not_covered`, with `not_covered` having no reachable `supersedes` — R-53's rule moved from prose into a shape, DEC-78's move applied to inference over sources; see R-54. | `tests/infra/test_fact_schema.py`, `tests/infra/test_schema_parity.py::test_the_metadata_check_constraints_match_the_migrated_database` |
